# Downcoding and underpayment: domain, payer, legal and appeal research for ODA

Status counts: BACKGROUND 3, CONTESTED 1, DISPUTED 3, UNVERIFIED 79, VERIFIED 4.

Compiled from the research run of 2026-11-06. Each lens was researched by an agent with live web access; claims marked critical were then sent to two independent checkers (a source checker and a skeptical domain reviewer). The run exhausted its usage allowance before every check finished, so each finding carries its true status:

- **VERIFIED**: every checker that ran confirmed it.
- **CONTESTED**: checkers disagreed; the corrected wording is shown.
- **DISPUTED**: every checker that ran rejected it; use the corrected wording, not the original.
- **UNVERIFIED**: researched with sources, but no independent check completed. Confirm before relying on it in a letter or a contract discussion.
- **BACKGROUND**: not marked critical, not checked.

## X12 835 semantics for code changes, downcoding, denials and fees

X12 005010X221A1 semantics were verified from X12 RFIs, the x12.org code lists (CARC, RARC, provider-adjustment codes), CMS/CGS Medicare documents, payer PLB guides and five of ODA's own TriZetto .RMT files. Key results: SVC06 is the submitted procedure composite and is required whenever any component of SVC01 (code or modifier) differs; Medicare (CGS) and UMR demonstrably populate it, while UHC's only public 835 guide marks it Not Used. Downcoding by Cigna/Aetna/Anthem-style programs often leaves the billed code in SVC01 and reduces payment under an ordinary CO 45, so detection needs allowed-amount (AMT B6) comparison plus the CARC 150/186 and RARC M85/N610/M25/N22 signals; MPPR (CARC 59 with payer-added modifier 51 in SVC06), sequestration (253), MIPS (144/N807), reversals (CLP02 22) and COB (OA 23) must be excluded from downcoding counts. Fee leakage is explicit in PLB segments (Zelis "CS:ZELIS TRANSACTION FEE" at 2.47% of gross claim payments; UHC uses AH). The TriZetto file-name convention was decoded from the files (P<payerID>D<payeeID>U<seq>-<TRN02>-<payer>-<TIN>.RMT) and file timestamps do not reflect payment dates, so dedupe and dating must use in-file segments.

### [UNVERIFIED] In 005010X221A1, SVC01 is the adjudicated procedure composite (code plus up to four modifiers), SVC06 is the originally submitted composite and is situationally required whenever any component of SVC01 (code OR modifier) differs from what was submitted, and SVC07 is the submitted units, required when paid units (SVC05) differ. A payer that changes the code without sending SVC06 is out of compliance with the TR3.

X12 RFI #2320 quotes the SVC06 situational rule: “Required when the adjudicated procedure code provided in SVC01 is different from the submitted procedure code from the original claim” and rules: “If the submitted procedure code is not included in the SVC01, then it must be included in the SVC06”; it cites TR3 §1.10.2.6 (Procedure Code Bundling and Unbundling) para 4: “reference the original submitted code in SVC06.” RFI #1784: “The guide requires sending any modifiers used in adjudication in SVC01”; modifiers submitted but not used in adjudication “are reported in SVC06”; the SVC06 rule “applies to all information within the composite.” RFI #1819: “When the submitted modifier is not used in adjudication, then an SVC06 is required. All of SVC06 is tied to SVC01, not just one component... changes to a modifier represents changes to the procedure code as part of the composite as a whole.” RFI #1950: the 2110 SVC loop is required for “all service lines in a professional claim.” CMS 835 flat file labels SVC01-2 “Adjudicated Procedure Code”, SVC06-2 “Procedure Code” (situational), SVC07 “Original Units of Service Count”. CMS Medicare Claims Processing Manual Ch. 22 (835 mapping tables): “SVC06-1 Situational, but required if the procedure or drug code has been changed during adjudication”; “SVC06-3--7 Situational, but required for Medicare if modifiers are changed”; “SVC07 Situational, but required for Medicare if the paid units of service is different than the billed units of service.” OneHealthPort best-practice (WA payers): “SVC06 will be used to reflect what was received on the 837. SVC01 will be used to reflect any differences, or changes, made by the health plan.” Caution: some vendor pages (e.g., Huntington developer portal) invert the meaning (“SVC06 = adjudicated code”); they are wrong. The X12 8020 change log (AAHAM copy) shows X12 is adding an RA qualifier and explicit modifier-difference language to SVC06, confirming current intent.

**What it means for ODA:** Parser must store SVC01 and SVC06 as full composites (qualifier, code, mod1-4). Detection rule D1 (payer-reported downcode, highest evidence tier, no 837 needed): SVC06 present AND SVC06 code != SVC01 code AND both in the E/M family (99202-99215, 99242-99245) AND SVC01 level < SVC06 level. Modifier-only differences are NOT downcoding: payer-added 51 => MPPR; removed 25 => E/M bundling edit. SVC05 < SVC07 => unit cutback (MUE) category. Every EMA charge line must appear in the 835 for professional claims; a missing line is an anomaly to flag. Absence of SVC06 when the code was changed is a TR3 violation citable in appeals/complaints (RFI #2320).

Sources: [x12.org](https://x12.org/resources/requests-for-interpretation/rfi-2320-835-svc06-requirement) · [x12.org](https://x12.org/resources/requests-for-interpretation/rfi-1784-835-reporting-dental-modifiers) · [x12.org](https://x12.org/resources/requests-for-interpretation/rfi-1819-dental-modifier-835) · [x12.org](https://x12.org/resources/requests-for-interpretation/rfi-1950-svc-835-required-always) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/medicare/billing/electronicbillingeditrans/downloads/835-flatfile.pdf) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/regulations-and-guidance/guidance/manuals/downloads/clm104c22_remit_phase3_apr24-03_r2.pdf) · [onehealthport.com](https://www.onehealthport.com/sites/default/files/content-uploads/bpr/Processing_Reporting_Remittance_Information.pdf) · [aahamwi.starchapter.com](https://aahamwi.starchapter.com/images/downloads/aaham_hot_topic_document.pdf) · [developer.huntington.com](https://developer.huntington.com/enterprisepayments/docs/edi-835)

### [UNVERIFIED] Medicare (CGS J15) and UMR demonstrably populate SVC06 in ODA's own files whenever they alter the procedure composite; UHC's only public 835 companion guide (Vision/Spectera) marks SVC06 'Not Used'; several plans populate SVC06 only for bundling; the SVC06 practice of Anthem, Humana, UHC commercial/MA, Cigna and Aetna is not publicly documented and must be measured from ODA's corpus.

KY Medicare file (P15102D<practice id>U33232-<trace>-KYMEDICARE-<practice id>.RMT): 96 claims, 221 service lines, 22 lines with SVC06 populated, each where CGS appended modifier 51 (e.g., SVC*HC:13121:51*383.09*162.58**1*HC:13121; SVC*HC:17000:59:51*61.11*25.94**1*HC:17000:59) and each carrying CAS CO 59. UMR file (P39026D<practice id>U835747-...): SVC*HC:11401:51*0.00*64.15***HC:11401 with CAS*CO*45*-64.15 and LQ*HE*M15 (bundling representation). CMS Ch.22 mapping makes SVC06 mandatory for Medicare when a code or modifier is changed. UHC Vision 835 companion guide table: SVC06-1 through SVC06-7 “Not Used”, SVC07 “QUANTITY_BILLED”. Summa (Ohio) companion guide: “This element will be used if the submitted procedure code was bundled. The new bundled procedure code will be placed here” (note: non-standard placement). OneHealthPort (Premera/Regence et al.) commits to SVC06 = what was received on the 837. Prior ODA analysis (CONTEXT_CHECKPOINT.md; scrape historicalSignals.csv): 116 claims where “SVC06 reports a higher office E/M code than SVC01 and the original code matches an EMA charge” — payer breakdown not recorded.

**What it means for ODA:** Build a per-payer 'SVC06 reporting rate' metric: lines with SVC06 divided by lines whose 835 code/modifier differs from the EMA charge line. Payers with SVC06 give self-incriminating evidence for appeal packets (SVC06 vs SVC01 printed verbatim). Payers that never send SVC06 but show allowed amounts below schedule on level 4/5 E/M are 'silent downcoders' and need the EMA-comparison rule (finding on silent downcoding). Report the 116 prior candidates by payer as the first dashboard cut.

### [UNVERIFIED] CLP02 claim status values in 5010 are 1/2/3 (processed as primary/secondary/tertiary), 4 (denied), 19/20/21 (processed as primary/secondary/tertiary and forwarded to another payer), 22 (reversal of previous payment), 23 (not our claim, forwarded) and 25 (predetermination pricing only, no payment; not used by Medicare). A reversal must restate the entire prior claim with negated amounts under CLP02=22 followed by the corrected claim; secondary payers report the prior payer's impact as CAS*OA*23 and the allowed/coverage amount in AMT*AU (claim) or AMT*B6 (line).

CGS 835 Companion Guide Table 11: CLP02 codes “1, 2, 3, 4, 19, 20, 21, 22, 23”; “‘25’ (Predetermination Pricing Only - No Payment) does not apply to Medicare.” CMS Ch.22 mapping: “Codes 5-17, 25 and 27 do not apply to Medicare.” BCBSIL PLB guide reversal example: “CLP*SMITH001*22*-285*-173.45**MC*180050B99990*11~” then “CLP*SMITH001*1*285*157.83**MC*180050B99991*11~”, net -15.62 “reflected in the PLB04 with the Reason Code ‘WO’ (negative)”. X12 RFI #2325: “Reporting N702, OA 96 and CO 45 for the previously paid lines is not correct. Instead the service lines are to be reported in the reversal claim (CLP02=22) with all original amounts reversed” per TR3 §1.10.2.8 Reversals and Corrections. RFI #2143 (secondary reporting): “Every claim reports the same original submitted charge, not just the unpaid balance”; “Report any prior payer(s) payment and adjustment amounts other than patient responsibility ... using Claim Adjustment Group Code OA and CARC 23”; coverage/allowed “using qualifier AU (claim level) or B6 (service level)”. RFI #2535 quoting §1.10.2.13: the impact “may be up to the actual amount of the primary payment(s) plus contractual adjustment(s)”. ODA files: Medicare file CLP02 distribution 19×84, 1×11, 2×1 (crossovers to supplements); Tricare for Life file CLP02=2 with CAS*OA*23*105.97 and LQ*HE*N23, line SVC*HC:99214*127.80*21.83 with AMT*B6*107.42; Mutual of Omaha CLP02=2 with OA 23 on every line; Anthem FEP as secondary used CAS*OA*94 (“Processed in Excess of charges.”) with N431.

**What it means for ODA:** Rules: exclude CLP02=22 claims from underpayment math and net each with its corrected claim (match CLP01 patient control number + payer claim number CLP07 or DOS); a correction that lowers the E/M after an earlier payment is a distinct 'retroactive downcode' event. For CLP02 in {2,3,20,21} compare AMT B6 to the primary's allowed, not to the fee schedule, and treat OA 23 as COB, never as payer reduction. CLP02=4 claims carrying CO 252 + N463 (UHC) or CO 252 + N4 (Humana MA, with REF*0K*RECONSIDERATION) are records/COB denials to route to a resubmission queue, not write-offs. CLP02=19 means a crossover remit from the supplement should follow; do not count PR amounts as lost until it arrives.

Sources: [cgsmedicare.com](https://www.cgsmedicare.com/pdf/edi/835_compguide.pdf) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/regulations-and-guidance/guidance/manuals/downloads/clm104c22_remit_phase3_apr24-03_r2.pdf) · [bcbsil.com](https://www.bcbsil.com/docs/provider/il/claims/payment/plb-segment-on-era-government-programs.pdf) · [x12.org](https://x12.org/resources/requests-for-interpretation/rfi-2325-reversal-and-correction-method) · [x12.org](https://x12.org/resources/requests-for-interpretation/rfi-2143-secondary-claim-reporting-cob) · [x12.org](https://x12.org/resources/requests-for-interpretation/rfi-2535-835-secondary-payment-oa23-clarifications) · dropbox:/Trizetto Data/Trizetto/PTDDIRD<group NPI>U13342-<trace>-TricareforLife-NoTaxId.RMT · dropbox:/Trizetto Data/Trizetto/P71412D<payee id>U59329-<trace>-MUTUALOFOMAHACOMMERCIAL-<practice id>.RMT

### [UNVERIFIED] Claim adjustment group codes are CO (contractual obligations), PR (patient responsibility), OA (other adjustments) and, for non-Medicare payers, PI (payor initiated reductions); Medicare never uses PI. The official x12.org texts for the CARCs that accompany E/M level reductions, code changes, bundling and multiple-procedure reductions are listed verbatim below (accessed 2026-11-06).

CMS Ch.22 §60.1: “CO - Contractual Obligations. This group code shall be used when a contractual agreement between the payer and payee, or a regulatory requirement, resulted in an adjustment. Generally, these adjustments are considered a write off for the provider and are not billed to the patient.” “OA - Other Adjustments. This group code shall be used when no other group code applies to the adjustment.” “PR - Patient Responsibility. This group code shall be used when the adjustment represent an amount that may be billed to the patient or insured.” CGS CG: “Medicare contractors are limited to use of the ‘CO’, ‘OA’, and ‘PR’ group codes; ‘PI’ is not used.” (PI = “Payor Initiated Reductions” per payer companion guides; UMR uses PI*B10 in ODA files.) x12.org CARC texts — 4: “The procedure code is inconsistent with the modifier used.” 11: “The diagnosis is inconsistent with the procedure.” 16: “Claim/service lacks information or has submission/billing error(s). Usage: Do not use this code for claims attachment(s)/other documentation. At least one Remark Code must be provided (may be comprised of either the NCPDP Reject Reason Code, or Remittance Advice Remark Code that is not an ALERT.)” 18: “Exact duplicate claim/service (Use only with Group Code OA except where state workers' compensation regulations requires CO)”. 22: “This care may be covered by another payer per coordination of benefits.” 23: “The impact of prior payer(s) adjudication including payments and/or adjustments. (Use only with Group Code OA)”. 29: “The time limit for filing has expired.” 45: “Charge exceeds fee schedule/maximum allowable or contracted/legislated fee arrangement. Usage: This adjustment amount cannot equal the total service or claim charge amount; and must not duplicate provider adjustment amounts (payments and contractual reductions) that have resulted from prior payer(s) adjudication. (Use only with Group Codes PR or CO depending upon liability)”. 50: “These are non-covered services because this is not deemed a 'medical necessity' by the payer.” 59: “Processed based on multiple or concurrent procedure rules. (For example multiple surgery or diagnostic imaging, concurrent anesthesia.)” 94: “Processed in Excess of charges.” 96: “Non-covered charge(s). At least one Remark Code must be provided...” 97: “The benefit for this service is included in the payment/allowance for another service/procedure that has already been adjudicated.” 121: “Indemnification adjustment - compensation for outstanding member responsibility.” 144: “Incentive adjustment, e.g. preferred product/service.” 150: “Payer deems the information submitted does not support this level of service.” (Start 10/31/2002, modified 09/30/2007). 151: “Payment adjusted because the payer deems the information submitted does not support this many/frequency of services.” 186: “Level of care change adjustment.” (Start 06/30/2005). 187: “Consumer Spending Account payments (includes but is not limited to Flexible Spending Account, Health Savings Account, Health Reimbursement Account, etc.)” 197: “Precertification/authorization/notification/pre-treatment absent.” 234: “This procedure is not paid separately. At least one Remark Code must be provided...” 236: “This procedure or procedure/modifier combination is not compatible with another procedure or procedure/modifier combination provided on the same day according to the National Correct Coding Initiative or workers compensation state regulations/ fee schedule requirements.” 252: “An attachment/other documentation is required to adjudicate this claim/service. At least one Remark Code must be provided...” 253: “Sequestration - reduction in federal payment”. B10: “Allowed amount has been reduced because a component of the basic procedure/test was paid. The beneficiary is not liable for more than the charge limit for the basic procedure/test.” B15: “This service/procedure requires that a qualifying service/procedure be received and covered. The qualifying other service/procedure has not been received/adjudicated.” Codes 4, 11, 16, 50, 59, 96, 97, B15 carry the usage note “Refer to the 835 Healthcare Policy Identification Segment (loop 2110 Service Payment Information REF), if present.”

**What it means for ODA:** Dashboard code-category map: downcode signals {150, 186}; bundling/E-M-25 edits {97, B10, B15, 236, 234, 4}; MPPR {59}; contractual {45 — but a 45 equal to the full charge is a denial in disguise}; timely filing {29}; authorization {197}; medical necessity {50}; non-covered {96}; documentation {252, 16}; COB {22, 23, 18, 94}; patient account offsets {187 negative PR}; sequestration {253 expected 2% on Medicare/MA}; MIPS/incentive {144 negative = bonus}. Group code sets liability: PI/CO with a denial CARC = provider loss; PR = patient balance. Display official text beside every code in evidence packets.

Sources: [x12.org](https://x12.org/codes/claim-adjustment-reason-codes) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/regulations-and-guidance/guidance/manuals/downloads/clm104c22.pdf) · [cgsmedicare.com](https://www.cgsmedicare.com/pdf/edi/835_compguide.pdf)

### [UNVERIFIED] Official x12.org Remittance Advice Remark Code texts (accessed 2026-11-06) for the codes relevant to E/M downcoding, code edits and bundling; N22 marks a payer-initiated code change, M85/N610/M25/M26 mark E/M level review or reduction, and N793 is a deactivated MBI alert unrelated to downcoding.

N22: “Alert: This procedure code was added/changed because it more accurately describes the services rendered.” (modified 07/01/2015). M85: “Subjected to review of physician evaluation and management services.” M25: “The information furnished does not substantiate the need for this level of service...” (modified 11/01/2010). M26: “The information furnished does not substantiate the need for this level of service...” (also refer to N356). N610: “Alert: Payment based on an appropriate level of care.” (start 07/15/2013). M127: “Missing patient medical record for this service.” N657: “This should be billed with the appropriate code for these services.” (start 07/15/2013). N381: “Alert: Consult our contractual agreement for restrictions/billing/payment information related to these charges.” M80: “Not covered when performed during the same session/date as a previously processed service for the patient.” N431: “Not covered with this procedure.” N463: “Missing support data for claim.” N793: “Alert: Starting January 1, 2020, Medicare will ONLY accept claims submitted with the Medicare Beneficiary Identifier (MBI)...” (Stop 07/01/2020). N19: “Procedure code incidental to primary procedure.” N20: “Service not payable with other service rendered on the same date.” M144: “Pre-/post-operative care payment is included in the allowance for the surgery/procedure.” M15: “Separately billed services/tests have been bundled as they are considered components of the same procedure. Separate payment is not allowed.” M86: “Service denied because payment already made for same/similar procedure within set time frame.” N30: “Patient ineligible for this service.” N56: “Procedure code billed is not correct/valid for the services billed or the date of service billed.” N130: “Consult plan benefit documents/guidelines for information about restrictions for this service.” N174: “This is not a covered service/procedure/equipment/bed, however patient liability is limited to amounts shown in the adjustments under group 'PR'.” Also seen in ODA files: N4 “Missing/Incomplete/Invalid prior Insurance Carrier(s) EOB.”; N23 “Alert: Patient liability may be affected due to coordination of benefits with other carriers and/or maximum benefit provisions.”; N525 “These services are not covered when performed within the global period of another service.”; N702 “Decision based on review of previously adjudicated claims or for claims in process for the same/similar type of services.”; N807 “Payment adjustment based on the Merit-based Incentive Payment System (MIPS).” Per CARC rules, 16, 96, 234 and 252 require a non-ALERT RARC.

**What it means for ODA:** RARC-driven classification: N22 => code changed (expect SVC06; if SVC06 absent, flag TR3 non-compliance); M85/N610/M25/M26 => E/M level review/downcode even when the billed code is unchanged; M15/N19/N20/M144/N525/M80/N431 => bundling, same-day or global-period edits (E/M-25, G2211, modifier 79/24 graft/repair denials — appealable with documentation); M127/N463/N729/N4 => records or COB attachment requests (resubmit, not appeal); N381/N130 => contract/plan restriction (pull the contract clause); N56/N657/M86 => coding or frequency edits; N30/N174 => eligibility/non-covered. Ignore N793.

Sources: [x12.org](https://x12.org/codes/remittance-advice-remark-codes) · dropbox:/Trizetto Data/Trizetto/P61101D<payee id>U135947-<trace>-HUMANA-<practice id>.RMT · dropbox:/Trizetto Data/Trizetto/P15102D<practice id>U33232-<trace>-KYMEDICARE-<practice id>.RMT

### [UNVERIFIED] PLB03-1 provider-level adjustment codes have official X12 definitions (WO overpayment recovery, FB non-claim balance forward, 72 provider refund/authorized return, L6 interest, CS adjustment explained separately, AH claim transmission/origination fee, BD bad debt, 50 late charge, 51 late-filing interest penalty, 90 early payment allowance); a positive PLB04 reduces the payment and a negative one increases it; Medicare Part B uses only CS, AP, FB, LE, L6, 50, SL, WO, B2, J1 and IR and never AH.

x12.org Provider Adjustment Reason Codes: 50 “The amount of the late charge, late claim filing penalty, or Medicare late cost report penalty.”; 51 “Late filing interest penalty assessment amount”; 72 “Provider refund amount”; 90 “Early payment allowance amount”; AH “Claim transmission fee amount”; AM “Loan repayment amount”; AP “Advanced or accelerated payment amount”; B2 “Rebate amount”; B3 “Recovery amount”; BD “Bad debt amount”; BN “Bonus amount”; CS “Adjustment amount, detailed information is provided separately to explain the adjustment.”; E3 “Withholding amount”; FB “Non-claim related balance forward amount”; HM “Hemophilia clotting factor add-on payment amount”; IP “Incentive payment amount”; IR “Internal Revenue Service 1099 withholding amount”; IS “Lump sum based on an interim rate”; J1 “Amount not reimbursed based on a demonstration program or other limitation that prevents issuance of payment.”; L3 “Penalty amount”; L6 “Interest amount”; LE “Internal Revenue Service non-1099 withholding amount”; OB “Affiliated provider(s) offset amount”; PI “Periodic Interim Payment (PIP) lump sum amount”; PL “Final payment or settlement amount”; RA “Retroactive adjustment amount”; SL “Student loan garnishment amount”; TL “Third party liability determination amount”; WO “Overpayment recovery amount”; WU “Non-Internal Revenue Service withholding amount related to a federal payment levy program”. CMS transmittal R812OTN: 4010 codes “50, 51, 72, 90, AM, AP, B2, B3, BD, BN, C5, CR, CS, CT, CV, CW, DM, E3, FB, FC, GO, IP, IR, IS, J1, L3, L6, LE, LS, OA, OB, PI, PL, RA, RE, SL, TL, WO, WU, AND ZZ. In version 005010A1, two new codes – AH and HM – have been added, and code ZZ has been deleted”; its crosswalk gives 5010 TR3 labels 50 “Late Charge”, 72 “Authorized return”, AP “Acceleration of Benefits”, BD “Bad Debt Adjustment”, BN “Bonus”, C5 “Temporary Allowance”, CS “Adjustment”, E3 “Withholding”, IR “Internal Revenue Service”, IS “Interim Settlement”, L3 “Penalty”, LE “Levy”. CGS CG PLB03-1: “Medicare does not use ‘AH’, ‘AM’, ‘CR’, ‘CT’, ‘CW’, or ‘FC’.” CMS Ch.22 (Part B): “PLB03-1 Required. Only codes CS, AP, FB, LE, L6, 50, SL, WO, B2, J1, and IR apply to Medicare carriers.” Sign convention (CMS Ch.22 §20): “Positive adjustment amounts reduce the amount of the payment and negative adjustment amounts increase it.” UHC 835 PLB guide (PCA-1-24-02982-POE-QRG_10252024): FB “A negative value represents a balance moving forward to a future payment advice. A positive value represents a balance being applied from a previous payment advice”; WO positive = recouped, negative = overpayment reported but not yet recouped; refund received = negative 72 offset by positive WO; L6 reported “as a negative amount and adds to the check total ... sum of the AMT*I segments”; IR positive = IRS withholding; J1 positive = suppressed payment; CS = reissued lost check or FB write-off (“Payer Write-Off”); PLB03-2 carries TRN02 (FB), DOS + patient account number (WO/72) or voided check number. BCBSIL examples: “PLB*1999999999*20261231*L6*-25~”; “PLB*15483NN082*20261231*WO:JONES001 181580099999*-1156~” (identified) vs positive (withheld after 90 days MA/60 days Medicaid); “WO:JONES001 CHKNO 4873500*57.58*72:JONES001 CHKNO 4873500*-57.58”.

**What it means for ODA:** Parse all six PLB pairs (PLB03/04 ... PLB13/14) with PLB03-2 reference text. Categorize: fees (AH; CS whose reference text contains FEE), recoupments (WO positive, FB), interest income (L6 negative), refunds acknowledged (72 negative + WO positive, net zero), withholding (IR/LE/WU/SL), write-offs by payer (CS negative with FB). Tie WO amounts to reversed CLP pairs and to overpayment letters; a 'Fees & recoupments' tile should separate avoidable fees from legitimate recoupments. AH in a Medicare file would be anomalous.

Sources: [x12.org](https://x12.org/codes/provider-adjustment-reason-codes) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/regulations-and-guidance/guidance/transmittals/downloads/r812otn.pdf) · [cgsmedicare.com](https://www.cgsmedicare.com/pdf/edi/835_compguide.pdf) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/regulations-and-guidance/guidance/manuals/downloads/clm104c22_remit_phase3_apr24-03_r2.pdf) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/regulations-and-guidance/guidance/manuals/downloads/clm104c22.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/resources/edi/EDI-835-Provider-Level-Adjustments.pdf) · [bcbsil.com](https://www.bcbsil.com/docs/provider/il/claims/payment/plb-segment-on-era-government-programs.pdf)

### [UNVERIFIED] Third-party payment-vendor fees are deducted inside the 835: Zelis fees appear in ODA's files as PLB <payee>*<FYE>*CS:ZELIS TRANSACTION FEE*<amount> at a constant 2.47% of gross claim payments, and UHC/Optum uses PLB reason AH with a numeric reference; these fees are avoidable by enrolling in the payer's no-fee standard ACH.

UMR file: BPR02 1225.55; sum of CLP04 = 1256.59 (0+60.57+269.05+68.33+91.31+95.02+409.14+120.74+142.43+0+0+0); PLB*<practice id>*20261231*CS:ZELIS TRANSACTION FEE*31.04 → 31.04/1256.59 = 2.47%. Mutual of Omaha file (payment 2024-01-08): BPR02 69.11, PLB*<group NPI>*20241231*CS:ZELIS TRANSACTION FEE*1.75 → 1.75/70.86 = 2.47%. The prompt's UHC example PLB*<group NPI>*20261231*AH:<reference>*21.89 uses AH, whose current x12 definition is “Claim transmission fee amount” (added in 5010 per CMS R812OTN); Optum's own OptumRx 835 PLB sheet maps AH:530 = “Transaction Fees”, AH:444 = “DIR fees”, AH:750 = “Performance Network Fees”, CS:02 = adjustments. CS official text: “Adjustment amount, detailed information is provided separately to explain the adjustment.” Bomi (billing vendor) on UMR: UMR “delivers its provider payments through Zelis”; virtual card and fee-based “fast” EFT carry “a 2–3% processing fee”; opt out of the virtual card, enroll in UMR's “no-fee standard ACH EFT through its payer-sponsored Zelis ePayment Center”, a Zelis enrollment for another payer does not cover UMR, and if refused “file a CMS Administrative Simplification (ASETT) complaint” (vendor assertion; verify). UHC provider site (search result): Optum Pay Basic ACH no cost, Premium 0.5% per ACH. Zelis publishes no rate (“Specific fee information is disclosed at the time of enrollment”).

**What it means for ODA:** Add a 'fee leakage' KPI: sum PLB amounts where PLB03-1 in {AH} or (PLB03-1 = CS and PLB03-2 matches FEE), grouped by payer and month, with annualized run-rate and % of gross payments (expect 2.47% on Zelis-routed payers). Each dollar is recoverable going forward by switching the payer to standard no-fee ACH; list the payers (UMR, Mutual of Omaha, any 'PZELIS' routed payer, UHC AH) as an action queue. Alert when a new PLB reference string appears.

### [UNVERIFIED] An 835 must balance at three levels — service line (SVC02 minus all service CAS amounts = SVC03), claim (CLP03 minus claim-level and service-level CAS = CLP04) and transaction (sum of CLP04 minus all PLB amounts = BPR02); AMT*B6 is the service allowed amount (allowed = paid + patient-responsibility CAS), AMT*AU is the claim coverage amount, and REF*6R returns the line item control number sent on the 837 — all verified in ODA's files.

CMS Ch.22 §30: “these remittances must balance at the service, claim, and provider levels... As a failsafe measure claim adjustment reason code121 and PLB reason code 90 may be used at the line, claim, and provider level respectively”; §20: “The check amount is the sum of all claim-level payments, including claims and service-level adjustments, less any provider level adjustments.” NCPDP WG45 835 FAQ v2.2 (Dec 2025) 2.6.1: CLP03 minus the sum of CAS amounts must equal CLP04 — “Yes” (TR3 §1.10.2.1.3); 2.6.2: sum of CLP04 minus sum of PLB04/06/08/10/12/14 equals BPR02 — “Yes”. BCBSIL: “The sum of all claim payments (CLP04) minus the sum of all provider level adjustments (in the PLB segment) equals the total payment (BPR02).” ODA UMR file checks: SVC*HC:99214*223.65*95.02 with CAS*PR*3*20.00 and CAS*CO*45*108.63 → 223.65-20.00-108.63 = 95.02; AMT*B6*115.02 = 95.02 + 20.00; transaction: 1256.59 - 31.04 = 1225.55 = BPR02. Anthem FEP: SVC*HC:99213*146.79*10.00, CO 45 86.79, PR 3 50.00, B6 60.00 = 10.00 + 50.00. Medicare file: AMT*B6 on all 221 lines. RFI #2143: allowed/coverage reported “using qualifier AU (claim level) or B6 (service level)”; CGS CG 2110 AMT01 values “B6, KH, 2K, ZL, ZM, ZN, ZO”, Medicare Part B “Only KH and B6 apply”. Negative CAS amounts increase payment (UMR CAS*CO*45*-64.15; Medicare CO 144 MIPS; PR 187 consumer-account offsets like CAS*PR*2*41.41**187*-41.41). REF*6R on every ODA line carries EMA's 8-digit line control number (e.g., 50000001); a UMR payer-created bundling line carried REF*6R*3.

**What it means for ODA:** Implement the three balance assertions as ingestion QA; files or claims that fail indicate parser bugs or payer errors and must not feed dollar totals. Use AMT B6 as the allowed amount for fee-schedule and downcode comparisons (paid amounts are distorted by deductibles); fall back to SVC03 + sum of PR CAS when B6 is absent. Join 835 lines to EMA charge lines on REF*6R first, then CLP01 + DOS + code. Use AMT AU on secondary claims. Treat negative CAS as payment increases, never as underpayments.

### [UNVERIFIED] ODA's TriZetto .RMT file names decode as P<TriZetto payer ID>D<payee identifier>U<TriZetto-assigned number>-<TRN02 check/EFT trace number>-<TriZetto payer name without spaces>-<payee TIN or 'NoTaxId'>.RMT; the payee token is REF*PQ when present, else REF*TJ (TIN <practice id>), else the N1*PE NPI; the 'U' number does not occur inside the file; TriZetto re-envelopes every 835 (ISA08/GS03 = ODA site ID '3LDA', sequential ISA13) and file dates reflect delivery, not payment.

Verified by reading five files. UMR: ISA06/GS02/REF*2U = 39026 (UMR's standard payer ID), TRN02 = <trace> (= filename token), REF*EV = REF*TJ = <practice id>, N1*PE NPI <group NPI>. Anthem: ISA06 = ANTHM (filename PANTHMD), TRN02 = <trace>, REF*EV = FEP, REF*TJ = <practice id>. Humana: ISA06 = HUMANA but filename P61101 (TriZetto's ID for Humana), D<payee id> = REF*PQ*<payee id>, TRN02 = <trace>. Mutual of Omaha: P71412 = ISA06 71412, D<payee id> = REF*PQ*<payee id>, TRN02 = <trace>. Tricare for Life: PTDDIR = ISA06 TDDIR, D<group NPI> = N1*PE NPI (no REF*PQ, no REF*TJ → trailing 'NoTaxId'), TRN02 = <trace>. KY Medicare: P15102 = ISA06 15102 (CGS), D<practice id> = REF*TJ, TRN02 = <trace>, REF*EV = Z6UP. Payer-name token is TriZetto's payer-list name (e.g., 'UnitedHealthCareofallstates', 'Wausau' for UMR 39026, 'KYMEDICARE'), not N1*PR verbatim. U-token values in a 12-file listing range from U297 to U835747 with no order by payer or date, and 'U33232' / '835747' do not occur in file text. ISA13 values rise with ISA date across payers (<trace>, <trace>, <trace> on 2026-08-31; <trace>, <trace>, <trace> on 2026-09-04); every file is one ISA/GS/ST. Delivery vs payment: UMR ISA date 260904 but BPR16 20260423; Humana ISA 260831 but BPR16 20240301; Medicare ISA 260831 but BPR16 20240228; Mutual of Omaha ISA 260831 but BPR16 20240108 — a bulk historical redelivery; Dropbox client_modified 2026-08-31..09-05, server_modified 2026-09-10. TriZetto portal ERA options documented by PM vendors: “Use Multiple File Name Options”, “Use Check Number in File Name”, “Use Check Amount in File Name”, “Use Payer Name in File Name” (SOAPware), “Use Check Date in File Name” and “File Format: X12 835 5010” (ReLiMed) — consistent with the trace-number and payer-name tokens. ChiroTouch's TriZetto guide notes the Site ID is four characters, matching '3LDA'.

**What it means for ODA:** Never derive payer or date from the file name: payer = N1*PR + REF*2U (+ ISA06), payment date = BPR16, production date = DTM*405. Dedupe on (REF*2U, TRN02, BPR02, BPR16) rather than filename or ISA13 (redeliveries get new ISA13 values); the prior 33,615 files → 32,617 unique 835s likely reflects redelivery. The weekly job must be idempotent against bulk historical re-pulls and must not use Dropbox mtime as 'new this week'. Filename tokens can still serve as a fast pre-filter (payer ID, trace number).

### [UNVERIFIED] Payers increasingly downcode without changing the billed code: the 835 then shows the submitted E/M in SVC01 with a reduced allowed amount (AMT B6) and an ordinary CO 45, no SVC06 and often no explanatory RARC; when flagged at all, the signals are CARC 150 or 186 and RARC M85, N610, M25/M26, or N22 if the code was changed. Cigna (R49, DOS on/after 2025-10-01), Anthem (policy dated 2026-02-15, paused in California through at least 2026-09-01), Aetna and reportedly Humana run such programs.

AMA ‘Payer evaluation and management (E/M) downcoding programs — what you need to know’ (2022): “certain payers even pay at the adjusted lower rate without changing the billed code, making it extremely difficult to identify in coding audits”; sample payer policy text: “we may adjust your payment if the claim detail doesn't support the billed level of service. We will not change the procedure code you bill.”; example remark codes “CO150 ... M85 ... N610 Alert: Payment based on appropriate level of care ... CARC 186: Level of care change adjustment”; staff should be “comparing the payment for each billed CPT code to the anticipated amount”; watch 99204/99205/99214/99215 and modifier 25 claims. AMA policy: “if payment is adjusted, then the corresponding code should be adjusted accordingly, and clear remittance advice should be provided.” Cigna R49 (AAFP): effective Oct. 1, 2025; codes 99204-99205, 99214-99215, 99244-99245; one-level reduction when “the primary diagnosis and other claim-based criteria” do not support the level; contest by faxing documentation to 833-392-2092; bypass request to EMCodingAccuracy@CignaHealthcare.com for physicians with ≥5 downcoded claims, granted if ≥80% of reviewed adjusted claims were billed appropriately; Cigna expected ~99% of clinicians unaffected; paused in California. natrevmd: Maryland's regulator ordered Cigna to stop automatic downcoding in March 2026; the page's claim that Cigna uses CO-150 is the author's interpretation, not a Cigna document. ACDIS: Cigna “can unilaterally downcode claims by one level”; providers “will now be required to file an appeal to justify the initial coding.” Anthem (CMA): automatic E/M downcoding policy “originally scheduled to take effect Feb. 15, 2026”, pause extended “through Sept. 1, 2026” while regulators review; states covered not stated. Aetna (search snippets only; pages not fetched): program expanded March 2025 to all commercial states except Louisiana, adjustments “never ... less than a Level 3”, early removal after ~5–6 months of successful appeals. Humana auto-downcoding reported by AOA (page unreachable, HTTP 503).

**What it means for ODA:** Detection rule D2 (silent downcode): for E/M lines without SVC06, compare AMT B6 to the expected allowed for the billed code (contracted fee schedule, or empirical modal B6 per payer/code/year from ODA's own history, e.g., 2025 primary-payer allowed: Medicare KY 99213 ~$70.81–83.30, BCBS 99213 $51.00 / 99214 $63.89, Humana MA 99213 $66.64, UHC MA 99213 $76.64); if B6 matches the lower level's allowed within ±2% → 'probable silent downcode'. Rule D3: any CARC 150/186 or RARC M85/N610/M25/M26/N22 → 'payer-flagged downcode'. Dollar impact = expected allowed(billed) − B6 (not charge-based). Track per-payer monthly rates to expose the start/stop cycles ODA has experienced. Appeal packets: AMA template plus Cigna fax/email routes; fee schedules are a required data input (gap).

Sources: [ama-assn.org](https://www.ama-assn.org/system/files/payer-em-downcoding-resource.pdf) · [aafp.org](https://www.aafp.org/fpm/blogs/getting-paid/cigna-downcoding-em) · [natrevmd.com](https://natrevmd.com/cignas-downcoding-policy-r49-what-denial-code-c150-means-for-your-practice/) · [acdis.org](https://acdis.org/articles/news-new-cigna-policy-automatically-%E2%80%98downcode%E2%80%99-certain-em-codes) · [cmadocs.org](https://www.cmadocs.org/newsroom/news/view/anthem-extends-pause-on-e-m-downcoding-policy-through-sept-1)

### [UNVERIFIED] Under TR3 §1.10.2.6, bundling must be reported by paying the bundled line on the changed code with the original code in SVC06 and adjusting the other submitted lines off with CARC 97 (or OA 94 when the paying line's charge is rolled up); in ODA's files E/M-with-modifier-25 and G2211 denials appear as the E/M line paid $0 under PI/CO B10, 97 or a full-charge CO 45 while the procedure pays, and global-period denials appear as CO 97 + N525.

RFI #2320 (X12) quoting §1.10.2.6 para 4: “When bundling, report all of the originally submitted procedures in the remittance advice. Report all procedures as paying on the changed (bundled) procedure code, and reference the original submitted code in SVC06.” and “Report the other procedure or procedures as originally submitted, with an adjudicated code of the bundled procedure code and a Claim Adjustment Reason Code of 97”; the RFI question describes CO*97 plus “CAS OA*94 with a negative amount” on the rolled-up line, and X12 required SVC06 on that line. RFI #2551 confirms §1.10.2.6 governs bundling. X12 8020 change log CR 1091: use the adjustment quantity “with the OA*94 adjustment to reduce the units to zero for each of the services that aren't paying.” ODA examples (UMR): SVC*HC:99214:25*223.65*0.00~CAS*PI*B10*223.65 on a claim where SVC*HC:17110*177.56*91.31 paid; SVC*HC:G2211*29.19*0.00~CAS*PI*B10*29.19; bundling line SVC*HC:11401:51*0.00*64.15***HC:11401~CAS*CO*45*-64.15~REF*6R*3~LQ*HE*M15 while the 12032 line's charge equals the whole claim charge (482.98) and AMT*AU*732.44. UHC (prompt excerpt): SVC*HC:G2211*26.02*0.00~CAS*CO*45*26.02~LQ*HE*M80 — a CO 45 equal to the full charge contradicts the CARC 45 usage note (“This adjustment amount cannot equal the total service or claim charge amount”) and OneHealthPort guidance “Do not use CO45 to represent a denial.” Humana MA: SVC*HC:15260:79*932.73*0.00~CAS*CO*97*932.73~LQ*HE*N525 (graft denied as within a global period despite modifier 79). B10 text: “Allowed amount has been reduced because a component of the basic procedure/test was paid...”; M15: “Separately billed services/tests have been bundled...”

**What it means for ODA:** Rules: (B1) E/M 99202-99215 with modifier 25, SVC03 = 0 and CAS code in {B10, 97, 236, 234, B15} on a claim where a procedure line paid → 'E/M-25 bundling denial' (appeal with significant-and-separately-identifiable documentation); dollarize at expected allowed of the E/M. (B2) G2211 denials by commercial payers → separate policy category (Medicare pays G2211). (B3) CO 45 amount == SVC02 → 'denial coded as contractual' — never auto-write-off; this is the exact mechanism by which billers posted silent denials/downcodes as contractual adjustments. (B4) SVC02 = 0 with SVC06 present → TR3 bundling representation; reconstruct the original charge from EMA, not from SVC02. (B5) CO 97 with N525/M144 on lines carrying modifier 79/24/25/57 → global-period bundling appeal.

Sources: [x12.org](https://x12.org/resources/requests-for-interpretation/rfi-2320-835-svc06-requirement) · [x12.org](https://x12.org/resources/requests-for-interpretation/rfi-2551-835-bundling) · [aahamwi.starchapter.com](https://aahamwi.starchapter.com/images/downloads/aaham_hot_topic_document.pdf) · [x12.org](https://x12.org/codes/claim-adjustment-reason-codes) · [onehealthport.com](https://www.onehealthport.com/sites/default/files/content-uploads/bpr/Processing_Reporting_Remittance_Information.pdf) · dropbox:/Trizetto Data/Trizetto/P39026D<practice id>U835747-<trace>-UMR-<practice id>.RMT · dropbox:/Trizetto Data/Trizetto/P61101D<payee id>U135947-<trace>-HUMANA-<practice id>.RMT

### [UNVERIFIED] In ODA's Medicare (CGS) 835s, CARC 59 lines are multiple-procedure payment reductions in which CGS appends modifier 51 and reports the submitted composite in SVC06; CO 253 (2% sequestration) and CO 144 with N807 (MIPS, negative = bonus) appear on nearly every line; and most claims are CLP02 19 crossovers — none of these are downcoding and all would create false positives if not whitelisted.

KY Medicare file: 96 claims, 221 lines; CLP02 19×84, 1×11, 2×1; CAS counts CO 253 ×210, PR 2 ×210, CO 144 ×208, PR 1 ×24, CO 59 ×22, CO 45 ×17; LQ N807 ×208, N782 ×1; SVC06 populated on exactly the 22 CO 59 lines, all differing from SVC01 only by a payer-added 51 (e.g., SVC*HC:17311:51*612.98*260.13**1*HC:17311; SVC*HC:29580:RT:51*57.43*24.38**1*HC:29580:RT); no PLB; AMT B6 on all 221 lines. Official texts: 59 “Processed based on multiple or concurrent procedure rules...”; 253 “Sequestration - reduction in federal payment”; 144 “Incentive adjustment, e.g. preferred product/service.”; N807 “Payment adjustment based on the Merit-based Incentive Payment System (MIPS).” Prior ODA analysis: 10,724 service lines carrying CARC 59 across the corpus. CGS CG: Medicare uses only CO/OA/PR group codes, LQ qualifier HE only, CLP02 25 never; CMS Ch.22: SVC06 required when Medicare changes a code or modifier.

**What it means for ODA:** Classify SVC06 differences before calling anything a downcode: if the only difference is an added 51 (or 59/XS/XU) and CAS contains 59 → 'MPPR' (validate the 100%/50% reduction against the MPFS multiple-procedure indicator; destruction, biopsy and repair codes are MPPR-eligible), not downcoding. Treat CO 253 as an expected 2% of (allowed − PR) on Medicare and MA remits; treat negative CO 144/N807 as MIPS bonus income; expect a supplemental 835 after CLP02 19. Any PI group code in a Medicare file is anomalous.

Sources: dropbox:/Trizetto Data/Trizetto/P15102D<practice id>U33232-<trace>-KYMEDICARE-<practice id>.RMT · [x12.org](https://x12.org/codes/claim-adjustment-reason-codes) · [x12.org](https://x12.org/codes/remittance-advice-remark-codes) · [cgsmedicare.com](https://www.cgsmedicare.com/pdf/edi/835_compguide.pdf) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/regulations-and-guidance/guidance/manuals/downloads/clm104c22_remit_phase3_apr24-03_r2.pdf)

### [UNVERIFIED] Several behaviors visible in ODA's remits are non-compliant with X12/TR3 rules and can be cited in appeals, payer-rep escalations or HIPAA transaction-standard complaints: changing a code without SVC06, coding a denial as CO 45 for the full charge, sending CARC 16/96/234/252 without a non-alert RARC, and reversing payments with $0 lines instead of CLP02=22; AMA's sample appeal letter and policy language supply the narrative.

RFI #2320: “If the submitted procedure code is not included in the SVC01, then it must be included in the SVC06.” CARC 45 usage: “This adjustment amount cannot equal the total service or claim charge amount.” CARC 16, 96, 234, 252: “At least one Remark Code must be provided (may be comprised of either the NCPDP Reject Reason Code, or Remittance Advice Remark Code that is not an ALERT.)” RFI #2325: $0 lines with N702/OA 96/CO 45 instead of a CLP02=22 reversal “is not correct.” CMS Ch.22 §60.1: “MACs do not have discretion to omit appropriate codes and messages.” CAQH CORE 360 (CMS MM11881, search result): Medicare must use CORE-required CARC/RARC combinations for the four CORE business scenarios; CAQH updates the combinations three times a year. AMA sample E/M appeal letter: “[Health insurer] has inappropriately downcoded the CPT E/M code submitted and changed the code to [new code], resulting in the inappropriate reduction of payment for delivered medical care... Downcoding of CPT E/M codes is not appropriate without review of medical record documentation.” and quotes AMA policy: “The AMA vigorously opposes the practice of unilateral, arbitrary recoding and/or bundling by all payers.” AMA principles: outliers only, education first, medical-record review before reduction, prior notification, clear remittance (“if payment is adjusted, then the corresponding code should be adjusted accordingly, and clear remittance advice should be provided”). Bomi (vendor): CMS ASETT complaint route for payers refusing no-fee EFT (unverified scope).

**What it means for ODA:** The appeal-packet generator should include an '835 evidence' page: verbatim SVC/CAS/LQ/AMT segments, official code texts, balancing math, EMA charge-line comparison and, where applicable, the TR3/X12 compliance citation (RFI numbers). Aggregate per-payer 'remittance compliance defects' (CO45-full-charge lines, code changes without SVC06, CARC 16/252 without RARC, improper reversals) as leverage in payer-rep escalations and as the basis for CMS ASETT complaints (confirm ASETT applies to HIPAA 835 content violations).

### Open questions

- Meaning of the 'U<number>' token in TriZetto file names (e.g., U835747, U33232): not present in file content; likely a TriZetto remit/batch sequence — confirm with TriZetto Provider Solutions support, and confirm whether the trailing 'Use Check Amount/Date' options are enabled for ODA's account.
- Which ODA payers ever populate SVC06 for E/M code changes (payer breakdown of the 116 prior SVC06>SVC01 claims) — must be computed from the full corpus; Anthem, Humana, UHC commercial/MA, Cigna and Aetna have no public 835 companion-guide statement on SVC06 (only UHC's Vision guide, which says Not Used).
- Verbatim 005010X221A1 TR3 text of the SVC06/SVC07 situational rules and sections 1.10.2.6 (bundling), 1.10.2.8 (reversals), 1.10.2.13 (secondary) — the TR3 is licensed; only X12 RFI quotations and CMS/payer paraphrases were available.
- Exact 5010 TR3 label for PLB code AH (commonly tabulated as 'Origination Fee'; the current x12.org list says 'Claim transmission fee amount') and what UHC's PLB03-2 reference '<reference>' denotes (Optum Pay premium fee, virtual card, or other).
- Cigna R49 remittance presentation (specific CARC/RARC or proprietary explanation codes) and whether R49 is active for Kentucky claims as of November 2026 after the California pause and the March 2026 Maryland order.
- Scope of Anthem's automatic E/M downcoding policy (Feb 15, 2026, paused in California through Sept 1, 2026): whether it applies to Anthem BCBS Kentucky and its current status.
- Aetna and Humana auto-downcoding program details (official policy text, targeted codes, remit codes, appeal/removal thresholds): only search snippets and an unreachable AOA page (HTTP 503) were available.
- CAQH CORE-required CARC/RARC combinations for CARC 150 and 186 (which RARCs are mandated) — the CORE code-combination spreadsheet was not opened.
- Whether ODA's 10,724 CARC 59 lines reflect correct MPPR percentages (requires MPFS multiple-procedure indicators and the payer's MPPR policy) and how commercial payers (BCBS/Anthem, Humana MA, UHC) represent MPPR (with or without SVC06).
- Contracted fee schedules by payer/product (BCBS/Anthem KY commercial and FEP, Humana MA, UHC commercial/MA/UMR, Cigna, Tricare) are required to dollarize silent downcodes; absent contracts, empirical modal AMT B6 per payer/code/year must serve as the baseline (data gap already flagged by the prior analysis: 'Executed contracts and effective fee schedules by product/network').
- How many of the 33,615 .RMT files are TriZetto redeliveries of the same remittance (same REF*2U + TRN02 + BPR02) versus distinct 835s; the Aug 31–Sep 5, 2026 bulk delivery of 2024 payments suggests redelivery is common.
- Whether CGS (Medicare KY/IN) ever downcodes E/M on prepayment or medical review in ODA's corpus and which RARCs it uses (M25/M26/N22 expected) — no CGS lines with CARC 150/186 were seen in the single Medicare file examined.

## Payer E/M downcoding and claim-editing programs, 2023 to 2026

Research lens: payer E/M downcoding and claim-editing programs 2023-2026, for ODA's payer mix. Key results: (1) Cigna's "Evaluation and Management Coding and Accuracy" policy R49 (commercial, effective 10/01/2025) lowers 99204-99205/99214-99215/99244-99245 by exactly one level from claim data alone, is reinstated on records, has a published bypass (5+ adjusted claims, email, 80% threshold), was paused for fully insured California products (Oct/Nov 2025), was ordered stopped in Maryland (Consent Order MIA-2026-03-009, 3/13/2026, $80,000) and shows no revision/retirement in Cigna's Jan-Oct 2026 policy bulletins. (2) Aetna's vendor-run E&M Claim and Code Review (levels 4-5, "edits are not clinical", never below level 3, one-year inclusion, early removal after ~75% overturn, 180-day reconsideration). (3) Anthem/Elevance runs a diagnosis-weighted outlier program (since 1/1/2021; restarted May 2023 for Medicare Advantage and Medicaid in Kentucky, Indiana, Ohio, Wisconsin; floor 99203/99213; remit message "E/M procedure code reduced based on diagnostic information"; CO-186 reported on remits), added prepayment coding validation of modifiers 24/25/58/59/78/79/X{EPSU} on 4/1/2025, and its newest automatic E/M policy (CA notice 11/13/2025, eff. 2/15/2026) is paused through at least 9/1/2026. (4) UHC's E/M policy 2026R5007A allows records requests for peer-deviating billers and adjustment/denial of the E/M level, but no new 2025-26 UHC automatic downcoding program was found; UHC commercial bundles G2211 into the E/M (Rebundling policy Q13), explaining ODA's UHC/UMR G2211 $0 lines. (5) Humana MA policy CP2024003 (04/2024) adjusts 99204/99205/99214/99215 (and others) "based on the severity of the patient's condition" from claim diagnosis coding; Humana disputes must be filed within 18 months of payment. (6) Blue plans: BCBSMA's program publishes the 835 signature REF*9A*DOWNCODED + RARC N22 with the original line denied and a new lower-level line added; BCBSIL/BCBSTX began commercial E/M editing 7/1/2026. (7) AMA (Policy D-320.972, BOT Report 11-I-26, model "Transparency in Downcoding Act"), Indiana (HB 1271, signed March 2026), Illinois (SB 3114, eff. 2028), Virginia (SB 164/HB 484, April 2026), Maryland (Bulletin 26-9 to all insurers), and MGMA (2026 Burden Report; draft federal MA bill) supply appeal language and legal levers; Kentucky has no downcoding statute found. (8) CMS finalized (Nov 2026) an 80% payment for the second service when an O/O E/M with modifier 25 is billed with a 0-day global procedure in 2027 (AADA estimates ~6% Medicare cut for dermatology), which MA/commercial payers are expected to mirror. Detection rules, deadlines, and open questions are itemized in findings.

### [DISPUTED] Cigna's reimbursement policy R49, titled "Evaluation and Management Coding and Accuracy" (commercial; effective 10/01/2025; last updated 07/01/2025; annual review date 10/01/2026), authorizes Cigna to lower CPT 99204-99205, 99214-99215 and 99244-99245 by exactly one level "when the encounter criteria on the claim does not support the higher-level E/M CPT code reported," and commits to pay the originally billed level when records substantiate MDM or time.

**Corrected (source check):** Cigna Reimbursement Policy R49, "Evaluation and Management Coding and Accuracy" (Commercial; Effective Date 10/01/2025; Related policy R30 Evaluation and Management Services), states: "Cigna may adjust the E/M CPT code 99204-99205, 99214-99215, 99244-99245 to a single level lower when the encounter criteria on the claim does not support the higher-level E/M CPT code reported. For example, a claim may be adjusted as follows: 99215 to 99214, or 99214 to 99213." and "When a code level has been adjusted and, subsequently, medical records are submitted that substantiate the complexity and Medical Decision Making (MDM) or time associated with the reported E/M CPT code level, the code will be reimbursed at the level initially submitted." Those quotations are from the 07/01/2025 notification version of R49 (Last Updated 07/01/2025; Annual Review 10/01/2026; Policy History "07/01/2025 Notification of new policy"), a public copy of which is hosted by the Florida Hospital Association; the same notification text appears in Cigna's July 2025 monthly policy-update bulletin, which lists R49 as New. Cigna's October 2025 bulletin lists R49 (and R30) as "Updated" without describing the change, so the current version carries a later Last Updated date and its exact text is available only behind the CignaforHCP.com login; cite the policy by number and title and quote it as the 07/01/2025 notification language unless the current version is pulled from the portal. Cigna's bulletins for November 2025 through October 2026 list no further revision or retirement of R49 (the November 2026 bulletin was not yet posted as of 2026-11-06). Per Cigna's Provider Newsroom (Nov 18, 2025): adjustments apply only to claim lines of providers whose records show "a consistent pattern of coding at a higher E/M level for routine services compared to their peers"; services "may be adjusted by one level"; adjusted claims are paid promptly at the lower level and the explanation of payment shows the adjustment; Evernorth Behavioral Health claims are excluded; almost 99 percent of in-network providers (more than 97 percent of those regularly billing level 4 and 5) were expected to be unaffected; reconsideration is requested with the full encounter record via CignaforHCP.com > Claims > Search Claims > Claim number > Start a reconsideration, or secure fax 833.392.2092, with a right to an administrative appeal if the determination is upheld; providers with five or more adjusted claims may request a bypass by emailing EMCodingAccuracy@CignaHealthcare.com, granted if at least 80 percent of a reviewed subset of adjusted claims were billed appropriately; "Cigna Healthcare will conduct periodic claim reviews to verify compliance" and "continued inclusion in the policy will be determined by their coding patterns" (ISMA's July 10, 2025 summary phrases this as providers "may be eligible to be removed from the program"). No cited source states that reconsiderations are reviewed by certified coders. Jurisdictional limits: the policy was paused in California after the California Medical Association's inquiry to state regulators (AAFP editor's note, undated), and in Maryland the Insurance Administration's Consent Order MIA-2026-03-009 (signed by Cigna Feb 25, 2026; MIA letter March 13, 2026) imposed an $80,000 penalty and required Cigna, within 30 days, to "cease the automatic reduction of the code level of billed evaluation and management codes" and align R49 with Maryland law; MIA Bulletin 26-9 (April 7, 2026) extends that prohibition to all Maryland third-party payors. The July 2026 Oncology Practice Management statement that R49 ran "for roughly 5 months" refers to this Maryland order, is consistent with the consent order and with Part B News (Jan 26, 2026, policy in effect), and does not indicate a nationwide retirement; for Kentucky claims the policy remains in force per Cigna's bulletins.

_Why:_ Core language confirmed verbatim: the FHA-hosted PDF (fetched, 5 pages) is Cigna Reimbursement Policy R49, Commercial, Effective 10/01/2025, Last Updated 07/01/2025, Annual Review 10/01/2026, titled "Evaluation and Management Coding and Accuracy", related policy R30, with both quoted sentences exactly as claimed and Policy History "07/01/2025 Notification of new policy". Cigna's July 2025 bulletin (static.cigna.com) lists R49 as New with the same notification text; the Maryland consent order independently refers to the "Evaluation and Management Coding and Accuracy (R49) Reimbursement Policy" and corroborates Cigna's practice of adjusting at the claim-line level, paying the lower code and requiring an appeal. Why refuted: (1) materially outdated version metadata. I downloaded every Cigna monthly bulletin from August 2025 through October 2026 (November/December 2026 return 404) and grepped by number and title: the October 2025 bulletin lists "Evaluation and Management Coding Accuracy - (R49)" and R30 as "Updated" (no comment text). So "last updated 07/01/2025" describes only the pre-effective notification copy; the current version has a later update date and its text is behind the C

**Corrected (expert review):** Cigna reimbursement policy R49, "Evaluation and Management Coding and Accuracy" (Commercial; professional services on a CMS-1500 or electronic equivalent; related policy R30), was announced in Cigna's July 2025 bulletin and applies to dates of service on or after 10/01/2025. Its operative text, unchanged across versions, reads: "Cigna may adjust the E/M CPT code 99204-99205, 99214-99215, 99244-99245 to a single level lower when the encounter criteria on the claim does not support the higher-level E/M CPT code reported. For example, a claim may be adjusted as follows: 99215 to 99214, or 99214 to 99213," and "When a code level has been adjusted and, subsequently, medical records are submitted that substantiate the complexity and Medical Decision Making (MDM) or time associated with the reported E/M CPT code level, the code will be reimbursed at the level initially submitted." HOWEVER, the version the claim cites (FHA-hosted copy, Last Updated 07/01/2025) is superseded. Cigna's currently hosted version (static.cigna.com, fetched 2026-11-06; PDF generated 08/07/2026) shows header dates Effective 07/01/2025 / Last Updated 09/30/2025 / Annual Review 10/01/2026, a history table ("07/01/2025 Notification of new policy; 09/23/2025 Added clarifying language around exclusions, complex conditions; 10/01/2025 Effective date for policy"), and added exclusions: "Claims for services involving children under the age of one-year, certain transplants, and certain complex conditions will not be subject to an E/M level adjustment. Claims submitted by behavioral health providers are excluded from the E/M level adjustment. However, claims for behavioral services are not otherwise excluded." Cigna's FAQ names the exclusions as claims for cancer, transplants, and treatment of children under age one; the announcement adds that Evernorth Behavioral Health claims are not affected. Cigna's October 2025 bulletin lists R49 as "Updated"; its bulletins for November 2025 through October 2026 contain no further R49 revision or retirement (the November 2026 bulletin was not yet posted on 2026-11-06), and the policy PDF, announcement and FAQ remain live, so R49 has not been retired nationally. Program mechanics per Cigna (announcement posted 11/18/2025 and FAQ): adjustments apply "by one level only for providers who our records indicate as having a consistent pattern of coding at a higher E/M level for routine services compared to their peers" ("approximately three percent of providers"; "almost 99 percent of all in-network providers will not be affected... including more than 97 percent of providers who regularly bill level 4 and 5 E/M codes"); selection used 12 months of claims, focusing on diagnoses such as earache or sore throat, associated diagnoses and other services in the same encounter; each claim line is reviewed individually, claims are not pended, and payment is issued promptly at the lower level; the EOP "will clearly indicate if a service was adjusted," and adjusted claims are viewable on CignaforHCP.com (Claims > Search Claims > Adjusted claims) from 11/21/2025; the FAQ lists no CARC/RARC codes, so the 835 signature must be confirmed empirically. Reconsideration: CignaforHCP.com > Claims > Search Claims > claim number > "Start a reconsideration" with the full encounter record, or secure fax 833.392.2092; certified coders review; if upheld, administrative appeal rights apply. Bypass: providers with five or more adjusted claims email EMCodingAccuracy@CignaHealthcare.com; Cigna requests documentation for a subset of claims and grants the bypass if at least 80 percent were billed appropriately; bypassed providers stay exempt unless periodic review shows a substantive change; "Cigna Healthcare will conduct periodic claim reviews to verify compliance." R49 is NOT uniformly in force: (1) California: after CMA complaints (8/20/2025) and a DMHC inquiry, Cigna paused implementation for fully insured commercial HMO products effective 10/01/2025 and for CDI-regulated fully insured PPO products from 11/09/2025 (initially 30 days) pending regulator review, while self-insured lines remained in effect (CMA 10/01/2025 and 11/10/2025; CMA still described the policy as paused on 2/20/2026 when it sponsored AB 2431 to prohibit automatic downcoding; final bill status not verified). (2) Maryland: Consent Order MIA-2026-03-009 (Cigna signed 2/25/2026; executed 3/13/2026) imposed an $80,000 administrative penalty, found that adjusting billed codes instead of paying, denying with reasons, or requesting specific additional information violated Insurance Article 15-1005(c) and delayed amounts owed (4-113(b)(5)), and ordered Cigna within 30 days to "cease the automatic reduction of the code level of billed evaluation and management codes" and document that R49 was aligned with Maryland law; MIA Bulletin 26-9 (4/07/2026, "Prompt Payment of Claims - Downcoding Practices Prohibited") extended the prohibition to all Maryland health insurers, nonprofit health service plans, HMOs and TPAs. Neither document requires reprocessing of already-adjusted claims (the Oncology Practice Management statement that the order requires reprocessing is not supported by the order's text). The Oncology Practice Management (July 2026) phrase that R49 "ran for roughly 5 months" refers to 10/01/2025 through the 3/13/2026 Maryland order and does not conflict with Part B News (1/22/2026, policy in effect) or establish a nationwide end. Neither the California pause nor the Maryland order binds Cigna in Kentucky, but the Maryland reasoning (a prompt-pay statute requires pay/deny/request-records, not unilateral code substitution) is a transferable appeal argument whose Kentucky analog has not been verified here.

_Why:_ Method: web search budget was exhausted, so every finding comes from direct fetches of primary documents (text-extracted with pdftotext where PDF). Confirmed: the FHA-hosted R49 PDF contains exactly the quoted policy sentences, title "Evaluation and Management Coding and Accuracy," Commercial, R49, Effective 10/01/2025, Last Updated 07/01/2025, Annual Review 10/01/2026, related policy R30, CMS-1500 scope; the July 2025 Cigna bulletin lists R49 as New; Cigna's newsroom announcement (posted 11/18/2025) and FAQ confirm the peer-pattern targeting, ~3% affected / ~99% and >97% unaffected figures, prompt payment at the lower level, EOP indication, reconsideration path and fax number, certified-coder review, administrative appeal, bypass email with the 5-claim/80% rule, periodic reviews, and the Evernorth Behavioral Health carve-out; ISMA's article corroborates; Part B News (1/22/2026) visible text says the policy took effect October 1; I downloaded and grepped every Cigna monthly bulletin from August 2025 through October 2026 and found no R49 revision or retirement after October 2025 (November 2026 returns 404 as of today). Why refuted (materially outdated/overstated for an appeal letter

Policy text (copy of the Cigna PDF hosted by FHA): "Cigna may adjust the E/M CPT code 99204- 99205, 99214-99215, 99244-99245 to a single level lower when the encounter criteria on the claim does not support the higher-level E/M CPT code reported. For example, a claim may be adjusted as follows: 99215 to 99214, or 99214 to 99213." and "When a code level has been adjusted and, subsequently, medical records are submitted that substantiate the complexity and Medical Decision Making (MDM) or time associated with the reported E/M CPT code level, the code will be reimbursed at the level initially submitted." Related policy: R30 Evaluation and Management Services. Cigna Provider Newsroom announcement (read): applies only to providers with "a consistent pattern of coding at a higher E/M level for routine services compared to their peers"; screening looks at claim data, associated diagnoses and other services in the encounter; adjusted claims are paid promptly at the lower level and the Explanation of Payment shows the adjustment; does not affect Evernorth Behavioral Health claims; "Cigna Healthcare will conduct periodic claim reviews to verify compliance" and providers "may be eligible to be removed from the program." Dispute path: CignaforHCP.com > Claims > Search Claims > claim number > "Start a reconsideration", submitting the full encounter record, or secure fax 833.392.2092; reconsiderations are evaluated by certified coders; if upheld, an administrative appeal is available. Bypass: providers with five or more adjusted claims who believe they bill per AMA guidelines email EMCodingAccuracy@CignaHealthcare.com; Cigna requests documentation for a subset of claim history and grants the bypass if at least 80% of the adjusted E/M claims were billed appropriately. Cigna said almost 99% of in-network clinicians (more than 97% of those billing level 4/5) are unaffected initially; other reports quote ~1% or ~3%. Cigna's own monthly policy-update bulletins for Jan-Oct 2026 (downloaded from static.cigna.com) list no revision or retirement of R49 (the July 2025 bulletin is where R49 was announced). A July 2026 Oncology Practice Management article asserts R49 "ran for about 5 months," which conflicts with Part B News (1/22/2026: policy in effect) and is unconfirmed.

**What it means for ODA:** Detection rule for Cigna remits: flag any office/consult E/M line where the paid code (SVC01) is exactly one level below the submitted code (SVC06) or where the allowed amount matches the next-lower level's fee for 99204/99205/99214/99215 with a paid-as-billed status; group by rendering NPI and count adjusted claims, because the 5-claim threshold unlocks the written bypass request, and track the 80% overturn target. Appeal packet must include the full encounter note and cite the policy's own reinstatement sentence. Dashboard should also surface per-provider 'in program' status and periodic-review timing.

Sources: [fha.org](https://www.fha.org/common/Uploaded%20files/FHA/Education-Events/Education/R49_Evaluation_and_Management_Coding_Accuracy.pdf) · [providernewsroom.com](https://providernewsroom.com/cigna-healthcare/new-reimbursement-policy-for-professional-evaluation-and-management-services-claims-effective-october-1-2025/) · [aafp.org](https://www.aafp.org/pubs/fpm/blogs/gettingpaid/entry/cigna-downcoding-em.html) · [ismanet.org](https://www.ismanet.org/ISMA/Resources/e-Reports/7-10-25/Cigna.aspx) · [static.cigna.com](https://static.cigna.com/assets/chcp/pdf/coveragePolicies/policy_updates/july_2025_policy_updates.pdf) · [static.cigna.com](https://static.cigna.com/assets/chcp/pdf/coveragePolicies/policy_updates/march_2026_policy_updates.pdf) · [pbn.decisionhealth.com](https://pbn.decisionhealth.com/Articles/Detail.aspx?id=550232) · [oncpracticemanagement.com](https://www.oncpracticemanagement.com/issues/2026/july-2026-vol-16-no-4/downcoding-in-the-oncology-practice-how-to-identify-it-and-how-to-fix-it) · [static.cigna.com](https://static.cigna.com/assets/chcp/pdf/coveragePolicies/policy_updates/october_2025_policy_updates.pdf) · [static.cigna.com](https://static.cigna.com/assets/chcp/pdf/coveragePolicies/policy_updates/october_2026_policy_updates.pdf)

### [VERIFIED] Aetna's "Evaluation and Management (E&M) Program" within its Claim and Code Review Program uses a contracted vendor's certified coders to review level 4 and 5 office/outpatient, consultation and ophthalmological E&M codes for select providers using only the claim and member/provider claim history ("The edits are not clinical"), pays the lower level without separate notice, and allows reconsideration with records; inclusion lasts about a year with early removal after a high overturn rate.

**Checker's wording:** Aetna's "Evaluation and Management (E&M) Program," part of its Claim and Code Review Program (CCRP), uses a contracted vendor (not named by Aetna; AAO-HNS's letter calls it "Availity," which appears to conflate Aetna's provider portal with the vendor) whose certified coders review level 4 and 5 new- and established-patient E&M codes for office, outpatient, consultation and ophthalmological services, for "select providers," using "the claim billed and the member and provider claim history"; Aetna's program sheet states "The edits are not clinical. They are based on external coding guidelines." Listed places of service are office, off-campus and on-campus outpatient hospital, urgent care facility, inpatient hospital and hospital emergency room (not only office/outpatient). The only publicly reachable Aetna program document is the New York version (form 2208020-01-01 (5/23), ©2023, PDF created Nov 2023), which says methodology "depends on the market"; the national "EM Program / Expanded Claim Edits, May 2025" version sits behind Availity/APMA member login. Downcoded claims are paid at the lower level with no separate notification (ISMA; CSRO: "Physicians are not being notified"), but the remittance itself flags the change: per APMA (6/2/2025) the ERA carries RARC N22 ("procedure code was added/changed because it more accurately describes the services rendered") and the EOB carries message R11 ("...Our payment reflects the more appropriate E&M code... you have the right to dispute"); Aetna told APMA "Claims will never be adjusted to less than a Level 3 reimbursement." Disputes: a written request with medical records to the address on the EOB or via the Availity "Contact us" function; "Some contracts have special dispute provisions. If yours does not, follow the standard timeframes for a claim reconsideration or appeal." Aetna's live disputes page: reconsideration within 180 calendar days of the initial claim decision (Availity or mail; usually an EOB/letter within 30 business days), then appeal within 60 calendar days of the reconsideration decision (decision within 60 calendar days; if the issue did not qualify for reconsideration, 180 days for Medicare and commercial or 65 days for Medicare non-contracted providers). Scope/timing per the ISMA e-Report (URL issue folder 6-12-25; the article itself is undated and says "in late March"): the program expanded from 12 pilot states to all Aetna commercial states except Louisiana, with Medicare Advantage expansion "planned for later this year"; inclusion "generally lasts for one year"; early removal by successfully appealing 75% of downcoded claims (the AAO-HNS 9/3/2025 letter repeats the 75% figure). Aetna told APMA (6/2/2025): the program is commercial-only ("no Medicare Advantage claims are adjusted under this program"), nationwide, all provider types; the vendor "conducts a yearly analysis of all applicable providers to identify over users" and re-evaluates annually; early removal follows overturning "a certain threshold of adjusted claims, over a period of approximately 5–6 months," requires the provider to proactively contact its plan representatives, and Aetna "will not reassess that provider under this program for three years"; fewer than 1% of podiatrists were entered and more than 90% of their level 4/5 claims were paid as billed. Conflicting signal: CSRO's 7/16/2025 letter demanding an immediate halt to blanket downcoding of 99214/99215 and to records demands for every level 4/5 claim was addressed to "Aetna Medicare Advantage, Attention: Medicare Provider Disputes, P.O. Box 14067, Lexington, KY 40512" (Aetna's Medicare non-contracted provider appeals mailbox); this is CSRO's characterization, not an Aetna confirmation, and no Aetna public notice of a Medicare Advantage rollout of the E&M program was found in OfficeLink Updates through November 2026, so MA status is unresolved. Separately, Aetna's quarterly CCRP notices (commercial, Medicare and Student Health) added edits effective 9/1/2025 (DWT 6/26/2025: high-dollar, implant, anesthesia and bundled-services claims get additional review) and continue quarterly through at least 1/1/2027, with Aetna stating from Aug 2026 that it "may request medical records" for those claim types. The earlier ED E/M review (Aetna OfficeLink Updates 4/8/2024: from 7/8/2024 Aetna reviews physician and facility ER claims for level 4/5 E&M and "may adjust your payment," for commercial, Medicare and Student Health members) drew a formal CMA objection (per DWT); the asserted July 2024 AMA letter and AMA statement that Aetna's policy did not change could not be verified from any accessible source and should not be cited without a primary document.

Aetna program document (NY version, dated 5/23): "We contract with a vendor to review coding for E&M services. For select providers, our vendor will evaluate the appropriateness of levels 4 and 5 E&M codes... The edits are not clinical. They are based on external coding guidelines. Our vendor uses certified coders who review the claim billed and the member and provider claim history to make the edit decision." Places of service include office and outpatient hospital. Disagreement: written request with medical records to the address on the EOB or via the "Contact us" function on Availity; "Some contracts have special dispute provisions. If yours does not, follow the standard timeframes for a claim reconsideration or appeal." Aetna's disputes page: reconsideration within 180 calendar days of the initial claim decision (Availity or mail; decision within 30 business days); appeal within 60 calendar days of the reconsideration decision (decision within 60 calendar days). Scope/timing: ISMA (6/12/2025) reports the program expanded in late March 2025 from 12 pilot states to all Aetna commercial states except Louisiana, with Medicare Advantage expansion "planned for later this year"; inclusion "generally lasts for one year"; early removal by successfully appealing 75% of downcoded claims (AAO-HNS 9/3/2025 letter states the same 75% rule). Aetna told APMA (6/2/2025): a vendor "conducts a yearly analysis of all applicable providers"; "Claims will never be adjusted to less than a Level 3 reimbursement"; "no Medicare Advantage claims are adjusted under this program"; early removal after overturning "a certain threshold of adjusted claims, over a period of approximately 5-6 months," requires the provider to contact plan representatives, and Aetna will "not reassess that provider under this program for three years." Conflicting signal: the CSRO letter of 7/16/2025 was addressed to "Aetna Medicare Advantage, Attention: Medicare Provider Disputes" demanding a halt to downcoding of 99214/99215 and to records demands for every level 4/5 claim. Separately, Aetna announced CCRP expansion effective 9/1/2025 adding coding edits for high-dollar, implant, anesthesia and bundled services across commercial, Medicare and Student Health claims (DWT). AMA wrote Aetna in July 2024 about the ED E/M downcoding update; AMA reports Aetna's policy did not change.

**What it means for ODA:** Aetna is a minor payer for ODA, but its program is the template other payers copy: build the dashboard's per-provider 'program exposure' metric (share of level 4/5 claims paid below billed) and an overturn-rate tracker, because exemption at Aetna is earned by appealing and winning ~75% within a 5-6 month window and must be requested from the plan representative. Deadline engine: Aetna reconsideration 180 days from the EOB date, appeal 60 days after the reconsideration decision.

Sources: [aetna.com](https://www.aetna.com/content/dam/aetna/pdfs/aetnacom/healthcare-professionals/documents-forms/ny-em-code-claim-review.pdf) · [aetna.com](https://www.aetna.com/health-care-professionals/disputes-appeals/disputes-appeals-overview.html) · [ismanet.org](https://www.ismanet.org/ISMA/Resources/e-Reports/6-12-25/Aetna.aspx) · [apma.org](https://www.apma.org/about-apma/news/news-releases/2025/apma-takes-action-engaging-aetna-on-downcoding-policy/) · [entnet.org](https://www.entnet.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/AAO-HNS_Aetna_Comment_Letter_Downcoding_9.2025.pdf) · [csro.info](https://csro.info/UserFiles/file/Memos/UrgentActionRequired-Aetna-CeaseUnjustDowncodingandAdministrativeBurdenforLevel4and5EandMCodes.pdf) · [dwt.com](https://www.dwt.com/insights/2025/06/aetna-expanded-claim-edits-september-2025) · [aetna.com](https://www.aetna.com/content/dam/aetna/pdfs/olu/officelink-updates-april-2024-olu-edition-4.8.24.pdf) · [aetna.com](https://www.aetna.com/health-care-professionals/newsletters-news/office-link-updates-june-2025.html) · [aetna.com](https://www.aetna.com/content/dam/aetna/pdfs/olu/officelink-updates-august-2026-olu.pdf)

### [VERIFIED] Anthem/Elevance runs an analytics-based E/M "correct coding" program that selects physicians "coding at a higher E/M level as compared to their peers with similar risk-adjusted members," reduces payment (not the billed code) using diagnosis-based risk weights with a floor of 99203/99213, and restarted it in May 2023 for Medicare Advantage and Medicaid in Kentucky, Indiana, Ohio and Wisconsin; remittances carry the message "Evaluation and Management procedure code reduced based on diagnostic information. Disputes must include documentation," and practices report CARC CO-186 on these lines.

**Checker's wording:** Anthem/Elevance operates an analytics-based E/M "correct coding" program that selects claims from physicians who code at higher E/M levels than their peers with similar risk-adjusted patients (outliers identified from one year of data compared with regional peers). Selected claims are reviewed before payment; Anthem may deny and request resubmission, pend for documentation, or adjust payment to the lower level it deems supported. When it adjusts, "the coding on the physician's claim will stay the same — only the payment will be adjusted"; the maximum level is tied to MDM complexity, physicians who coded by time can appeal with time documentation, and Anthem monitors dispute and overturn rates. Roster mechanics: per the Aug 2021 description Anthem "will add and drop physicians every six months, when it conducts eligibility reviews" and physicians whose coding reverts to the norm are removed; per the May 2023 description outliers are removed monthly once they meet undisclosed dispute-review criteria based on all impacted claims.
