Targeted codes: 99204, 99205, 99214, 99215. The review assigns a risk-adjusted weight to each diagnosis code; the diagnosis with the highest supported E/M sets the level, never below 99203 or 99213. The program was publicized in 2021 (AAPC posters in Indiana, Georgia and Kentucky reported it from late 2020/2021), was paused in some markets after AAFP and medical-society pushback, and on May 12, 2023 AAFP FPM reported that Elevance had "recently announced" through its provider portal that it was restarting the program in its Medicare Advantage and Medicaid markets in Wisconsin, Indiana, Kentucky and Ohio (the exact restart date is not stated; commercial lines were not described as paused). The explanation-of-benefits/remittance-advice message is "Evaluation and Management procedure code reduced based on diagnostic information. Disputes must include documentation." — free text on the EOB, not an 835 segment — and Anthem says "the remittance code varies by line of business," so a detection rule should not rely on one code. Practices report the adjustment as CARC CO-186 ("Level of care change adjustment," X12), e.g., a billed 99214 processed and paid as 99213 (Indiana, Georgia, Kentucky posters, 2021; a Virginia poster reported a renewed January 2026 uptick of Anthem CO-186 downcodes); disputes go through Availity or by fax (posters used the Provider Adjustment Form with chart notes and had claims reprocessed at the billed level). The program was still active in 2025: NBC News (Oct 9, 2025) reported that Anthem has acknowledged downcoding higher-level claims for certain office visits, that an Anthem downcoding notice sent in May (year not stated; context implies 2025) says doctors "whose coding patterns improve and are no longer identified as an outlier are eligible to be removed from the program," and that a Missouri dermatologist lost nearly $14,000 to Anthem downcoding over almost two years and recouped over a third through appeals. NOT VERIFIED (sources unreachable/unsearched): the exact quotation "coding at a higher E/M level as compared to their peers with similar risk-adjusted members" and the "used since January 1, 2021" date attributed to Anthem's Georgia provider-news page (page returns an empty bot-protected response), and the Medscape/Becker's 2021 "analytical tools to review evaluation and management codes" statement.

AAFP FPM (8/5/2021): Anthem uses analytics to select physicians who bill higher E/M levels than peers with similar risk-adjusted patients, reviews selected claims prepayment, and may deny and request resubmission, pend for documentation, or adjust payment to the lower supported level; "the coding on the physician's claim will stay the same, only the payment will be adjusted"; maximum level is based on MDM complexity; physicians coding by time can appeal with time documentation; Anthem monitors dispute and overturn rates and "will add and drop physicians every six months" at eligibility reviews. Anthem's Georgia provider news (search snippet of the Anthem page) says the tool has been used since January 1, 2021 and providers whose coding patterns improve are eligible for removal. AAFP FPM (5/12/2023): Elevance announced through its provider portal it was "restarting their evaluation and management (E/M) downcoding program in Medicare Advantage and Medicaid markets where it had been paused (Wisconsin, Indiana, Kentucky, and Ohio)"; targeted codes 99204, 99205, 99214, 99215; each diagnosis carries a risk-adjusted weight and the highest supported E/M sets the level, "never below 99203 or 99213"; EOB message quoted above; appeals via Availity or fax; outliers are removed monthly once they meet undisclosed dispute-review criteria based on all impacted claims. AAPC forum (read): Indiana/Georgia/Kentucky posters in 2021 saw 99214 paid as 99213 with CO-186 ("Level of care change adjustment"), reprocessed after submitting the Provider Adjustment Form with chart notes; a Virginia poster reported a new uptick in Anthem CO-186 downcodes in January 2026. Anthem told Medscape/Becker's (2021) it uses "analytical tools to review evaluation and management codes" during adjudication and that physicians may dispute with a statement and documentation. NBC News (10/9/2025): Anthem acknowledged downcoding certain office visits; its May (2025) notice said providers whose coding patterns improve may be removed; one physician lost nearly $14,000 to Anthem downcoding over about two years and recovered over a third on appeal.

**What it means for ODA:** For Anthem BCBS Kentucky and Anthem Medicare Advantage (ODA's 'BCBS Medicare Advantage'), detection must not rely on SVC06 alone: Anthem leaves the billed code and cuts payment, so compare the line's allowed amount (AMT*B6) against the provider's contracted or historically observed allowed for the billed level and flag CAS CO-186 and any payer message text containing "reduced based on diagnostic information". Appeals must attach documentation (Availity dispute or fax, Provider Adjustment Form), and the program's six-month/monthly re-evaluation cadence explains why downcoding stops after successful appeals and resumes months later; the dashboard should show per-provider Anthem downcode rate by month to time exemption requests and renewed appeal campaigns. Also use the most specific ICD-10 codes on Anthem claims, since the algorithm keys on diagnosis weight.

Sources: [aafp.org](https://www.aafp.org/pubs/fpm/blogs/gettingpaid/entry/anthem_emcoding_reviews.html) · [aafp.org](https://aafp.org/pubs/fpm/blogs/gettingpaid/entry/elevance-downcoding-resumes.html) · [aapc.com](https://www.aapc.com/discuss/threads/anthem-downcoding.181067/) · [nbcnews.com](https://www.nbcnews.com/news/amp/rcna230714) · [providernews.anthem.com](https://providernews.anthem.com/georgia/articles/evaluation-and-management-services-correct-coding-professional-1) · [x12.org](https://x12.org/codes/claim-adjustment-reason-codes) · [media-cldnry.s-nbcnews.com](https://media-cldnry.s-nbcnews.com/image/upload/t_fit-2000w,f_jpg,q_auto:best/rockcms/2025-09/250929-Anthem-Downcoding-Notice-ew-1243p-826b19.jpg)

### [BACKGROUND] Anthem's newest automatic E/M claim downcoding policy (California provider notice dated November 13, 2025, effective February 15, 2026) subjects physicians "coding at a higher E/M level compared to their peers with similar risk-adjusted members" to additional review, potential downcoding or delayed payment based solely on claim information; after CMA complaints it was paused pending DMHC review and the pause has been extended through at least September 1, 2026.

CMA (2/23/2026): Anthem's November 13 provider notice described review, "potential downcoding or delayed payment" for physicians coding higher than risk-adjusted peers; CMA says the review is based solely on claim information, the notice gave few details on selection or how the 'appropriate' level is set, and the policy "may violate California prompt payment and disclosure laws." Pause sequence reported by CMA: to March 1, then April 1, May 1, June 1, and (6/18/2026) "through at least Sept. 1, 2026" while regulators review. Becker's (2/27/2026) confirmed the extension to April. No outcome of the DMHC review and no equivalent Kentucky/Indiana notice was found; the AASM (11/14/2025) lists Anthem (Elevance Health) among plans with active or proposed downcoding programs that "rely on claims analytics rather than clinical review."

**What it means for ODA:** Expect Elevance to roll the 2026 version of its automatic E/M policy into non-California markets, including Kentucky and Indiana, with little notice; the dashboard should alert on any step change in the Anthem downcode rate (paid level below billed, CO-186, allowed-amount drops) and the synthesis step should check Anthem Kentucky provider news for a parallel notice.

Sources: [cmadocs.org](https://www.cmadocs.org/newsroom/news/view/anthem-agrees-to-pause-automatic-e-m-downcoding-policy-until-march-1) · [cmadocs.org](https://www.cmadocs.org/newsroom/news/view/ArticleId/51218/Anthem-extends-pause-on-E-M-downcoding-policy-through-Sept-1) · [aasm.org](https://aasm.org/growing-trend-of-downcoding-among-commercial-payers/)

### [CONTESTED] Since April 1, 2025 Anthem applies a prepayment coding validation review to outpatient professional claims carrying modifiers 24, 25, 58, 59, 78, 79, anatomical modifiers (E1-E4, F1-F9, TA, LT, RT, LC, LD, RC, LM, RI) and X{E,P,S,U}, in which clinical analysts (RNs and certified coders) judge whether the modifier properly overrides the edit (including NCCI) it is meant to bypass; this sits on top of Anthem's history of modifier 25 cuts (a 50%-then-25% reduction announced for 2018 and rescinded in February 2018) and a February 2019 Kentucky newsletter policy to deny an E/M with modifier 25 on the day of a related procedure when a recent (about 60 days) service for the same or similar diagnosis is on record.

**Corrected (expert review):** Anthem Blue Cross and Blue Shield Kentucky (Commercial line of business) announced in its March 2025 Provider Newsletter (published 2025-03-01; the identical article ran in Indiana, which ISMA summarized on 4/3/2025) that "Beginning April 1, 2025, we will update our prepayment coding validation review process for outpatient claims to include claims submitted with modifiers 24, 25, 58, 59, 78, 79, E1–E4, F1–F9, TA, LT, RT, LC, LD, RC, LM, RI, XE, XP, XS, and XU"; the review "aligns with published reimbursement policies and National Correct Coding Initiative (NCCI) correct coding guidelines," evaluates "the use of modifiers in conjunction with the edits they bypass (such as the NCCI)," and "Registered nurses and coders who are clinical analysts will review claims pending validation, along with any related services, to determine whether it is appropriate for the modifier to bypass the edit"; disputes go through the provider-manual claims payment dispute process. Anthem wrote "outpatient claims" (not "outpatient professional claims") and "coders" (not "certified coders"). April 2025 was an expansion of an existing program, not its start: Anthem Kentucky's June 2019 notice "Reimbursement Policy Update: Clinical Validation – Professional" implemented, for dates of service on or after September 1, 2019, a "pre-payment clinical validation review process" of claims with modifiers -25, -59, -57, LT/RT and other anatomical modifiers, with "clinical analysts who are registered nurses and coders" reviewing pended claims and related services; so Anthem modifier-25 prepayment reviews/denials can appear throughout ODA's 2024–2026 835 data. History: Anthem's modifier 25 cut (50% planned for 1/1/2018, reduced to 25% and delayed to 3/1/2018 for CA, CO, CT, IN, KY, ME, MO, NV, NH, NY, OH, WI) was withdrawn in late February 2018 by letter from Anthem EVP/Chief Clinical Officer Craig E. Samitt, MD to the AMA (AMA 2/26/2018; KMA 2/23/2018); KMA (1/31/2018) reported Anthem would conduct post-service reviews of professional modifier 25 claims. The February 2019 Anthem Kentucky Provider Newsletter article "Update regarding E/M with modifier 25 – Professional" (Commercial; the same article ran in Anthem's five Central-region states, so it was a regional rule, not a Kentucky-only one) stated that providers often bill a duplicate E/M on the day of a procedure "even when the same provider (or a provider with the same specialty within the same group TIN) recently billed a service or procedure which included an E/M for the same or similar diagnosis," and that "Beginning with claims processed on or after March 1, 2019, Anthem may deny the E/M service with a modifier 25 billed on the day of a related procedure when there is a recent service or procedure for the same or similar diagnosis on record," inviting medical records of the related visits that "demonstrate an unrelated, significant, and separately identifiable E/M service" for reprocessing. Anthem never published a day count; "sixty (60) days" was KMA's stated understanding in its 3/6/2019 letter to Anthem Kentucky president Kennan Wethington. The same edit was announced for Anthem Medicare Advantage for claims processed on or after May 1, 2019 and for Kentucky Medicaid (April 2019). No Anthem Kentucky newsletter from 2019 through November 2026 withdraws or postpones that edit (by contrast, the May 2019 ER level-5 E/M post-pay review was expressly postponed in January 2020); the commercial policy was renamed "Modifiers 25 and 57 – Professional" effective March 1, 2023 (99211 with modifier 25 nonreimbursable), but whether the "recent service" denial language is in the current policy text, and the policy number C-09011, could not be verified because Anthem's policy library was not retrievable. KMA's 90-day argument rests on KRS 304.17A-235(3), which requires at least 90 days' notice of a "material change" (one that decreases provider payment or significantly increases administrative expense) sent in an orange envelope marked "ATTENTION! CONTRACT AMENDMENT ENCLOSED!"; however, subsection (6) requires only 15 days' notice for changes to "an edit program or specific edits," so the notice argument is contestable for claims edits. Anthem E/M programs omitted by the original claim that bear directly on downcoding detection in Kentucky: (1) Commercial "Evaluation and management services correct coding (Professional)": beginning November 1, 2020 Anthem uses "an analytic solution" to select E/M claims from providers "identified as coding at a higher E/M level as compared to their peers with similar risk-adjusted members," pays "at the supported E/M code level and fee schedule rate" based on medical decision-making complexity, "will not impact every level 4 or 5 E/M claim," removes providers whose patterns improve, and allows dispute with records; a Medicare Advantage reminder (March 2022) states the maximum E/M level is based on MDM or time and paid at the supported level; (2) for claims processed on or after September 1, 2022, new-patient E/M claims for a patient seen by the same group/specialty within three years are denied (resubmit as established or dispute with records); (3) the Documentation and Reporting Guidelines for E/M Services – Professional policy was aligned to CMS/AMA 2021–2023 MDM and time rules (updates effective April 1 and June 14, 2023; time must be exact, else MDM is used); (4) Code and Clinical Editing Guidelines – Professional and Facility C-25002 replaced C-09004 for dates of service on or after October 1, 2025 (NCCI PTP/MUE, OCE, clinical criteria); (5) Medicare Advantage: 99211 with modifier 25 nonreimbursable from dates of service April 1, 2026; for claims processed on or after May 1, 2026, E/M billed with CMS "XXX"-global procedures must carry modifier 25, and from July 1, 2026 such claims are reviewed to confirm a significant, separately identifiable E/M (it may be for the same condition but must not include work of the procedure, staff supervision or result interpretation), with possible post-payment review; (6) Commercial "New requirements for E/M services" (October 2026 newsletter, 13 states including Kentucky): for dates of service on or after November 1, 2026 Anthem follows CMS reimbursement guidelines for E/M billed with XXX-global procedures (modifier 25 required for separate reimbursement), and for dates of service on or after January 1, 2027 it will review modifier-25 E/M claims billed with XXX procedures, which "may not be eligible for reimbursement" or may require a corrected claim; (7) for claims processed on or after December 1, 2026, outpatient facility claim lines missing or mismatching a required anatomical modifier are not reimbursed.

_Why:_ Method: the shared web-search budget was exhausted at the start of this task, so verification relied on fetching the cited sources directly, extracting the KMA letter PDF locally, and decoding Anthem's own Provider News pages (the site is a JavaScript app; the article JSON is embedded in the page, so I pulled it with curl and parsed it). I scanned every Anthem Kentucky newsletter issue from January 2019 through November 2026 for article titles about E/M, modifiers, coding validation, claims editing and reimbursement policy, and read the relevant articles. Anthem's commercial reimbursement-policy library is bot-protected; one retry with cookies was blocked by the permission system and I did not pursue it, so the current text of the Modifiers 25 and 57 policy (and the number C-09011) remains unverified. Findings. Confirmed: the April 1, 2025 modifier review exists with exactly the claimed modifier list, RN/coder clinical analysts, the NCCI-bypass test and a dispute route (Anthem Kentucky article 24286, Commercial, March 2025; Indiana twin 24285; ISMA summary; WCHSB blog, which names no state). Confirmed: the 2018 history (50% then 25%, 3/1/2018, Samitt letter withdrawing it in late F

ISMA (4/3/2025) summarizing Anthem's March 2025 Provider Newsletter article "Certain modifiers for outpatient claims will receive prepayment coding review": "Beginning April 1, 2025, Anthem implemented a prepayment coding validation review process for outpatient claims" submitted with the listed modifiers; the review checks compliance with Anthem reimbursement policies and NCCI; "Clinical analysts, including registered nurses and certified coders, evaluate the claim and its associated services." WCHSB (Wisconsin) repeats the same modifier list. AMA (2/26/2018): Anthem's modifier 25 policy (50% cut planned for 1/1/2018, reduced to 25% and delayed to 3/1/2018) was withdrawn by letter from Anthem's Craig Samitt to the AMA; KMA (1/31/2018) noted Anthem would conduct post-service reviews of modifier 25 claims. KMA letter to Anthem Kentucky (3/6/2019) quotes the February 2019 Anthem Kentucky Provider Newsletter: "Beginning with claims processed on or after March 1, 2019, Anthem may deny the E/M service with a modifier 25 billed on the day of a related procedure when there is a recent service or procedure for the same or similar diagnosis on record," with 'recent' understood as sixty days; KMA argued this creates a coding rule beyond CPT and that KRS 304.17A-235 requires 90-day notice of material changes. Whether the 2019 Kentucky policy remains in force was not confirmed. Anthem's standing commercial policy C-09011 (Modifiers 25 and 57) allows separate reimbursement for a significant, separately identifiable E/M with modifier 25 subject to its guidelines (search snippet; not read in full).

**What it means for ODA:** For Anthem (including Anthem FEP lines such as ODA's 99214-24 example), separately track E/M lines carrying modifiers 24 and 25 and procedure lines with 59/X-modifiers: flag $0 or reduced payment with any CAS code, measure the denial/reduction rate before and after 4/1/2025, and check whether a same-or-similar-diagnosis visit occurred within the prior 60 days (the 2019 Kentucky trigger). Appeal packets for modifier 25 denials should document the separately identifiable work and cite CPT modifier 25 definitions; the KRS 304.17A-235 notice argument should be verified by the legal lens before use.

Sources: [ismanet.org](https://www.ismanet.org/ISMA/Resources/e-Reports/4-3-25/Anthem_Prepayment_Modifier.aspx) · [insights.wchsb.com](https://insights.wchsb.com/2025/04/07/insurance-updates-april-2025/) · [ama-assn.org](https://www.ama-assn.org/practice-management/claims-processing/win-doctors-anthem-rescinds-pay-cut-same-day-services) · [kyma.org](https://kyma.org/anthem-update-modifier-25/) · [kyma.org](https://kyma.org/shared/content/uploads/2019/03/Kennan-Wethington-Anthem-letter.pdf) · [providernews.anthem.com](https://providernews.anthem.com/indiana/articles/certain-modifiers-for-outpatient-claims-will-receive-prepaym-24285 (Anthem primary article linked by ISMA; returned HTTP 202 empty body, not readable here)) · [anthem.com](https://www.anthem.com/ky/provider/individual-commercial/reimbursement (Kentucky commercial reimbursement policy index; policy list served by /content/anthem/jcr:content/servlets/formsAssetMetadata.json)) · [anthem.com](https://www.anthem.com/content/dam/digital/docs/provider/commercial/policy/reimb/ABCBS-11232022.pdf (Anthem Commercial Reimbursement Policy C-09011, Modifiers 25 and 57, eff. 11/23/2022, approved 05/22/2024)) · [anthem.com](https://www.anthem.com/content/dam/digital/docs/provider/commercial/policy/reimb/multi-bcbs-cr-rp-015670-22.pdf (Anthem Medicare Advantage Reimbursement Policy G-06003, Modifiers 25 and 57, approved 9/24/2025, eff. 04/01/2026)) · [anthem.com](https://www.anthem.com/content/dam/digital/docs/provider/commercial/policy/reimb/C-09004.pdf (now Commercial Reimbursement Policy C-25002, Code and Clinical Editing Guidelines, approved 03/25/2025, eff. 10/01/2025))

### [VERIFIED] UnitedHealthcare's Evaluation and Management (E/M) Policy, Professional (Commercial and Individual Exchange, policy 2026R5007A; a parallel Medicare Advantage version exists) states that UHC may request medical records when "a physician or Other Qualified Health Care Professional has a billing pattern that deviates significantly from their peers, or claim attributes indicate possible billing errors," may compare a provider's "level of care escalation data" to historical and peer data, and that providers "may experience adjustments to, or denials of" the office visit E/M code if documentation does not support the level; UHC switched from denying to adjusting-and-paying under-documented E/M claims in 2019, and no new 2025-2026 UHC automatic E/M downcoding program was found.

**Checker's wording:** UnitedHealthcare's "Evaluation and Management (E/M) Policy, Professional" for Commercial and Individual Exchange (Policy Number 2026R5007A; a parallel UHC Medicare Advantage version, also numbered 2026R5007A, carries the same substantive language) states: "This reimbursement policy explains when medical records may be requested to ensure that the appropriate level of CPT E/M code is reimbursed based on the health care services provided"; Q&A 2: "Will UnitedHealthcare require medical records for all reported E/M services? A: No. UnitedHealthcare may request medical records when the data indicates a physician or Other Qualified Health Care Professional has a billing pattern that deviates significantly from their peers, or claim attributes indicate possible billing errors" (the MA version says "physician or other health care professional"); "Note: A providers' level of care escalation data and parenteral narcotic use data may be compared to their historical data and to peer data" (MA version has the typo "parental"); and "Providers may experience adjustments to, or denials of the office visit or other outpatient E/M code or emergency department E/M code reported if the documentation does not support the E/M level submitted. The provider may resubmit the claim with a revised E/M code for denied claims." Q&A 3 says cloned EHR/hard-copy documentation "would not be acceptable documentation to support the claim as billed." Commercial policy history: approved 4/21/2016, published 9/1/2016, anniversary review 5/25/2025, version change 8/1/2025 ("Qualified" wording), anniversary review and version change 4/1/2026; MA history: implemented 9/1/2016, version change 4/1/2026. Per AAFP FPM (David Twiddy, Jan. 28, 2019, quoting Kent Moore): "Under a policy change effective in the second quarter of 2019, UHC will no longer automatically deny a claim based on the documentation but instead may adjust the level of a submitted code to reflect what it believes is appropriate and then pay it. The policy appears aimed at Level 4 and 5 services. If a physician disagrees with the adjustment, he or she could still appeal as that process will not change." Note the current policy text still contemplates both adjustments and denials. All 23 UHC Commercial Reimbursement Policy Update Bulletins and all 23 UHC Medicare Advantage Reimbursement Policy Update Bulletins published for January 2025 through November 2026 (December 2026 not yet posted) contain no announcement of a new or expanded E/M level-review or downcoding program; the only E/M references are the Professional/Technical Component policy change (radiology review bundled into the E/M; Jan–Mar 2026), the 2027 global-OB code change (Oct–Nov 2026), and routine code-update listings (e.g., MA May 2025 "Evaluation and Management (EM), Professional" code update). AASM (Nov. 14, 2025) states it "identified additional health plans with active or proposed downcoding programs targeting higher-level evaluation and management (E/M) services" and lists UnitedHealthcare among them (with Ambetter, Anthem, BCBS NC/MA/TN, Humana, Kaiser, Molina, WellCare, Sunshine Health), adding that "many also rely on claims analytics rather than clinical review, allowing E/M codes to be automatically adjusted before payment," but it does not describe any UHC-specific program; the NBC News (Oct. 9, 2025) characterization could not be re-verified in this check and should not be cited. Reconsideration/appeal window (verified in the 2026 UnitedHealthcare Care Provider Administrative Guide, Chapter 10 "Claim reconsideration and appeals process"): "You must submit your reconsideration and appeal to us within 12 months from the date of the original claim EOB or PRA (or as required by law or your Agreement)"; the 2-step process "allows a total of 12 months for timely submission for both" steps, "not 12 months for step 1 and 12 months for step 2. If an appeal is submitted after the time frame has expired, we uphold the denial." UHC's live web page (uhcprovider.com/en/claims-payments-billing/appeals.html) likewise says "Timing: You have 12 months to complete the following steps: Step 1: File a claim reconsideration request. Step 2: File an appeal," while its FAQ adds "Timelines vary. Please refer to your Participation Agreement"; the older URL .../claim-reconsideration-appeals-process.html redirects to UHC's error page.

Policy text (UHC PDF): "This reimbursement policy explains when medical records may be requested to ensure that the appropriate level of CPT E/M code is reimbursed"; Q2: "Will UnitedHealthcare require medical records for all reported E/M services? A: No. UnitedHealthcare may request medical records when the data indicates a physician or Other Qualified Health Care Professional has a billing pattern that deviates significantly from their peers, or claim attributes indicate possible billing errors."; "Note: A providers' level of care escalation data and parenteral narcotic use data may be compared to their historical data and to peer data."; "Providers may experience adjustments to, or denials of the office visit or other outpatient E/M code or emergency department E/M code reported if the documentation does not support the E/M level submitted. The provider may resubmit the claim with a revised E/M code for denied claims." Cloned EHR documentation "would not be acceptable." History: policy published 9/1/2016, anniversary review 4/1/2026. AAFP FPM (1/28/2019): starting Q2 2019 UHC would no longer automatically deny E/M claims whose documentation did not support the level but "may adjust the level of a submitted code... and then pay it," aimed at level 4 and 5; appeals unchanged. UHC's commercial and MA reimbursement policy update bulletins that could be retrieved for 2025-2026 (Feb 2025; Jan, Feb, Mar, Oct, Nov 2026) contain no E/M level-review announcement. AASM (11/14/2025) lists UnitedHealthcare among plans with "active or proposed" downcoding programs; NBC News (10/9/2025) did not describe UHC as downcoding. Reconsideration window: secondary sources describe UHC's 2026 Care Provider Administrative Guide as allowing 12 months from the original EOB/PRA for the combined reconsideration-and-appeal process unless the participation agreement provides otherwise (not verified against the guide); UHC's reconsideration web page returned 404.

**What it means for ODA:** For UHC commercial, UHC Medicare Advantage, Optum and UMR remits, treat records requests (CARC 252 with N-series remark codes) on level 4/5 E/M lines as the leading indicator of a peer-deviation flag and track response deadlines; treat paid-below-billed E/M lines after a records review as appealable (documentation-based, so the appeal must rebut specific MDM/time elements). Because UHC adjusts rather than denies, the dashboard must fingerprint allowed amounts by code to catch silent level reductions. Deadline engine should default to 12 months for UHC but mark it unverified until the 2026 Administrative Guide or ODA's participation agreement is checked.

Sources: [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/comm-reimbursement/COMM-Evaluation-and-Management-Policy.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/medadv-reimbursement/MEDADV-Evaluation-and-Management-Policy.pdf) · [aafp.org](https://aafp.org/pubs/fpm/blogs/gettingpaid/entry/UHC_downcoding.html) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/comm-reimbursement/rpub/UHC-COMM-RPUB-March-2026.pdf) · [aasm.org](https://aasm.org/growing-trend-of-downcoding-among-commercial-payers/) · [aafp.org](https://www.aafp.org/fpm/blogs/getting-paid/UHC_downcoding) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/en/policies-protocols/commercial-policies/commercial-reimbursement-policies.html) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/en/policies-protocols/medicare-advantage-policies/medicare-advantage-reimbursement-policies.html) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/medadv-reimbursement/rpub/UHC-MEDADV-RPUB-NOV-2026.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/medadv-reimbursement/rpub/UHC-MEDADV-RPUB-MAY-2025.pdf)

### [VERIFIED] UnitedHealthcare commercial plans (and, per its policy, Individual Exchange) do not pay G2211 separately: UHC's Rebundling Policy, Professional (2026R0056A) answers that "visit complexity for services G2211 and G0545 are structured in the reimbursement for evaluation and management services and not paid separately," a change effective for dates of service on or after September 1, 2024; Medicare (and MA plans following CMS) deny G2211 when the same-day O/O E/M carries modifier 25, except, from January 1, 2025, when the modifier-25 trigger is an allowed Part B preventive service, immunization administration or Annual Wellness Visit.

**Checker's wording:** UnitedHealthcare commercial plans and UHC Individual Exchange plans do not pay G2211 separately. UHC's "Rebundling Policy, Professional" (currently Policy Number 2026R0056A, version dated 1/1/2026, "Last Published 01.01.2026" on UHC's commercial policy index as of 2026-11-06) is titled a "Commercial and Individual Exchange" policy and states it "applies to all UnitedHealthcare Commercial benefit plans" and "all Individual Exchange benefit plans" and "to all products and all network and non-network physicians and Other QHPs." Q&A 13 reads verbatim: "Q: Will UnitedHealthcare Commercial and Individual Exchange allow separate reimbursement for codes G2211 and G0545 for visit complexity when billed on the same date of service for the same patient by the same physician or Other QHP with an Evaluation and Management Service? A: No, visit complexity for services G2211 and G0545 are structured in the reimbursement for evaluation and management services and not paid separately." The policy's History shows "9/1/2024 – Q&A #13 added," "1/1/2025 – Q&A #13 revised," "4/1/2025 – Q&A #13 revised," and "1/1/2026 – Policy Version change" (so the current wording, including G0545, post-dates the original 9/1/2024 entry; G0545 is a 2025 hospital inpatient/observation infectious-disease add-on irrelevant to dermatology office claims). The policy states UHC "uses a customized version of the Optum Claims Editing System known as iCES Clearinghouse" and notes that provider contracts, legislative mandates and benefit documents "may supplement, modify or, in some cases, supersede" it. XiFin (7/10/2024), citing UHC's June 2024 reimbursement policy update bulletin (that bulletin URL is now dead), reported G2211 would be included in the Commercial Rebundling Policy for dates of service on or after September 1, 2024. An August 1, 2024 joint letter to UHC's CMO signed by eight organizations (AAN, AANP, AAPA, ACR, ACP, AGA, ASAM, CSRO; ACR was the contact, hosted on ACP's site) protested UHC's "decision to discontinue commercial plan reimbursement for... G2211 effective September 1, 2024" and confirms UHC had paid G2211 on commercial plans from January 1 to August 31, 2024; the current policy shows no reversal through the 1/1/2026 version. UMR's "Information on Payment of Out-of-Network Benefits" disclosure states "UnitedHealth affiliates may apply certain payment policies," e.g., the Multiple Procedure Policy (100% primary, 50% secondary, 25–50% subsequent); no UMR-specific G2211 or modifier 25 policy was located. Medicare: per CMS MLN MM13473 (revised April 29, 2025; CRs 13473 and 13705; transmittals R12461CP and R13199OTN), "G2211 isn't payable when you report the associated office and outpatient E/M visit with modifier 25, except in certain cases starting January 1, 2025," and "Starting January 1, 2025: G2211 is payable even if you report the base code with modifier 25 only when the service or other procedure requiring the reporting of modifier 25 is an allowed Part B service" per Attachment 1 of CR 13705 ("Part B preventive services, Immunization administrations, Annual Wellness Visits"); the CY2025 PFS final rule fact sheet frames this as AWV, vaccine administration or any Medicare Part B preventive service (including the IPPE) in the office/outpatient setting. Additionally (omitted from the original claim), the CY2026 PFS final rule (CR 14315 / Transmittal R13507CP issued 12/5/2025; MLN MM14315, effective 1/1/2026) extends G2211 payment to the home or residence E/M family (99341, 99342, 99344, 99345, 99347–99350) without changing the modifier 25 rule. UHC's Medicare Advantage "Add-on Codes Policy, Professional" (2026R9007F, Q&A 6 updated 8/11/2026) mirrors CMS: "Reimbursement for code G2211 will not be provided when reported on the same date of service as an O/O E/M visit (codes 99202-99205, 99211-99215) and home or residence E/M codes (99341–99345; 99347–99350) that is reported with modifier 25... unless certain Part B preventive services are provided on the same day." ODA's remits (UHC G2211 $0 with CO-45 + M80 "Not covered when performed during the same session/date as a previously processed service for the patient"; UMR G2211 $0 with PI-B10 "Allowed amount has been reduced because a component of the basic procedure/test was paid") are consistent with these bundling policies and, for dates of service on or after 9/1/2024, are not appealable on policy grounds absent a contract or state-law override; UHC commercial G2211 denials for DOS 1/1/2024–8/31/2024, and any Medicare/MA G2211 denial where the base E/M did not carry modifier 25 (or where the modifier-25 trigger was a Part B preventive service, immunization administration or AWV on/after 1/1/2025), remain legitimate review candidates.

UHC Rebundling Policy Q13 (quoted above); the policy notes UHC uses "a customized version of the Optum Claims Editing System known as iCES Clearinghouse." XiFin (7/10/2024), citing UHC's June 2024 reimbursement policy bulletin: "Effective with dates of service on or after September 1, 2024, HCPCS code G2211 will be included within the UnitedHealthcare Commercial Rebundling Policy, Professional." ACP-led coalition letter asked UHC to reverse; no reversal found. CMS MLN MM13473 (rev. 4/29/2025): "G2211 isn't payable when you report the associated office and outpatient E/M visit with modifier 25, except in certain cases starting January 1, 2025"; CR 13705: "Starting January 1, 2025: G2211 is payable even if you report the base code with modifier 25 only when the service or other procedure requiring the reporting of modifier 25 is an allowed Part B service... Part B preventive services, Immunization administrations, Annual Wellness Visits." UHC's Medicare Advantage Add-On Codes policy (search snippet, not read in full) mirrors this. UMR's published payment disclosure (search snippet) says UnitedHealth affiliates' payment policies such as the Multiple Procedure Policy (secondary procedures at 50%) may apply to UMR-administered plans; no UMR-specific modifier 25 or G2211 policy was found. ODA's observed remits (UHC G2211 $0 with CO-45 + M80 "Not covered when performed during the same session/date as a previously processed service"; UMR G2211 $0 with PI-B10 "Allowed amount has been reduced because a component of the basic procedure/test was paid") are consistent with the commercial bundling policy.

**What it means for ODA:** Detection/triage rule: classify G2211 $0 lines by line of business: UHC/UMR/UHSS commercial G2211 denials are policy-based (not appealable, should be excluded from the 'downcoding losses' total and instead reported as a contractual leakage category for contract negotiation); Medicare and MA G2211 denials where the E/M carried modifier 25 for a dermatology procedure are correct under CMS rules (do not appeal); MA/Medicare G2211 denials where the E/M had no modifier 25, or where the only same-day service was a preventive service after 1/1/2025, are appealable. For UMR's B10 denial of 99214-25 on the same claim as 17110, the controlling UMR/UHC edit was not identified; log as a modifier-25 bundling denial requiring records-based appeal.

Sources: [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/comm-reimbursement/COMM-Rebundling-Policy.pdf) · [xifin.com](https://www.xifin.com/billing-beat/uhc-rebundling-policy-professional-effective-9-1-2024/) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/document/mm13473-how-use-office-and-outpatient-evaluation-and-management-visit-complexity-add-code-g2211.pdf) · [acponline.org](https://www.acponline.org/sites/default/files/acp-policy-library/letters/joint_letter_to_unitedhealthcare_on_commercial_reimbursement_for_g2211_2024.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/medadv-reimbursement/MEDADV-Add-On-Codes-Policy.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/comm-reimbursement/COMM-Add-On-Codes-Policy.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/en/policies-protocols/commercial-policies/commercial-reimbursement-policies.html) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/comm-reimbursement/rpub/UHC-COMM-RPUB-January-2025.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/comm-reimbursement/rpub/UHC-COMM-RPUB-April-2025.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/comm-reimbursement/rpub/UHC-COMM-RPUB-November-2026.pdf)

### [DISPUTED] Humana's Medicare Advantage Claims Payment Policy CP2024003, "Reimbursement Level of Certain Evaluation and Management (E/M) Services" (published 04/2024), states that Humana reviews 99204, 99205, 99214, 99215 (plus 92004/92014, 99223/99233, 99244/99245/99254/99255, 99284/99285, nursing facility and home codes), "determines the reimbursement level based on the severity of the patient's condition," and "When the billed E/M service code is higher than what is supported by the claim diagnosis coding, Humana adjusts reimbursement to the E/M service level determined by the review"; Humana's 2025 provider manual requires claim disputes within 18 months of the paid date via Availity's "Dispute Claim" tool.

**Corrected (source check):** Humana Claims Payment Policy CP2024003, "Reimbursement Level of Certain Evaluation and Management (E/M) Services" (Application: Medicare Advantage Products; Published date 04/2024; current edition carries "Revision date: 10/2025"), states: "The following policy applies to only professional services." and "Humana determines the reimbursement level based on the severity of the patient's condition. When the billed E/M service code is higher than what is supported by the claim diagnosis coding, Humana adjusts reimbursement to the E/M service level determined by the review." The ORIGINAL 04/2024 edition (the copy hosted by the AAO) listed 92004/92014, 99204/99205/99214/99215, 99223/99233, 99244/99245/99254/99255, 99284/99285, 99306/99309/99310 and 99344/99345/99349/99350. The CURRENT edition served by Humana's claims-payment-policies site (as of 2026-11-06) is narrowed to office/outpatient codes only: "Humana reviews the higher-level office or other outpatient E/M services, CPT codes 99204, 99205, 99214 and 99215, to determine the level for reimbursement"; the ophthalmology, inpatient, consultation, ED, nursing-facility and home codes were removed. Humana's live policy list (123 policies) still shows the policy as Medicare Advantage, published 4/4/2024, and contains no commercial E/M reimbursement-level policy. The AAO letter of February 25, 2025 to Kate Goodrich, MD (SVP, Chief Medical Officer, Humana) objects that the policy "automatically adjusts the level of reimbursement without consideration of the American Medical Association Current Procedural Terminology (CPT) E/M coding guidelines," infers that Humana uses the claim diagnosis code(s) as the proxy for severity, says the policy "completely ignores time-based billing," and quotes AMA: "the final diagnosis for a condition does not, in and of itself, determine the complexity or risk"; it does not mention Cotiviti. AAPC forum posts (Oct 28, 2024 to Feb 12, 2025) report that "Since August, claims sent to Humana medicare have been downcoded by them without requesting/reviewing records," that Humana has "a downcoding policy for level 4 and 5 based on claims information (so the diagnosis mostly)" and "will require an appeal," with posters appealing each claim individually. Oncology Practice Management (July 2026, Vol 16 No 4) asserts, without citation, that "Humana expanded predictive analytics-based downcoding into commercial and Medicare Advantage plans the same year" (2025); this remains unverified and no Humana commercial E/M policy document exists in Humana's live policy list. No source read links Cotiviti to Humana's E/M edits. Dispute process: Humana's 2025 Provider Manual (589003ALL1024) says "For all products: If providers disagree with Humana's initial adjudication of a claim, they can request a dispute/reopening of the issue," submitted by any of three methods: online via Availity Essentials (Claim Status tool > "Dispute Claim" button > Appeals worklist), by phone (800-448-6262 or the number on the member ID card), or by mail (Humana Correspondence, P.O. Box 14601, Lexington, KY 40512); required content includes claim number, charge and payment amounts, "Proposed correct payment amount," "Difference between the amount paid and the proposed correct payment amount," "Brief description of the basis for the contestation request," and "Relevant supporting documentation (e.g., medical records, copy of invoice, referral form)"; "Claims disputes must be received by Humana within 18 months of the date the claim was paid—unless state or federal law or the agreement require another time period—or the claim will not be reopened"; escalation to HumanaProviderServices@humana.com (Provider Concierge Unit). The CURRENT 2026 Provider Manual (773902ALL0725, non-delegated, PDF dated Feb 2026) keeps the same three channels, required content and escalation path but restates the deadline as "within 18 months from the date the provider received notice of the original claim determination unless state or federal law or the agreement requires another time period or the claim will not be reopened," and adds: "Multiple provider dispute requests per provider encounter are generally unacceptable... to explicitly identify specific new material information that justifies further review" (Concierge reference ID now within 3-5 business days instead of 72 hours). Availity is an available channel, not a required one. Member-side Medicare Advantage appeals (65 calendar days from the adverse determination notice) are a separate process from the provider dispute window in both manuals.

_Why:_ All five cited URLs were opened. Three were PDFs that the fetcher could not parse, so I extracted their text with pdftotext from the downloaded files. (1) The AAO-hosted CP2024003 PDF matches the claim verbatim (Application: Medicare Advantage Products; Published date 04/2024; "applies to only professional services"; the full seven-family code list; the "severity of the patient's condition" / "claim diagnosis coding" / "adjusts reimbursement" sentences). (2) The AAO letter is dated February 25, 2025, addressed to Kate Goodrich, MD, SVP/CMO, Humana, references CP2024003, and contains the quoted criticisms; the claim's "AMA CPT" is an abbreviation of the letter's "American Medical Association Current Procedural Terminology (CPT)". No Cotiviti mention. (3) The AAPC thread contains the quoted "without requesting/reviewing records" (Oct 28, 2024), the Feb 12, 2025 description of a level 4/5 policy "based on claims information (so the diagnosis mostly)" that "will require an appeal," and per-claim appeals. (4) The 2025 Provider Manual PDF (589003ALL1024 GHHMH6TEN, 37 pages) contains every quoted dispute-process sentence, including the 18-months-from-paid-date rule, the Availity "Dispute 

**Corrected (expert review):** Humana Claims Payment Policy CP2024003, "Reimbursement Level of Certain Evaluation and Management (E/M) Services" (Application: Medicare Advantage Products; Published date 04/2024; "The following policy applies to only professional services"), states verbatim: "Humana determines the reimbursement level based on the severity of the patient's condition. When the billed E/M service code is higher than what is supported by the claim diagnosis coding, Humana adjusts reimbursement to the E/M service level determined by the review." The April 2024 version (the copy hosted by the American Academy of Ophthalmology) listed 92004/92014, 99204/99205/99214/99215, 99223/99233, 99244/99245/99254/99255, 99284/99285, 99306/99309/99310 and 99344/99345/99349/99350. The version currently served from Humana's own claims-payment-policy site (same policy number, marked "Revision date: 10/2025", retrieved 2026-11-06) has been NARROWED: "Humana reviews the higher-level office or other outpatient E/M services, CPT codes 99204, 99205, 99214 and 99215, to determine the level for reimbursement." All other code families were removed; the Medicare Advantage-only scope, professional-only scope and diagnosis-based methodology are unchanged. Humana's live policy list (123 policies as of 2026-11-04) contains no commercial-line E/M level-of-service policy and no reference to any edit vendor (e.g., Cotiviti); the only other E/M policies are "Multiple E/M Services" (Kentucky Medicaid, CP2024009) and a 2018 facility ED-level policy. The AAO letter of February 25, 2025 to Humana SVP/CMO Kate Goodrich, MD objected that CP2024003 "automatically adjusts the level of reimbursement without consideration of the American Medical Association Current Procedural Terminology (CPT) E/M coding guidelines," infers the level from "the diagnosis code(s) on a claim," and "completely ignores time-based billing," quoting CPT: "the final diagnosis for a condition does not, in and of itself, determine the complexity or risk" (verified in AMA's 2023 E/M guidelines, "Number and Complexity of Problems Addressed"). AAPC forum posts (Oct 28, 2024 to Feb 12, 2025) report Humana Medicare downcoding level 4/5 office visits since August 2024 "without requesting/reviewing records," based mostly on diagnosis codes, requiring a per-claim appeal; no policy number, vendor or appeal outcome is given. Oncology Practice Management (July 2026, Shawntea Gordon) asserts, without any citation, that "Humana expanded predictive analytics-based downcoding into commercial and Medicare Advantage plans the same year" (context implies 2025, the year is not stated); this is unsourced and inconsistent with Humana's own MA policy dating from April 2024 and should not be cited in an appeal. Dispute process: Humana's 2025 Provider Manual (589003ALL1024) stated "For all products: If providers disagree with Humana's initial adjudication of a claim, they can request a dispute/reopening of the issue," via three channels: online on Availity Essentials (Claim Status tool, "Dispute Claim" button, then the Appeals worklist under Claims & Payments), by phone (800-448-6262) or by mail (Humana Correspondence, P.O. Box 14601, Lexington, KY 40512); written requests should include provider name, NPI/Tax ID, member name/ID, date of service, claim number, charge amount, payment amount, "Proposed correct payment amount," "Difference between the amount paid and the proposed correct payment amount," "Brief description of the basis for the contestation request" and supporting documentation; and "Claims disputes must be received by Humana within 18 months of the date the claim was paid, unless state or federal law or the agreement require another time period, or the claim will not be reopened." That manual has been superseded: the 2026 Provider Manual (773902ALL0725, effective March 1, 2026, which "replaces and supersedes any and all other previous versions") keeps the same channels and content list but restates the deadline as "Claims disputes must be received by Humana within 18 months from the date the provider received notice of the original claim determination unless state or federal law or the agreement requires another time period or the claim will not be reopened," adds that "Multiple provider dispute requests per provider encounter are generally unacceptable" unless the request identifies "specific new material information that justifies further review," and provides escalation to HumanaProviderServices@humana.com (Provider Concierge Unit), which now issues a reference ID within 3 to 5 business days (72 hours in the 2025 manual). Separately, Medicare Advantage member appeals must be filed within 65 calendar days of the adverse determination notice (Humana manuals; CMS; 42 CFR 422.582(b) as amended at 89 FR 30827: 60 calendar days after receipt, with receipt presumed 5 days after the notice date), and nonparticipating providers may appeal MA claim denials within 65 calendar days of the denial only with a signed Waiver of Liability; neither is the contracted-provider dispute window.

_Why:_ Method: WebSearch budget was exhausted for this turn, so verification used direct fetches of the cited documents plus independent retrieval from Humana's own systems (the Next.js policy page's JSON API /api/forms/claimPaymentPolicy and Humana's Documentum document server), CMS, LII (eCFR was proxy-blocked) and the AMA CPT guideline PDF. All PDFs were text-extracted with pdftotext and quoted from the extracted text.

Confirmed: (1) The AAO-hosted CP2024003 PDF is exactly as claimed: Application Medicare Advantage Products, Published 04/2024, professional services only, the full code list, and the two-sentence diagnosis-based methodology. (2) The AAO letter (Feb 25, 2025, to Kate Goodrich MD, signed by Repka and McAllister) contains the quoted objections verbatim; the CPT sentence it quotes appears verbatim in AMA's 2023 E/M guidelines. (3) The AAPC thread matches: post #1 (Oct 28, 2024) reports Humana Medicare downcoding since August without record review; post #13 (Feb 12, 2025) describes a level 4/5 downcoding policy based on claim data, mostly diagnosis codes, requiring appeal; no vendor or policy number mentioned. (4) The 2025 manual contains the exact "For all products" sentenc

Policy text (copy hosted by the American Academy of Ophthalmology): application "Medicare Advantage Products"; "The following policy applies to only professional services."; code list as above. AAO letter to Humana's CMO (2/25/2025) objects that the policy "automatically adjusts the level of reimbursement without consideration of the AMA CPT E/M coding guidelines," uses diagnosis codes as the proxy, and "completely ignores time-based billing," quoting CPT: "the final diagnosis for a condition does not, in and of itself, determine the complexity or risk." AAPC forum posts (Oct 2024-Feb 2025) describe Humana downcoding level 4/5 E/M since August 2024 "without requesting/reviewing records," based mostly on diagnosis, requiring per-claim appeals. Oncology Practice Management (July 2026) asserts Humana "expanded predictive analytics-based downcoding into commercial and Medicare Advantage plans in 2025" (unverified; no Humana commercial E/M policy document found). No evidence linking Cotiviti to Humana's E/M edits was found (Cotiviti prepay programs are documented at Highmark, Horizon and Ambetter). Dispute process (Humana 2025 Provider Manual, 'Provider Claims Dispute Process'): "For all products: If providers disagree with Humana's initial adjudication of a claim, they can request a dispute/reopening"; online via Availity Essentials Claim Status > "Dispute Claim"; required content includes "Difference between the amount paid and the proposed correct payment amount," basis, and supporting documentation; "Claims disputes must be received by Humana within 18 months of the date the claim was paid—unless state or federal law or the agreement require another time period—or the claim will not be reopened"; escalation to HumanaProviderServices@humana.com (Provider Concierge Unit). Member-side MA appeals are 65 calendar days (not the provider dispute window).

**What it means for ODA:** Humana Medicare Advantage is a top-four ODA payer: the dashboard should compute Humana's paid-level-vs-billed-level and allowed-amount deltas for 99204/99205/99214/99215 by provider and by primary diagnosis, since the policy is explicitly diagnosis-driven; appeal letters should quote CP2024003's own sentence and the CPT guideline that diagnosis alone does not determine complexity, attach MDM/time documentation, and be batched through Availity within 18 months of payment (the longest window among ODA's payers, so Humana backlog recovery is feasible). The weekly ingest should also watch Humana's first-Friday 'claims processing edit notifications' for changes.
