CMS 'Parts C & D Enrollee Grievances, Organization/Coverage Determinations, and Appeals Guidance' (effective July 6, 2026): non-contract provider denial notices must state the provider 'Has the right to request a reconsideration,' 'Must submit a Waiver of Liability form holding the enrollee harmless regardless of the outcome of the appeal,' and 'Has 65 calendar days from the remittance notification date to request a reconsideration'; section 50.1.1: 'A non-contract provider, on his or her own behalf, may request a reconsideration (i.e., an appeal) for a denied claim only if the non-contract provider completes a Waiver of Liability (WOL) statement'; 'Plans must issue a substantive reconsideration decision within 60 days ... from when the WOL is received'; 'Even reconsideration requests submitted by non-contracted providers that relate to the type or level of service furnished to the enrollee must be reviewed in accordance with the administrative appeal processes'; examples that must receive full appeal rights include 'Downcoding,' 'Bundling issues and disputed rate of payment. Pre- and post-pay bundling and global payment determinations,' and 'Level of care or rate of payment denials. Payment of a reduced fee schedule amount for a course of treatment. For example, a provider bills a procedure code for a visit but the plan reimburses based on a lower level of care'; 'a non-contracted provider does not need to receive zero payment to request a reconsideration'; a billing agency may file without an AOR 'but ... may not sign the WOL on behalf of the provider'; and: 'No party may appeal a contract provider payment denial when the enrollee has no further financial liability. Contract provider disputes involving plan payment denials are governed by the appeals/dispute resolution provisions in the contract between the provider and the plan.' Payment timeframes in the guidance: 95% of non-contract clean claims within 30 days, others paid or denied within 60 days; contract providers per contract terms. CMS managed-care page: C2C Innovative Solutions is the Part C IRE for requests received on or after May 1, 2026 (MAXIMUS for earlier requests). The CY2025 (CMS-4205-F) and CY2026 (CMS-4208-F) fact sheets contain no 120-day provider appeal window; none was found.

**What it means for ODA:** Deadline engine for MA: appeal_due = 835 remittance date + 65 days (statutory 60 days from receipt with the 5-day presumption); flag at 45 days. For non-contracted MA claims, auto-assemble the packet: WOL signed by the provider (not the billing agent), the remittance showing the downcode, the note, and the argument that Original Medicare would pay the billed level (422.214). Quote the guidance's 'Downcoding' and 'Level of care or rate of payment' examples so the plan cannot route the request to a 'claims inquiry.' If the plan upholds, it must auto-forward to the IRE (C2C); track IRE outcomes per payer to build the pattern evidence the practice needs. For contracted MA products, the dashboard must instead track the contract's dispute window (data gap: contracts not in Dropbox) and use CMS's rules as persuasive authority plus a complaint to the plan's CMS account manager.

Sources: [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/42/422.574) · [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/42/422.578) · [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/42/422.582) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/medicare/appeals-and-grievances/mmcag/downloads/parts-c-and-d-enrollee-grievances-organization-coverage-determinations-and-appeals-guidance.pdf) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/medicare/appeals-grievances/managed-care) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/newsroom/fact-sheets/contract-year-2025-medicare-advantage-and-part-d-final-rule-cms-4205-f)

### [UNVERIFIED] For Medicare fee-for-service with CGS (J15 Kentucky; same federal rules for J8 Indiana), a redetermination must be requested within 120 days of receipt of the initial determination (receipt presumed 5 days after the notice) and CGS decides within 60 days, while coding/modifier/unit/fee-schedule errors are fixed through a clerical-error reopening within one year (myCGS: 12 months from the remittance date) with no appeal; reopenings cannot add unbilled services and 'third party payer errors do not constitute clerical errors.'

42 CFR 405.942(a): 'any request for redetermination must be filed within 120 calendar days' from receipt of the initial determination, 'the date of receipt of the initial determination will be presumed to be 5 calendar days after' the notice date 'unless there is evidence to the contrary'; (b): good-cause extension (serious illness, records destroyed, incorrect contractor information, non-receipt). 42 CFR 405.980(a)(1): a reopening 'is a remedial action taken to change a binding determination or decision ... even though the binding determination or decision may have been correct at the time it was made'; (a)(3): contractors must 'process clerical errors (which includes minor errors and omissions) as reopenings,' where 'clerical error includes human or mechanical errors on the part of the party or the contractor'; (b)/(c): a contractor may reopen, and a party may request reopening, 'Within 1 year from the date of the initial determination or redetermination for any reason' and 'Within 4 years ... for good cause' (any time for fraud); (a)(4): no reopening while the issue is under appeal. CMS IOM Pub. 100-04, Chapter 34, section 10.4: clerical errors are 'human or mechanical errors on the part of the party or the contractor, such as: Mathematical or computational mistakes; Transposed procedure or diagnostic codes; Inaccurate data entry; Misapplication of a fee schedule; Computer errors; or, Denial of claims as duplicates ...; Incorrect data items, such as provider number, use of a modifier or date of service'; 'A contractor shall not grant a reopening to add items or services that were not previously billed'; 'Third party payer errors do not constitute clerical errors'; section 10.2: a refusal to reopen 'is not appealable'; section 10.5: telephone reopenings are required for A/B MACs (B). CGS 'Reopening vs. Redetermination' job aid (revised December 16, 2025): 'A reopening must be requested within one year from the date of the initial determination'; 'Written Reopenings may take up to 60 days to process'; 'Redetermination requests must be submitted within 120 days from the initial claim determination'; good cause only for incorrect official information or unavoidable circumstances; redeterminations are for medical-necessity denials with documentation, MUE denials, shared-care/global denials, etc.; CGS level-1 page: '120 days from the date of receipt of the notice of initial determination,' 'CGS has 60 days to process an appeal,' no signature required since July 8, 2019, 'Appeals submitted for the GA modifier require a valid, signed ABN.' myCGS reopenings page: 'Requests must be submitted within 12 months of the original claim remittance date'; reopening types: Procedure Codes, Modifier, Units, Header Diagnosis Codes, Line Diagnosis Pointer, Billed Amount, Date of Service, Place of Service, Rendering NPI; payment-reducing changes go to Overpayment Recovery. Forms: Redetermination Request (cgsmedicare.com/pdf/partb_redeterminationform.pdf); myCGS reopenings (cgsmedicare.com/mycgs/ssi/forms/reopenings.html); clerical reopening guide (cgsmedicare.com/partb/forms/gateways/when_to.html).

**What it means for ODA:** Medicare FFS does not downcode E/M levels on routine adjudication, so Medicare KY/IN discrepancies are mostly ODA-side errors or edits (modifier 25/59, units, G2211 pointer, MUE). The dashboard should split Medicare FFS findings into two queues with separate clocks: 'reopening' (code/modifier/units/fee-schedule errors; deadline = remittance date + 12 months; file via myCGS, no records) and 'redetermination' (medical-necessity, MUE, global/shared-care denials; deadline = notice date + 5 + 120 days; attach records, ABN for GA). Because a reopening cannot add services never billed, missed G2211/add-on lines must go through a corrected claim or redetermination, not a reopening. Medicare FFS allowed amounts also serve as the benchmark for MA findings.

Sources: [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/42/405.942) · [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/42/405.980) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/regulations-and-guidance/guidance/manuals/downloads/clm104c34.pdf) · [cgsmedicare.com](https://www.cgsmedicare.com/partb/forms/pdf/reopen_vs_redet_jobaid.pdf) · [cgsmedicare.com](https://www.cgsmedicare.com/partb/appeals/level1.html) · [cgsmedicare.com](https://www.cgsmedicare.com/mycgs/ssi/forms/reopenings.html) · [cgsmedicare.com](https://www.cgsmedicare.com/partb/forms/gateways/when_to.html) · [cgsmedicare.com](https://www.cgsmedicare.com/partb/appeals/index.html)

### [UNVERIFIED] Federal Employee Program (Anthem FEP) claim disputes follow OPM's regulation, not Kentucky law: the covered individual (or someone with the member's 'specific written consent') has 6 months from the denial notice to ask the carrier to reconsider, the carrier has 30 days to respond, the member then has 90 days to ask OPM to review, and any lawsuit must be filed against OPM (not the carrier) only after exhausting that process and no later than December 31 of the third year after the year of service.

5 CFR 890.105(a)(1): 'All health benefits claims must be submitted initially to the carrier'; if denied in whole or part the covered individual may ask the carrier to reconsider and then ask OPM to review; both steps must be exhausted before judicial review. (a)(2): the procedures also apply to 'other individuals or entities' acting on the member's behalf who have 'the covered individual's specific written consent to pursue payment of the disputed claim.' (b)(1): the covered individual 'has 6 months from the date of the notice' of denial to request reconsideration in writing (extendable for circumstances beyond the individual's control). (b)(2): 'The carrier has 30 days after the date of receipt of a timely-filed request for reconsideration' to affirm the denial in writing, pay, or request additional information (then 30 days after receipt; decides on the record if nothing arrives within 60 days). (c)(1): the request must cite the applicable brochure provisions. (d): an affirmed denial must give 'specific and detailed reasons,' notice of the right to OPM review, and that OPM requests must be 'received within 90 days after the date of the carrier's denial notice.' (e)(1): OPM review request within 90 days of the carrier's affirmance (120 days if the carrier failed to respond); (e)(3): the carrier must give OPM requested information within 30 days; (e)(4): OPM issues a decision or status notice within 90 days. 5 CFR 890.107(c): a suit to recover benefits 'must be brought against OPM and not against the carrier,' with the remedy limited to an order that OPM require the carrier to pay; (d): the suit 'May not be brought prior to exhaustion of the administrative remedies provided in § 890.105' and 'May not be brought later than December 31 of the 3rd year after the year in which the care or service was provided,' and review 'will be limited to the record that was before OPM.' OPM FEHB Handbook: OPM is responsible for 'resolving disputed health insurance claims between the enrollee and the carrier'; carriers act 'on enrollee requests for reconsideration of disputed claims'; OPM's service standard for a disputed-claim review is 60 calendar days. The 2025/2026 Blue Cross Blue Shield Service Benefit Plan brochure (Section 8, disputed claims) could not be retrieved.

**What it means for ODA:** Flag FEP claims separately (Anthem FEP payer name/plan ID in the 835) and apply federal clocks: carrier reconsideration due = EOB/remit date + 6 months; OPM review due = carrier affirmance + 90 days; absolute litigation bar = Dec 31 of the 3rd year after the date of service. The provider cannot dispute in its own name, so intake must capture the member's specific written consent (a one-line authorization on the patient agreement) and appeals must be framed under the brochure's benefit provisions. Kentucky prompt-pay interest and DOI complaints should not be relied on for FEP; use the OPM path and, for patterns, a complaint to OPM's FEHB carrier-oversight staff (open question: specific OPM contact).

Sources: [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/5/890.105) · [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/5/890.107) · [opm.gov](https://www.opm.gov/healthcare-insurance/healthcare/reference-materials/fehb-handbook/)

### [UNVERIFIED] TRICARE (Kentucky is in the East Region, Humana Military) lets a participating/network provider, but not a non-participating provider, appeal a payment determination: a reconsideration request must be mailed within 90 days of the EOB, further appeal requires at least $50 in dispute and a hearing requires at least $300, and the TRICARE-determined allowable charge itself is non-appealable, so only factual/coding disputes (which code was payable) can be appealed.

32 CFR 199.10(a)(2): 'Any party to the initial determination, except CHAMPUS, may appeal an adverse determination'; 'A nonparticipating provider is not a party to the initial determination and may not appeal'; participating providers are parties. (a)(6): examples of non-appealable issues include 'A dispute regarding a requirement of the law or regulation,' 'The amount of the CHAMPUS-determined allowable cost or charge' (because the regulation sets the methodology), and DRG classification/weights. (a)(7): 'An amount in dispute is required for an adverse determination to be appealed'; it is 'the amount of money CHAMPUS would pay' if the disputed services were covered, excluding 'Amounts in excess of the CHAMPUS-determined allowable charge or cost' and cost-sharing. (b)(1)(iii): the reconsideration request 'must be mailed within 90 days after the date of the notice of initial determination,' must 'state the specific matter in dispute' and include the EOB. (b)(4)(v)/(b)(5)(i): further appeal only where the amount in dispute is '$50 or more'; formal review decisions are final if 'less than $300.' tricare.mil/appeals: factual appeals (TRICARE 'denied or stopped payment for services or supplies') and medical-necessity appeals must be postmarked within 90 days; East Region appeals go to 'Humana Military Appeals, P.O. Box 740044, Louisville, KY 40201-9973'; a factual decision is final under $50; formal review is final under $300; at $300 or more an independent hearing may be requested.

**What it means for ODA:** For Tricare 835s: deadline = EOB date + 90 days (postmark); aggregate all downcoded lines on a claim to test the $50 (reconsideration) and $300 (hearing) thresholds; frame the dispute as 'the submitted code was the correct, payable code' (a factual determination) rather than as a dispute over the allowable amount, which is non-appealable. Data gap: confirm ODA's participating/network status with Humana Military and whether the network contract routes coding disputes through a separate claims-review channel before 199.10 reconsideration.

Sources: [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/32/199.10) · [tricare.mil](https://www.tricare.mil/appeals)

### [UNVERIFIED] Statutory momentum against automated adverse decisions is real and citable: Texas SB 815 (signed June 20, 2025, effective September 1, 2025) added Insurance Code section 4201.156 providing that 'A utilization review agent may not use an automated decision system to make, wholly or partly, an adverse determination,' with commissioner audit authority, for health plans delivered, issued or renewed on or after January 1, 2026; together with Indiana's record-review precondition for CPT changes and CMS's 2026 guidance naming 'Downcoding' as an appealable determination, these are the verified authorities for the 'trend' section of appeal letters. Laws in Georgia, Oklahoma, Colorado, Louisiana, Arkansas and Mississippi could not be verified in this run.

Texas SB 815 enrolled text: Section 1 amends Ins. Code 4201.002 to define 'Automated decision system' as 'an algorithm, including an algorithm incorporating an artificial intelligence system, that uses data-based analytics to make, suggest, or recommend certain determinations' and 'Artificial intelligence system' as 'any machine learning-based system that, for any explicit or implicit objective, infers from the inputs' ...; Section 2 adds 4201.156: '(a) A utilization review agent may not use an automated decision system to make, wholly or partly, an adverse determination'; '(b) The commissioner may audit and inspect at any time a utilization review agent's use' of such systems; (c) does not prohibit administrative/support uses; Section 3 amends 4201.303(a)(3) so adverse-determination notices include 'a description of and the source of the screening criteria and review procedures used as guidelines'; Section 4: applies to utilization review for plans 'delivered, issued for delivery, or renewed' on or after January 1, 2026; Section 5: 'This Act takes effect September 1, 2025.' Bill history: Senate passed 03/26/2025, House 05/24/2025, Governor signed 06/20/2025, 'Effective on 9/1/25'; companion HB 2922. Other verified anti-downcoding/appeal authorities: IC 27-8-5.7-6.5 and IC 27-13-36.2-4.5 (no CPT alteration without rule violation or human record review); CMS Parts C&D guidance section 50.1.1 (effective July 6, 2026) listing 'Downcoding' and 'Level of care or rate of payment denials' as appealable. Not verified (search budget exhausted; AMA model-bill/issue-brief URLs returned 404 and the AADA state-legislation page requires member login): the state downcoding statutes attributed to Texas, Georgia, Oklahoma, Colorado, Louisiana, Arkansas, Mississippi and any 2025-2026 enactments or federal bills.

**What it means for ODA:** Appeal-letter 'trend' paragraph can safely cite Texas Ins. Code 4201.156 (2025), Indiana IC 27-8-5.7-6.5 / 27-13-36.2-4.5, and CMS's 2026 appeals guidance, and should ask each payer to disclose whether an automated system changed the code and to identify the human reviewer. Add a dashboard attribute 'automated_downcode_suspected' (code substitution with no records request, same-day adjudication across many claims, identical pattern across providers) to quantify how much of the loss is attributable to algorithmic edits. Before citing any other state's statute, run the follow-up searches listed in open questions.

Sources: [capitol.texas.gov](https://capitol.texas.gov/tlodocs/89R/billtext/html/SB00815F.htm) · [capitol.texas.gov](https://capitol.texas.gov/BillLookup/History.aspx?LegSess=89R&Bill=SB815) · [codes.findlaw.com](https://codes.findlaw.com/in/title-27-insurance/in-code-sect-27-8-5-7-6-5/) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/medicare/appeals-and-grievances/mmcag/downloads/parts-c-and-d-enrollee-grievances-organization-coverage-determinations-and-appeals-guidance.pdf)

### Open questions

- Out-of-state downcoding statutes (Texas, Georgia, Oklahoma, Colorado, Louisiana, Arkansas, Mississippi) and any 2025-2026 enactments or federal bills: not verified because the run's shared WebSearch budget was exhausted before this agent started and the AMA Advocacy Resource Center model-bill/issue-brief URLs returned 404 while the AADA state-legislation page is login-gated. Follow-up: search 'AMA issue brief downcoding state law', 'AADA downcoding legislation', and each state's legislature for 'downcoding' + 'health insurer' (candidate terms: 'alter the CPT code', 'lesser monetary value', 'medical record has been reviewed').
- Kentucky Department of Insurance enforcement actions (agreed orders, fines, market-conduct findings) against Anthem, Humana or UnitedHealthcare in 2024-2026: nothing was found on insurance.ky.gov pages reachable without search. Follow-up: locate the DOI 'administrative actions'/legal division page, and file an open-records request citing KRS 304.17A-722(4) for prompt-pay market-conduct findings for each payer.
- Whether any newer CMS rule gives non-contracted providers 120 days to request an MA reconsideration: not found. The current regulation (42 CFR 422.582(b), as amended 89 FR 30827, Apr. 23, 2024) is 60 calendar days from receipt with receipt presumed 5 days after the notice, which CMS guidance expresses as 65 calendar days from the remittance notification date.
- Indiana late-payment interest percentage for the current year under IC 12-15-21-3(7)(A) (a whole-number rate equal to the state's prior-fiscal-year average general-fund investment yield): the numeric rate was not retrieved; needed to compute Indiana interest in the dashboard.
- Official Indiana General Assembly text and enactment history (P.L. number/year) of IC 27-8-5.7-6.5 and IC 27-13-36.2-4.5: FindLaw copies (current as of Jan. 1, 2026) were used; the IGA site did not render. Verify before quoting in a filing.
- Whether Kentucky has a statute or DOI bulletin restricting payer fees deducted from remittances (e.g., 835 PLB 'CS:ZELIS TRANSACTION FEE', PLB 'AH' origination fees, mandatory virtual-card payments): not researched here; KRS 304.17A-705 covers electronic payment only for pharmacy contracts. Note KRS 304.17A-724 extends prompt-pay duties to any entity the insurer contracts with for claims processing.
- FEP: the 2025/2026 Blue Cross Blue Shield Service Benefit Plan brochure (RI 71-005) Section 8 'disputed claims process' and FEP's provider-authorization requirements could not be downloaded (OPM/fepblue URLs returned HTML/404); also confirm FEHBA preemption (5 U.S.C. 8902(m)(1)) of Kentucky prompt-pay remedies for FEP claims, which was not fetched.
- ODA's contract status with each MA plan/product (Humana MA, UHC MA, BCBS MA, AARP/UHC, Optum, UHC Shared Services) and the dispute-resolution clauses in each commercial contract: required to decide between Subpart M (WOL) appeals and contract disputes, and to compute contract-specific deadlines; contracts are not in the Dropbox data described.
- Plan funding status (fully insured and Kentucky- or Indiana-regulated vs self-funded ERISA vs non-ERISA governmental/church) for each employer group behind Anthem, UMR, UHC and Humana commercial claims: the 835 does not carry it; needed to route each underpayment to the correct statute (KRS 304.17A-700 et seq., IC 27-8-5.7, or 29 CFR 2560.503-1).
- Patient-level authorizations: whether ODA's intake forms include (a) an ERISA designation of authorized representative (DOL FAQ B-2: an assignment is typically not such a grant), (b) the FEP member's 'specific written consent' under 5 CFR 890.105(a)(2), and (c) a TRICARE appeal authorization; all three are prerequisites for the practice to run appeals hands-off.
- KRS 304.17A-613, cross-referenced in KRS 304.17A-706(2)(c)3 for the interest accrual start date on retrospectively reviewed claims, was not fetched; confirm its timeframe.
- TRICARE East (Humana Military) network-provider dispute process: whether coding disputes by network providers go through a contractor 'claims review' step before the 32 CFR 199.10 reconsideration, and the current Humana Military appeals form/portal; only tricare.mil and the CFR were reviewed.
- Kentucky DOI Advisory Opinions 2020-006 (self-funded non-ERISA internal/external review) and 2023-04 (utilization review medical directors) were identified by title only; their text may add requirements relevant to governmental self-funded plans and UR physician review.

## What wins E/M downcoding appeals in dermatology, and the appeal ladder

Research lens: what wins E/M downcoding appeals in dermatology and the appeal ladder. The shared WebSearch budget was exhausted before this agent ran, so all facts below come from primary pages and PDFs fetched directly by URL (AMA, CMS/eCFR, CGS, OPM/FEP, Kentucky Legislature, UHC, Anthem KY, Cigna, Aetna, Humana, HFMA) plus two dermatology trade articles. Key results: (1) the CPT MDM table and the 2024 single-minimum time rule give two independent, quotable arguments per downcoded line; (2) AMA's own FAQ language ('simply reviewing a medication list does NOT constitute prescription drug management'; the clinician, not a coder, decides stable vs worsening) is the core of a 99214 defense; (3) payer-by-payer ladders and deadlines were confirmed from each payer's current manual: Anthem KY commercial reconsideration = 2 years from EOP then 90-day appeal; Anthem MA = 120 days then 30 days; UHC/UMR = 12 months total for both steps then 60-day Notice of Dispute; Cigna 180 days; Aetna 180/60; Humana MA non-par 65 days with WOL; Medicare FFS 120/180/60/60 days with ALJ AIC $200 in 2026; FEP 6 months to carrier then 90 days to OPM with member written consent; (4) CMS's MA guidance explicitly names 'Downcoding' and 'rate of payment ... lower level of care' as appealable for non-contracted providers, while contracted MA disputes run under the contract; (5) Kentucky KRS 304.17A-708(1) bars insurers from requiring an appeal when a claim is not paid at the contracted rate (30-day correction), and 304.17A-730 imposes automatic 12/18/21% interest; no Kentucky downcoding statute exists, but Indiana (HB 1271, 2026) and Illinois (SB 3114) now require notice, batching and no AI-only downcoding, usable as persuasive authority; (6) AMA's downcoding guide supplies remit signatures (CARC 150/186, RARC M85/N610, 'paid at lower rate without code change'), a sample appeal letter, and the removal-from-program playbook (appeal inclusion, submit several correctly coded claims, go through the provider rep); (7) a reusable template outline and KPI set are provided; (8) CY2027 Medicare will pay same-day E/M with 0-day-global procedures at 80%, which the engine must not misread as downcoding. Gaps: Cigna R49 policy text, Humana participating-provider deadlines, Anthem's E/M review criteria, and ODA's own contract dispute clauses.

### [UNVERIFIED] Office/outpatient E/M level (99202-99205, 99212-99215) is selected by medical decision making, where 2 of the 3 MDM elements must be met or exceeded, or by total time; the AMA MDM table defines exactly what separates Low (99203/99213) from Moderate (99204/99214) and High (99205/99215).

AMA 'Table 2 - CPT E/M Office Revisions, Level of Medical Decision Making (MDM)', revisions effective January 1, 2021 ('Based on 2 out of 3 Elements of MDM'). LOW (99203/99213): Problems = '2 or more self-limited or minor problems; or 1 stable chronic illness; or 1 acute, uncomplicated illness or injury'. Data = Limited, 1 of 2 categories (Cat 1: any combination of 2 of review of prior external note(s) from each unique source / review of the result(s) of each unique test / ordering of each unique test; Cat 2: assessment requiring an independent historian). Risk = 'Low risk of morbidity from additional diagnostic testing or treatment'. MODERATE (99204/99214): Problems = '1 or more chronic illnesses with exacerbation, progression, or side effects of treatment; or 2 or more stable chronic illnesses; or 1 undiagnosed new problem with uncertain prognosis; or 1 acute illness with systemic symptoms; or 1 acute complicated injury'. Data = Moderate, 1 of 3 categories (Cat 1: any combination of 3 of external notes per unique source / result of each unique test / ordering each unique test / independent historian; Cat 2: independent interpretation of a test performed by another physician (not separately reported); Cat 3: discussion of management or test interpretation with external physician/QHP). Risk = 'Moderate risk of morbidity ... Examples only: Prescription drug management; Decision regarding minor surgery with identified patient or procedure risk factors; Decision regarding elective major surgery without identified patient or procedure risk factors; Diagnosis or treatment significantly limited by social determinants of health'. HIGH (99205/99215): Problems = '1 or more chronic illnesses with severe exacerbation, progression, or side effects of treatment; or 1 acute or chronic illness or injury that poses a threat to life or bodily function'; Data = Extensive, 2 of 3 categories; Risk examples: 'Drug therapy requiring intensive monitoring for toxicity; Decision regarding elective major surgery with identified patient or procedure risk factors; Decision regarding emergency major surgery; Decision regarding hospitalization; Decision not to resuscitate'. STRAIGHTFORWARD (99202/99212): 1 self-limited or minor problem; minimal or no data; minimal risk. Footnote: 'Each unique test, order, or document contributes to the combination of 2 or combination of 3 in Category 1'. UHC's 2026 commercial E/M reimbursement policy (2026R5007A) restates the rule: 'When determining the level of MDM, two of the three elements for that level must be met or exceeded.' The AMA downcoding guide reproduces the same table alongside CMS documentation recommendations.

**What it means for ODA:** Encode the table as a rules engine: for each downcoded E/M line, ingest the EMA note's structured elements (problems with status flags, data items, risk items) and compute the supportable level; the appeal generator then prints an element-by-element MDM argument ('Problems: moderate because ...; Risk: moderate because prescription drug management of ...; therefore 2 of 3 elements = moderate = 99214') and attaches this table page as Exhibit A.

Sources: [ama-assn.org](https://www.ama-assn.org/system/files/2019-06/cpt-revised-mdm-grid.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/comm-reimbursement/COMM-Evaluation-and-Management-Policy.pdf) · AMA PDF 'Payer evaluation and management (E/M) downcoding programs: What you need to know' (AMA, Oct 2022, doc 22-761463) read locally at <scratchpad> ama-assn.org hosting URL not re-verified

### [UNVERIFIED] Time is an independent second basis for the level: 2021-2023 descriptors used ranges (99212 10-19, 99213 20-29, 99214 30-39, 99215 40-54; 99202 15-29, 99203 30-44, 99204 45-59, 99205 60-74 min), and the CPT 2024 code set removed the ranges so each office code now carries a single minimum total time that must be met or exceeded (10/20/30/40 and 15/30/45/60 minutes).

AMA press release (Sep 8, 2023) on the CPT 2024 code set lists 'Removal of time ranges from office or other outpatient visit codes (99202-99205, 99212-99215)' with format aligned to other E/M codes. AAPC's current 99214 code summary: 'a moderate level of medical decision making, and/or the provider spends 30 minutes or more of total time on the encounter on a single date.' The AMA downcoding guide (2022) table gives the pre-2024 ranges listed in the claim; their lower bounds equal the 2024 minimums. What counts (AMA CPT webinar FAQ): total time 'includes both the face-to-face time with the patient and/or family/caregiver and non-face-to-face time personally spent by the physician and/or other qualified health care professional(s) on the day of the encounter ... regardless of the location ... It does not include any time spent in the performance of other separately reported service(s)'; clinical staff time is excluded. AMA guide 'Include and Document': preparing to see the patient, reviewing history, exam, counseling, communicating with other professionals/referrals, documenting, interpreting and communicating results (not separately reported), care coordination; 'Do NOT include': other calendar days, separately reported services, time ordering tests whose interpretation is separately reported, unrelated conversation, travel, clinical staff time, general teaching. UHC policy Q&A 4: a visit meeting 'the time requirement of 24 minutes for code 99213' may be coded on time even if MDM is not met. CMS MLN006764 (May 2026) still uses 99215 = 40-54 and 99205 = 60-74 minutes as the base for Medicare prolonged code G2212 (Table 3).

**What it means for ODA:** Pull documented total time from the EMA encounter export (field to confirm). Detection/triage rule: if a downcoded line's note documents total time >= the billed code's minimum (99214 >= 30, 99204 >= 45, 99215 >= 40, 99205 >= 60), tag it 'time-supported' and appeal first on time (payer MDM algorithms cannot rebut documented time); if time is absent, make time capture a pre-bill prompt for levels 4-5.

Sources: [ama-assn.org](https://www.ama-assn.org/press-center/ama-press-releases/ama-releases-cpt-2024-code-set) · [aapc.com](https://www.aapc.com/codes/cpt-codes/99214) · [ama-assn.org](https://www.ama-assn.org/system/files/cpt-e-m-advancing-landmark-webinar-faq.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/comm-reimbursement/COMM-Evaluation-and-Management-Policy.pdf) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/Outreach-and-Education/Medicare-Learning-Network-MLN/MLNProducts/Downloads/eval-mgmt-serv-guide-ICN006764.pdf) · AMA downcoding guide PDF (local copy, see finding 1)

### [UNVERIFIED] The AMA's own clarifications decide most 99213-vs-99214 disputes: merely reviewing a medication list is not prescription drug management, the treating clinician (not a coder or an algorithm) decides whether a chronic condition is stable or worsening, and risk must be assessed and documented for the specific patient; applied to dermatology, these rules identify which common visit patterns legitimately reach 99214/99204.

AMA CPT webinar FAQ (answers by AMA CPT staff, reviewed by CPT Editorial Panel members): 'There is no "blanket" guidance for services to represent specific levels of risk. The physician is responsible for assessing (and documenting) the level of risk of the services to be performed including medicine management, (prescription or OTC), based on a specific patient's risk factors and the risks typically seen with the drug. For example, an NSAID in a person with kidney disease or on anticoagulant is of greater concern that most prescription drugs. Simply reviewing a medication list does NOT constitute prescription drug management.' AMA E/M revisions FAQ page: 'A problem is addressed or managed when it is evaluated and/or treated at the encounter'; the clinician 'is expected to determine whether the medical problem or illness is stable or worsening' and a coder should not decide; 'A unique test is defined by the CPT code set'; 'Review of all materials from any unique source counts as one element toward MDM'; 'Risk for any procedure depends on the specific patient-risk factors and circumstances' and the physician 'should document whether it is considered a' major or minor surgery. Derived dermatology applications of the table (not from a derm-specific published source; verify against the AAD member E/M tool): (a) 99214 - psoriasis stable on a biologic plus acne stable on spironolactone = '2 or more stable chronic illnesses' (moderate problems) plus prescription drug management (moderate risk) = 2 of 3 moderate; (b) 99214 - atopic dermatitis flare despite therapy with a new systemic or high-potency prescription = chronic illness with exacerbation plus prescription drug management; (c) 99204 - new patient with a changing pigmented lesion = '1 undiagnosed new problem with uncertain prognosis' plus decision regarding minor surgery (biopsy) with an identified patient risk factor (e.g., anticoagulation) documented; (d) 99215 - isotretinoin, methotrexate or other 'drug therapy requiring intensive monitoring for toxicity' with lab review when problems are also high/moderate; (e) a single stable chronic illness with a refill and no data is only 99213 even though the refill is prescription drug management, because problems are low and data minimal (2 of 3 = low) - a frequent legitimate payer position.

**What it means for ODA:** The appeal generator should quote these AMA sentences verbatim whenever the payer's stated rationale is diagnosis-based ('diagnosis does not support level'); EMA note templates should force an explicit status word per chronic skin disease (stable/worsening/side effect), name the drug managed and the patient-specific risk, and record identified risk factors for same-day minor procedures - the three elements most often missing when a 99214 is downcoded.

Sources: [ama-assn.org](https://www.ama-assn.org/system/files/cpt-e-m-advancing-landmark-webinar-faq.pdf) · [ama-assn.org](https://www.ama-assn.org/practice-management/cpt/cpt-evaluation-and-management-em-revisions-faqs) · [ama-assn.org](https://www.ama-assn.org/system/files/2019-06/cpt-revised-mdm-grid.pdf)

### [UNVERIFIED] ODA's two largest commercial payers publish the policy language under which they downcode: Anthem's 2026 Kentucky Commercial Provider Manual authorizes prepayment review of E&M claims and adjustment 'to reflect the lower E&M level supported by the Claim', and UHC's 2026 E/M policy states levels may be adjusted when documentation does not support them, that cloned documentation is unacceptable, and that records are requested when a provider's pattern deviates from peers.

Anthem KY Commercial Provider Manual (effective Sept 1, 2026), 'Evaluation and Management (E&M) Services': 'Prior to payment, Anthem may review E&M Claims to determine, in accordance with correct coding requirements and/or reimbursement policy as applicable, whether the E&M code level submitted is higher than the E&M code level supported on the Claim. If the E&M code level submitted is higher ... Anthem reserves the right to: Deny the Claim and request resubmission of the Claim with the appropriate E&M level; Pend the Claim and request that the Facility or Provider submit documentation supporting the E&M level billed; and/or Adjust reimbursement to reflect the lower E&M level supported by the Claim.' UHC Evaluation and Management (E/M) Policy, Professional, 2026R5007A (Commercial and Individual Exchange; applies to network and non-network): 'Providers may experience adjustments to, or denials of the office visit or other outpatient E/M code ... if the documentation does not support the E/M level submitted. The provider may resubmit the claim with a revised E/M code for denied claims.' Q2: 'UnitedHealthcare may request medical records when the data indicates a physician or Other Qualified Health Care Professional has a billing pattern that deviates significantly from their peers, or claim attributes indicate possible billing errors.' Q3: documentation 'that appears to be cloned (selected information from one source and replicated in another location by copy-paste methods) ... including but not limited to history of present illness (HPC), exam, and MDM, would not be acceptable documentation to support the claim as billed.' Note: 'A providers' level of care escalation data and parenteral narcotic use data may be compared to their historical data and to peer data.' Also: interpretation of a separately billed test cannot count toward MDM; UHC uses CMS global-package definitions (000/010 = minor, 090 = major surgery). CMS documentation recommendations compiled in the AMA guide: document an assessment/clinical impression per problem; initiation of or changes in treatment including medications; 'Worsening problems or problems that are failing to change as expected'; referrals/consults; 'Notes on the MDM process (not just the decision)'; secondary diagnoses that impact MDM; comorbidities that increase risk; the type and specific procedure if performed at the E/M.

**What it means for ODA:** Anthem KY's language ('supported on the Claim') permits claim-data-only downcoding without records - the exact practice AMA policy opposes and the Indiana/Illinois laws now ban - so every Anthem appeal should demand the specific 'correct coding requirement and/or reimbursement policy' relied on and the record review performed. For UHC-family claims, add a pre-bill cloned-note similarity check (HPI/exam/MDM text reuse across visits) because UHC states cloned text will not support the level.

Sources: [anthem.com](https://www.anthem.com/content/dam/digital/docs/provider/commercial/manuals/PM_KY_00011.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/comm-reimbursement/COMM-Evaluation-and-Management-Policy.pdf) · AMA downcoding guide PDF (local copy, see finding 1)

### [UNVERIFIED] Downcoding is detectable in the 835 only with specific rules: AMA lists the remittance signatures (CARC 150, CARC 186, RARC M85, RARC N610) and warns that some payers pay the lower rate without changing the billed code, so payment must be compared to the expected allowed amount per code; Cigna's October-2025 'R49' E/M program and Maryland's March-2026 enforcement against Cigna confirm the practice is active and contestable.

AMA downcoding guide, 'Recognizing when claims are being downcoded': 'certain payers even pay at the adjusted lower rate without changing the billed code, making it extremely difficult to identify in coding audits'; example remark codes: 'CO150: Payer deems the information submitted does not support this level of service'; 'M85: Subjected to review of physician evaluation and management services'; 'N610 Alert: Payment based on appropriate level of care'; 'CARC 186: Level of care change adjustment'; 'staff may need to review payment details, comparing the payment for each billed CPT code to the anticipated amount'; 'Pay special attention to claims indicating a high level of service (e.g., CPT 99204, 99205, 99214, and 99215) and complex claims, including those using modifier 25'; identifying whether a payer downcodes 'consistently, may help reveal whether you are subject to a payer's prepayment review program'. Example payer policy text quoted by AMA: 'we may adjust your payment if the claim detail doesn't support the billed level of service. We will not change the procedure code you bill.' Dermatology Times (June 23, 2026): 'Cigna introduced R49 in October 2025, and downcoding appeared across the authors' practices within weeks'; 'Thirteen major payers' run automated E/M programs; Maryland fined Cigna $80,000 in March 2026 and ordered it to stop automatic downcoding; practices noticed checks '$20 to $30 short'. AMA March 27, 2026 State Advocacy Update: the Maryland Insurance Administration found that Cigna improperly downcoded E/M claims, imposed an administrative penalty, and ordered the plan to stop.

**What it means for ODA:** Detection rules for the parser: (1) SVC06 present with a lower office E/M code in SVC01 (the 116 claims already found); (2) CAS CO/PI 150 or 186 on an E/M line; (3) LQ HE M85 or N610; (4) 'silent' downcode: SVC01 unchanged but paid/allowed on 99214 or 99204 equals the payer's known allowed for 99213/99203 within a tolerance (e.g., BCBS 99214 $63.89 vs 99213 $51.00; Medicare KY 99213 $70.81-$83.30) - requires a payer/code/year allowed-amount reference built from ODA's own clean lines; (5) CO-45 larger than the contract implies. Report by payer and provider, with a 'program entry' alert when a payer's downcode rate for one provider jumps.

Sources: AMA downcoding guide PDF (local copy, see finding 1) · [dermatologytimes.com](https://www.dermatologytimes.com/view/the-new-economics-of-dermatology-claims-understanding-and-responding-to-rising-denials) · [ama-assn.org](https://www.ama-assn.org/health-care-advocacy/advocacy-update/march-27-2026-state-advocacy-update)

### [UNVERIFIED] Anthem Blue Cross and Blue Shield Kentucky (commercial): Step 1 Claim Payment Reconsideration may be filed for 'a minimum of two (2) years from the issue date of the EOP'; Step 2 Claim Payment Appeal must be filed within 90 days of the reconsideration determination; both steps are prerequisites to contract dispute resolution/arbitration. Anthem Medicare Advantage: reconsideration within 120 calendar days of the EOP, appeal within 30 calendar days of the reconsideration letter; non-participating MA providers appeal within 65 days with a Waiver of Liability.

Anthem KY Commercial Provider Manual (eff. Sept 1, 2026), 'Claim Payment Disputes': dispute reasons include 'Disagreements over reduced or zero-paid Claims' and 'Claim code editing issues'; 'Claim Payment Reconsiderations can be submitted via phone, Availity Essentials, or in writing. Providers and Facilities have a minimum of two (2) years from the issue date of the EOP, unless otherwise required by State law (or such time period set forth in the Provider or Facility Agreement'; only one reconsideration per claim; 'Providers and Facilities must submit Claims Payment Appeals within ninety (90) days from the date of the determination of the Claims Payment Reconsideration'; untimely requests are 'deemed ineligible' and the member may not be billed. Required documentation: 'position statement explaining the nature of the dispute'; provider name/address/phone/email and NPI or TIN; member name and Anthem ID; 'A listing of disputed Claims, which should include the Anthem Claim number and the date(s) of service(s)'; all supporting documentation. Submit online through Availity (Claims & Payments > Claim Status > 'Dispute the Claim'), or mail with the Claim Payment Appeal Form or Reconsideration Form to Anthem Blue Cross and Blue Shield, Payment Dispute Unit, P.O. Box 105557, Atlanta, GA 30348-5557, or call the number on the member ID card. Clinical/medical-necessity denials use the separate Clinical Appeals process. Anthem Medicare Advantage Provider Guidebook (Jan 1, 2027): 'We accept reconsideration requests in writing, verbally and through our provider website within 120 calendar days from the date on the Explanation of Payment (EOP)'; plan aims to resolve within 45 calendar days; determination letter must cite 'applicable statutes, regulations, policies, claims, codes, or provider manual references'; 'We accept claim payment appeals through our provider website or in writing within 30 calendar days of the date on the reconsideration determination letter', resolved within 30 days; KY mail: Anthem Blue Cross and Blue Shield, ATTN: Medicare Payment Dispute Unit, P.O. Box 105187, Atlanta, GA 30348-5187. Section 7.2: nonparticipating providers 'must request a reconsideration of the denial within 65 calendar days from the remittance notification' with a signed Waiver of Liability Statement (CMS model form), mailed to Medicare Complaints, Appeals and Grievances, Attention: Non-Contracted Provider Appeals, Mailstop OH0205-A537, 4361 Irwin Simpson Rd, Mason, Ohio 45040; Section 7.1: nonparticipating providers who contend the plan paid 'for a different service from the one originally billed' may use the payment dispute resolution process.

**What it means for ODA:** Anthem commercial's two-year window means every Anthem/BCBS commercial downcode since January 2024 in ODA's files is still disputable today; the dashboard should compute days-to-deadline from the EOP issue date (835 BPR16/CLP data) per payer rule and sort the backlog by expiring value. Anthem MA (BCBS Medicare Advantage) at 120 days requires the weekly job to file within one cycle.

Sources: [anthem.com](https://www.anthem.com/content/dam/digital/docs/provider/commercial/manuals/PM_KY_00011.pdf) · [anthem.com](https://www.anthem.com/content/dam/digital/docs/provider/commercial/manuals/ABSCRPM-0010-20.pdf) · [providers.anthem.com](https://providers.anthem.com/kentucky-provider/claims/claims-submissions-and-disputes) · [anthem.com](https://www.anthem.com/ky/provider/individual-commercial/policies/manuals)

### [UNVERIFIED] UnitedHealthcare (commercial, Individual Exchange, Medicare Advantage, and by supplement UMR/UHSS): reconsideration and appeal must both be completed within 12 months of the original EOB/PRA date; 20 or more reconsiderations with the same administrative issue can be filed in bulk; after an upheld appeal a contractual Notice of Dispute must be sent within 60 days with specified claim data or the right to dispute is waived; MA non-contracted payment disputes go to the CMS PDR process within 120 days.

UHC 2026 Care Provider Administrative Guide, Chapter 10 'Claim reconsideration and appeals process': 'You must submit your reconsideration and appeal to us within 12 months from the date of the original claim EOB or PRA (or as required by law or your Agreement)'; 'The 2-step process ... allows a total of 12 months for timely submission for both steps (Step 1: Reconsideration and Step 2: Appeals)'; appeals must 'Attach all new supporting materials ... Please do not include any materials that you sent to us as part of the reconsideration request'; '20 or more claim reconsiderations (research request) ... for the same administrative issue (and attachments are not required), you may submit these in bulk online. Go to UHCprovider.com > Sign In > Claims & Payments > Claims Research Project'; 'If you believe there was an error with what we reimbursed, you must complete the claim reconsideration and appeal process ... before you can initiate the dispute resolution process set forth in your Agreement ... a Notice of Dispute must be sent no later than 60 days after the date of our decision on your appeal' and must include group number, subscriber number, patient name and DOB, provider TIN, first/last DOS, total billed charges, units, 'UnitedHealthcare payment received', 'Patient responsibility received', 'Contractual reimbursement expected', 'Balance due (variance due)', patient account number, 'Detailed issue description with the specific basis for the challenge', CPT/HCPCS code, claim/call reference number, reconsideration and appeal filed dates and reference numbers; 'Failure to timely comply ... will result in the waiver of your right to initiate dispute resolution'. Medicare member appeals: 'within 60 calendar days of the denial'. Capitation/delegation supplement: 'A noncontracted health care provider can use the CMS noncontracted health care provider Payment Dispute Resolution (PDR) process for any decision where they contend the amount paid ... is less than the amount that would have been paid under Original Medicare ... The health care provider must submit a payment dispute within 120 calendar days from the date of the original claim determination.' The guide covers 'UMR and UHSS' through a supplement; UMR's provider site: 'Easily appeal denied claims for reconsideration on the secure portal.' UHC's appeals page: digital submission required for most network professionals; 'Timelines vary. Please refer to your Participation Agreement.'

**What it means for ODA:** UHC-family downcodes older than 12 months from the PRA date (most of 2024 and early 2025) are likely unrecoverable except through the contract dispute clause; the engine should hard-prioritize UHC/UMR/Optum/UHSS lines inside the window, auto-build the Notice of Dispute data block (the 'variance due' field is exactly expected-allowed minus paid), and group identical-issue claims into Claims Research Project batches of 20+ (e.g., all 99214-with-modifier-25 B10 denials).

Sources: [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/admin-guides/2026-UHC-Administrative-Guide.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](http://www.uhcprovider.com/en/claims-payments-billing/appeals.html) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/en/admin-guides.html) · [umr.com](https://www.umr.com/provider)

### [UNVERIFIED] Cigna: appeal within 180 calendar days of the initial payment or denial notice (multi-patient fee-schedule disputes do not require individual appeals); Aetna: reconsideration within 180 days, appeal within 60 days of the reconsideration decision (65 days for Medicare non-contracted); Humana: MA non-participating appeals within 65 calendar days with a Waiver of Liability, commercial appeals on the member's behalf within the policy's limits; Humana participating-provider deadlines sit in its provider manual (not retrieved).

Cigna appeals page: 'Submit the appeal within 180 calendar days of the date of the initial payment' or denial notice, or 'the date of the last payment adjustment'; complete the 'Request for Health Care Provider Payment Review' form; include original EOB/EOP and records; mail to Cigna Healthcare National Appeals Unit, PO Box 188011 (PO Box 188062 for GWH-Cigna/'G' cards), Chattanooga, TN 37422; 'The review will be completed in 60 days' and outcome notified 'within 75 business days of receipt'; arbitration must be requested within one year of the final internal decision; customers cannot be billed for amounts lost to a missed deadline. The form (RequestForHealthCareProfessionalPaymentReview.pdf) says: 'Fee schedule or reimbursement terms for multiple patients do not require individual appeals'; Step 1 call Customer Service to review 'adverse determinations/payment reductions'; appeal 'within 180 days and allow 60 days for processing'; reason checkboxes include 'Modifier reimbursement', 'Your Cigna contract and the fee schedule or reimbursement terms', 'Mutually exclusive, incidental, or bundling procedure code denial', 'Other'; fields for claim number/DCN, 'Original Claim Amount Billed' and 'Original Claim Amount Paid'. Aetna disputes page: 'File your reconsideration within 180 calendar days of the initial claim decision'; 'File your appeal within 60 calendar days of the reconsideration decision'; '180 days for Medicare and commercial or 65 days for Medicare non-contracted providers' for issues not eligible for reconsideration; responses within 30 business days (reconsideration) and 60 calendar days (appeal); Availity or mail (Aetna Provider Resolution Team, PO Box 14020, Lexington, KY 40512). Humana reconsiderations/appeals page: 'Medicare Advantage plans: appeals for nonparticipating providers - To request an appeal of a denied claim, you need to submit your request in writing, via Availity Essentials or mail, within 65 calendar days from the date of the denial' with the original claim, remittance, clinical records and 'a signed Waiver of Liability form holding the enrollee harmless', to Humana Inc., P.O. Box 14165, Lexington, KY 40512-4165; 'Commercial plans: appeals for all healthcare providers - ... you may file an appeal on behalf of the covered person with authorization ... within the time limits set forth in the medical insurance policy ... If filing on your own behalf ... within the time frame established by applicable state law', to Humana Grievances and Appeals, P.O. Box 14546, Lexington, KY 40512-4546; 'Participating providers may find the reconsideration processes in the provider manuals'; Availity 'Dispute Claim' button adds the claim to an appeals worklist.

**What it means for ODA:** Maintain a payer-rule table {payer family -> level, clock start (EOB/PRA date vs decision date), days, channel, mailing address, form} and compute a deadline for every flagged line; Cigna's own form supports a single consolidated 'fee schedule/reimbursement terms' dispute for many patients, and its 'Modifier reimbursement' box is the right vehicle for the modifier-25 B10/$0 pattern seen in ODA's UMR/Cigna-type remits.

Sources: [cigna.com](https://www.cigna.com/health-care-providers/coverage-and-claims/appeals-disputes) · [static.cigna.com](https://static.cigna.com/assets/chcp/pdf/resourceLibrary/medical/RequestForHealthCareProfessionalPaymentReview.pdf) · [aetna.com](https://www.aetna.com/health-care-professionals/disputes-appeals/disputes-appeals-overview.html) · [provider.humana.com](https://provider.humana.com/coverage-claims/payment-integrity/reconsiderations-appeals)

### [UNVERIFIED] Medicare FFS (CGS J15 Kentucky; same federal rules for J8 Indiana): redetermination within 120 days of receipt of the initial determination (receipt presumed 5 days after the notice), QIC reconsideration within 180 days, ALJ within 60 days with a $200 amount in controversy in 2026 (and 2027), Council within 60 days, judicial review at $1,960 (2026); claims with 'common issues of law and fact' may be aggregated to reach the AIC; clerical errors (including a wrong modifier or transposed codes) must go through a reopening within one year, not an appeal.

42 CFR 405.942(a): 'any request for redetermination must be filed within 120 calendar days from the date a party receives the notice of the initial determination ... presumed to be 5 calendar days after the date of the notice'; good-cause extension in writing. CGS level 1 page: 'CGS has 60 days to process an appeal'; use the 'CGS Jurisdiction 15 Redetermination Request Form'; myCGS portal preferred. CGS Reopening vs Redetermination Job Aid (rev. Dec 16, 2025): 'Redeterminations do not accept faxed request'; each request requires beneficiary name, Medicare identifier, date of service, service appealed or ICN, requestor name; 'A Reopening is a process used to correct minor errors or omissions' such as 'Transposed procedure or diagnostic codes', 'Incorrect data items, use of a modifier, or date of service', 'Misapplication of a fee schedule'; 'A reopening must be requested within one year from the date of the initial determination'. CMS Pub 100-04 ch. 29: 'requests for adjustments to claims resulting from clerical errors must be handled and processed as reopenings'. 42 CFR 405.962: reconsideration 'within 180 calendar days from the date the party receives the notice of the redetermination' (QIC Part B North, C2C Innovative Solutions; CGS: 'do not send 2nd level appeals to CGS'). 42 CFR 405.1002: ALJ request 'within 60 calendar days after receipt of the notice of the QIC's reconsideration'. 42 CFR 405.1006(a)(1): aggregation allowed where claims 'are denied, or payment is reduced, for similar reasons and arise from a similar fact pattern'; AIC = amount charged minus Medicare payments and deductible/coinsurance. CGS level 3 page: ALJ AIC '$200 for requests filed on or before December 31, 2026' and '$200 for requests filed on or after January 1, 2027'; judicial review '$1,960' (2026) / '$2,000' (2027); Council review within 60 days (42 CFR 405.1102). CGS redetermination checklist for E/M: 'Office notes', 'Progress notes', and for 'Same group/multiple physicians - History/physical and progress notes from both physicians'. CGS 'when to file': claims adjusted causing an overpayment 'may be appealed with the supporting documentation'; return-reject (MA130) claims must be resubmitted, not appealed.

**What it means for ODA:** For Medicare KY/IN lines, the engine should route by cause: true E/M level reductions or medical-necessity reductions -> redetermination packet (120-day clock from 835 date + 5 days) with office/progress notes; modifier or code-entry errors ODA itself made -> myCGS reopening within 1 year; many small E/M deltas sharing one cause can be aggregated to meet the $200 ALJ threshold. Verify in ODA data whether CGS ever changes SVC06->SVC01 on E/M (expected rare); most Medicare 'underpayment' will be MPPR (CARC 59) and bundling, which are not appealable fee-schedule outcomes.

Sources: [ecfr.gov](https://www.ecfr.gov/api/versioner/v1/full/2026-11-04/title-42.xml?part=405&section=405.942) · [ecfr.gov](https://www.ecfr.gov/api/versioner/v1/full/2026-11-04/title-42.xml?part=405&section=405.962) · [ecfr.gov](https://www.ecfr.gov/api/versioner/v1/full/2026-11-04/title-42.xml?part=405&section=405.1002) · [ecfr.gov](https://www.ecfr.gov/api/versioner/v1/full/2026-11-04/title-42.xml?part=405&section=405.1006) · [ecfr.gov](https://www.ecfr.gov/api/versioner/v1/full/2026-11-04/title-42.xml?part=405&section=405.1102) · [cgsmedicare.com](https://www.cgsmedicare.com/partb/appeals/level1.html) · [cgsmedicare.com](https://www.cgsmedicare.com/partb/appeals/level2.html) · [cgsmedicare.com](https://www.cgsmedicare.com/partb/appeals/level3.html) · [cgsmedicare.com](https://www.cgsmedicare.com/partb/appeals/when.html) · [cgsmedicare.com](https://www.cgsmedicare.com/partb/forms/pdf/reopen_vs_redet_jobaid.pdf)

### [UNVERIFIED] Medicare Advantage: for contracted providers, payment disputes (including downcoding) are governed solely by the provider contract, not by CMS appeal rules; for non-contracted providers, CMS's July 2026 guidance explicitly lists 'Downcoding' and 'Level of care or rate of payment denials ... the plan reimburses based on a lower level of care' as appealable organization determinations, filed within 65 calendar days of the remittance with a Waiver of Liability, decided within 60 days, and auto-forwarded to the Independent Review Entity if not fully favorable.

CMS 'Parts C & D Enrollee Grievances, Organization/Coverage Determinations, and Appeals Guidance', effective July 6, 2026: 'No party may appeal a contract provider payment denial when the enrollee has no further financial liability. Contract provider disputes involving plan payment denials are governed by the appeals/dispute resolution provisions in the contract between the provider and the plan.' Section 50.1.1: 'A non-contract provider, on his or her own behalf, may request a reconsideration (i.e., an appeal) for a denied claim only if the non-contract provider completes a Waiver of Liability (WOL) statement'; 'Plans must issue a substantive reconsideration decision within 60 days ... from when the WOL is received'; 'Even reconsideration requests submitted by non-contracted providers that relate to the type or level of service furnished to the enrollee must be reviewed in accordance with the administrative appeal processes'; examples that 'must be afforded full administrative appeals rights' include 'Downcoding', 'Bundling issues and disputed rate of payment ... denial of procedure codes -- as mutually exclusive to another paid procedure code, or due to inclusion in a previously paid global surgical package', and 'Level of care or rate of payment denials. Payment of a reduced fee schedule amount for a course of treatment. For example, a provider bills a procedure code for a visit but the plan reimburses based on a lower level of care'; 'a non-contracted provider does not need to receive zero payment to request a reconsideration'. Non-contract provider denial notices must state the provider 'Has 65 calendar days from the remittance notification date to request a reconsideration'. Prompt pay: 'The MA plan must pay 95 percent of clean claims within 30 calendar days ... All other claims submitted by non-contract providers ... within 60 calendar days' (42 CFR 422.520); contract providers per contract. 42 CFR 422.590(b): payment reconsiderations decided and, if adverse, sent to the IRE 'no later than 60 calendar days'. 42 CFR 422.582(b) text still reads '60 calendar days after receipt' with receipt presumed 5 days after the notice; CMS guidance and plan notices implement 65 days from the notice date. CMS: 'effective May 1, 2026, C2C Innovative Solutions, Inc. is the Part C IRE'. CMS flow chart (CY2026): 65 days to file level 1; IRE; ALJ 60 days, AIC >= $200; judicial AIC >= $1,960.

**What it means for ODA:** The dashboard needs ODA's network status per MA plan (Humana MA, UHC MA, BCBS MA) - missing data. In-network: use the payer manual timelines (finding 6-8) and the contract's dispute clause; out-of-network: every downcode is a formal Subpart M appeal with WOL, a 60-day plan clock and automatic IRE review, and the appeal letter should quote CMS's own 'Downcoding' and 'lower level of care' examples.

Sources: [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/medicare/appeals-and-grievances/mmcag/downloads/parts-c-and-d-enrollee-grievances-organization-coverage-determinations-and-appeals-guidance.pdf) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/medicare/appeals-grievances/managed-care) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/medicare/appeals-grievances/managed-care/reconsideration-advantage-health-plan-part-c) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/medicare/appeals-and-grievances/mmcag/downloads/managed-care-appeals-flow-chart-.pdf) · [ecfr.gov](https://www.ecfr.gov/api/versioner/v1/full/2026-11-04/title-42.xml?part=422&section=422.582) · [ecfr.gov](https://www.ecfr.gov/api/versioner/v1/full/2026-11-04/title-42.xml?part=422&section=422.590)

### [UNVERIFIED] BCBS Federal Employee Program (Anthem FEP in Kentucky): the OPM disputed-claims process requires a written reconsideration request to the carrier within 6 months of the denial, a 30-day carrier response, then OPM review within 90 days of the carrier's affirmation (120 days if the carrier fails to respond), and a provider may act only with the member's specific written consent.

5 CFR 890.105(a)(2): the section 'applies to covered individuals and to other individuals or entities who are acting on the behalf of a covered individual and who have the covered individual's specific written consent to pursue payment of the disputed claim'. (b)(1): 'The covered individual has 6 months from the date of the notice ... that a claim (or a portion of a claim) was denied by the carrier in which to submit a written request for reconsideration'. (b)(2): carrier has 30 days to affirm in writing, pay, or request information (then 30 days after receipt; if the covered individual or provider does not respond within 60 days the carrier decides on the evidence it has). (c)(1): the request must 'give the reasons, in terms of applicable brochure provisions, that the denied claim should have been approved'. 2026 Blue Cross and Blue Shield Service Benefit Plan brochure, Section 8 (pp. 138-140): Step 1 'Write to us within 6 months from the date of our decision' with reasons 'based on specific benefit provisions in this brochure' and supporting documents; Step 2 'we have 30 days'; Step 3 'You must write to OPM within: 90 days after the date of our letter upholding our initial decision; or 120 days after you first wrote to us - if we did not answer ... within 30 days'; OPM address: U.S. Office of Personnel Management, Healthcare and Insurance, Federal Employees Insurance Operations, FEHB 1, 1900 E Street NW, Room 3425, Washington, DC 20415-3610; 'You are the only person who has a right to file a disputed claim with OPM. Parties acting as your representative, such as medical providers, must include a copy of your specific written consent'; OPM decides or reports status within 60 days; 'There are no other administrative appeals'; lawsuit against OPM in Federal court 'by December 31 of the third year after the year in which you received the disputed services'.

**What it means for ODA:** FEP lines (ODA's Anthem FEP remits with CO-45 plus PR-3 copays) cannot be appealed by the practice alone: capture a standing written consent/authorization from FEP patients at registration, generate the member-signed reconsideration letter citing the brochure's office-visit benefit, and track the 6-month and 90-day clocks separately from Anthem commercial rules; the local Blue Plan (Anthem KY) processes Step 1.

Sources: [ecfr.gov](https://www.ecfr.gov/api/versioner/v1/full/2026-11-04/title-5.xml?part=890&section=890.105) · [fepblue.org](https://www.fepblue.org/-/media/PDFs/Brochures/Standard-and-Basic-brochure-2026.pdf) · [fepblue.org](https://www.fepblue.org/plan-brochures)

### [UNVERIFIED] Kentucky law gives contracted-rate underpayments a non-appeal remedy and automatic interest: KRS 304.17A-708(1) says an insurer 'shall not require a provider to appeal errors in payment where the insurer has not paid the claim according to the contracted rate' and must correct within 30 days of documentation; KRS 304.17A-730 imposes 12%/18%/21% interest that the insurer must add without a request; KRS 304.17A-706 requires notice and 20-business-day completion of retrospective reviews with 12% interest; no Kentucky downcoding statute exists, but Indiana's 2026 law and Illinois' Transparency in Downcoding Act supply persuasive standards (notice, no diagnosis-only or AI-only downcoding, batching of appeals).

KRS 304.17A-702(1): insurer 'shall reimburse a provider for a clean claim or send a written or an electronic notice denying or contesting the claim within thirty (30) calendar days' (60 for organ transplants); (2)(b) pay the undisputed portion and state reasons for the rest. KRS 304.17A-730(1): interest 'on the amount of the claim that remains unpaid': 12% per annum if paid 1-30 days late, 18% if 31-60 days, 21% if more than 60 days, accruing from the date payment was due; (2) 'the insurer shall add the interest payable to the amount of the unpaid claim without the necessity for any claim for that interest to be made by the provider'. KRS 304.17A-706(1): a clean claim may be contested only for preexisting condition/COB/other-insurer, 'The insurer will conduct a retrospective review', fraud, or unpaid premium; (2)(c) for a retrospective review the insurer shall notify the provider 'within the claims payment time frame ... of the service that will be retrospectively reviewed and the specific information needed', 'Complete the retrospective review within twenty (20) business days of the insurer's receipt of the medical information', and add 12% interest (or the 304.17A-730 schedule); (3)(b) pay or deny within 30 days of receiving requested information. KRS 304.17A-708: (1) quoted in claim; 'Miscalculations in payments made by the insurer shall be corrected and paid within thirty (30) calendar days upon the insurer's receipt of documentation from the provider verifying the error'; (2) no duty to correct if requested more than 24 months after payment; (3) retroactive denials limited to 24 months and require a written statement of the basis. KRS 304.17A-714: overpayment notice within 24 months; provider has 30 calendar days to dispute; 'no recoupment shall be made until the dispute is resolved'; disputes completed within 30 days. KRS 304.17A-617: internal appeal of adverse benefit determinations 'may be initiated by the covered person, an authorized person, or a provider acting on behalf of the covered person'; decisions within 30 days; on request the reviewer must be 'a board-eligible or certified physician in the appropriate specialty'; 304.17A-621/-623 provide independent external review. The KRS Subtitle 17A section index (read Nov 2026) has no section titled for downcoding, claim edits or E/M. AMA March 27, 2026 update: Indiana's governor signed legislation (HB 1271) that 'requires plans to notify physicians when a claim is being downcoded', 'bars a plan from downcoding a claim based on a diagnosis alone', 'requires a clear appeals process that permits the batching of downcoded claims', bars automated tools including AI 'as the sole basis for downcoding a claim', and bars targeting physicians caring for complex patients; AMA June 18, 2026 update: Illinois passed SB 3114, the 'Transparency in Downcoding Act', to 'Prohibit a health insurer from using an automated process, system, or tool to downcode a claim', require notice with the clinical reason, and 'establish a clear and accessible process for disputing downcoded claims'; Virginia also acted and 'more than a dozen states' introduced bills; AMA has model legislation. Dermatology Times notes state DOI complaints apply to fully insured plans, not ERISA self-funded plans.

**What it means for ODA:** For Kentucky-regulated, fully insured plans (Anthem KY, Humana commercial, Cigna fully insured), letters should (a) frame a silent downcode as payment 'not according to the contracted rate' and demand correction within 30 days under 304.17A-708(1) without an appeal, (b) demand statutory interest from day 31 under 304.17A-730/-706, and (c) demand the retrospective-review notice 304.17A-706 requires; the dashboard should accrue interest per corrected line, flag lines approaching the 24-month limit, and record each plan's funding status (ERISA self-funded vs fully insured - missing data) to know when state law and a Kentucky DOI complaint apply; Kentucky advocacy (KMA/AADA) for an Indiana-style law is a strategic lever.

Sources: [apps.legislature.ky.gov](https://apps.legislature.ky.gov/law/statutes/statute.aspx?id=29329) · [apps.legislature.ky.gov](https://apps.legislature.ky.gov/law/statutes/statute.aspx?id=29344) · [apps.legislature.ky.gov](https://apps.legislature.ky.gov/law/statutes/statute.aspx?id=29332) · [apps.legislature.ky.gov](https://apps.legislature.ky.gov/law/statutes/statute.aspx?id=29333) · [apps.legislature.ky.gov](https://apps.legislature.ky.gov/law/statutes/statute.aspx?id=29336) · [apps.legislature.ky.gov](https://apps.legislature.ky.gov/law/statutes/statute.aspx?id=57343) · [apps.legislature.ky.gov](https://apps.legislature.ky.gov/law/statutes/chapter.aspx?id=38715) · [ama-assn.org](https://www.ama-assn.org/health-care-advocacy/advocacy-update/march-27-2026-state-advocacy-update) · [ama-assn.org](https://www.ama-assn.org/health-care-advocacy/advocacy-update/june-18-2026-state-advocacy-update) · [dermatologytimes.com](https://www.dermatologytimes.com/view/the-new-economics-of-dermatology-claims-understanding-and-responding-to-rising-denials)

### [UNVERIFIED] A winning appeal packet combines claim identifiers, the billed-vs-paid code and dollar variance, an element-by-element MDM (or time) argument quoting CPT guideline text, the payer's own policy, a demand for the specific edit/rule and record review relied on, state interest, and the clinical note with the assessment/plan highlighted; payers' own forms and manuals accept batched schedules of claims, so one letter can carry many claims. Reusable template outline with placeholders follows in detail.

AMA guide: 'Generally, your appeal will include: Patient identifying information; The relevant claim number and remittance advice; Specification of the line item you are appealing; The expected amount and the amount received and the billed and adjusted code; Any contract provisions that may be violated; Detailed explanation of the reason for appeal; Supporting clinical documentation'; keep current AMA/CMS E/M guidelines to attach; 'highlight the relevant assessment or plan in the clinical documentation'. AMA sample letter core: the payer 'has inappropriately downcoded the CPT E/M code submitted and changed the code to [new code], resulting in the inappropriate reduction of payment'; 'Downcoding of CPT E/M codes is not appropriate without review of medical record documentation'; AMA policy quote: 'The AMA vigorously opposes the practice of unilateral, arbitrary recoding and/or bundling by all payers.' Batching evidence: UHC Claims Research Project for 20+ same-issue reconsiderations; Cigna form: 'Fee schedule or reimbursement terms for multiple patients do not require individual appeals'; Anthem requires 'A listing of disputed Claims' with claim numbers and DOS; Medicare allows aggregation of claims with common issues (42 CFR 405.1006); Indiana HB 1271 mandates batching. TEMPLATE OUTLINE: (1) Header: [Date]; To: [Payer] [Appeals/Payment Dispute Unit address]; Via: [Availity/portal ticket #, mail]; Re: Claim Payment [Reconsideration/Appeal/Notice of Dispute] - inappropriate E/M downcoding; Provider: [Practice], TIN [ ], NPI [group/rendering], Contract # [ ]. (2) Claim schedule (attached table, one row per line): [Payer claim #/ICN], [Member ID], [Patient acct #], [DOS], [Rendering provider], [Billed CPT + modifiers], [Paid-as CPT (835 SVC01/SVC06)], [Billed charge], [Contracted/expected allowed], [Allowed/paid], [Variance due], [CARC/RARC on the line], [835 check/EFT trace # and date], [Reconsideration ref # and date, if appeal stage]. (3) Statement of the dispute: the remittance dated [ ] paid line [ ] at the rate for [lower code] although [billed code] was reported; no record request preceded the adjustment; the adjustment is a 'reduced ... Claim'/'claim code editing issue' under [payer manual section]. (4) Coding basis, per claim: Problems addressed = [list with status: exacerbation/stable chronic x2/undiagnosed new problem with uncertain prognosis] -> [level]; Data = [unique tests ordered/reviewed, external notes, independent historian/interpretation] -> [level]; Risk = [prescription drug management of DRUG with patient-specific risk; decision regarding minor surgery with identified risk factor X; drug therapy requiring intensive monitoring] -> [level]; 'Two of three elements meet or exceed [moderate/high]; CPT [year] E/M guidelines, MDM table (Exhibit A)'. Alternative/time basis: 'Total time on the date of the encounter documented as [N] minutes, which meets or exceeds the [30/45] minute threshold for [code] (Exhibit A).' (5) Authority: CPT E/M guidelines (attach table); AMA FAQ quotes (finding 3); payer's own E/M policy [cite: UHC 2026R5007A / Anthem KY manual 'E&M Services' / Cigna policy #]; AMA policy opposing unilateral recoding; for MA non-contracted: CMS Parts C&D guidance section 50.1.1 ('Downcoding'); for Kentucky-regulated plans: KRS 304.17A-708(1), -706(2)(c), -730. (6) Requests: reprocess each listed line at the billed code and pay [variance total]; pay statutory interest of [12/18/21]% from [due date]; identify in writing 'the specific edit, algorithm, policy or coding rationale relied on and whether the medical record was reviewed'; identify the reviewer's name, credentials and license; cease automated downcoding of this practice's claims absent record review and prior notice; confirm whether the practice is on any prepayment review/outlier list and the criteria for removal. (7) Reservation of rights: next level ([appeal within 90 days of determination / Notice of Dispute within 60 days / QIC / OPM / Kentucky DOI complaint / arbitration]). (8) Signature: [Physician or practice administrator], [phone/email]. Attachments: A) CPT MDM table and time thresholds; B) progress note(s) with assessment/plan and time highlighted; C) 835 remittance line(s) and EOB; D) superbill/claim (837 image); E) payer policy excerpt; F) member authorization or Waiver of Liability where required (FEP, MA non-contracted); G) prior correspondence and reference numbers.

**What it means for ODA:** Build the packet generator to emit one letter per payer per cycle with a machine-generated claim schedule (CSV and PDF), per-claim MDM/time blocks filled from EMA note data, the 835 line excerpt, and the payer-specific authority paragraph; store the payer's reference number and the deadline for the next level on each claim so the weekly job can escalate automatically.

Sources: AMA downcoding guide PDF (local copy, see finding 1) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/admin-guides/2026-UHC-Administrative-Guide.pdf) · [static.cigna.com](https://static.cigna.com/assets/chcp/pdf/resourceLibrary/medical/RequestForHealthCareProfessionalPaymentReview.pdf) · [anthem.com](https://www.anthem.com/content/dam/digital/docs/provider/commercial/manuals/PM_KY_00011.pdf) · [ecfr.gov](https://www.ecfr.gov/api/versioner/v1/full/2026-11-04/title-42.xml?part=405&section=405.1006) · [ama-assn.org](https://www.ama-assn.org/health-care-advocacy/advocacy-update/march-27-2026-state-advocacy-update)

### [UNVERIFIED] Getting off a payer's downcoding/prepayment-review list is itself an appeal: AMA advises appealing the practice's inclusion on the list through the provider representative and submitting documentation for several claims to demonstrate a pattern of correct coding; the entry criterion disclosed by UHC is peer/historical comparison of level-of-care escalation, and AMA's principles (outliers only, education first, record review before reduction, prior notification, written rationale, code changed when payment changes) define the demands to make. No payer in ODA's mix publishes an overturn-rate exemption threshold.

AMA guide section IV: 'Sometimes payers include physicians in global prepayment review programs, which expose all of the physician's claims to automatic downcoding. If you think you have been subject to such a program, you will need to appeal your inclusion on the payer's downcoding list. A good place to start is to reach out to the health plan's provider representative.
