To be removed from the program, you might need to submit documentation for several claims to demonstrate a pattern of correct coding.' AMA section VI principles: 'Outliers only: ... Any downcoding program should only target true outlier physicians whose coding patterns differ significantly from that of their same-specialty peers'; 'Education first'; 'Review of the medical record: ... it is never appropriate to downcode claims automatically or without a review of the medical record ... the physician should have an opportunity to provide supporting documentation before payment is reduced, not after'; 'Prior notification'; 'Clear communication: ... written notification of the adjustment, including the principal reason the claim was downcoded, specific clinical rationale for the decision, and a statement describing the process for appeal ... if payment is adjusted, then the corresponding code should be adjusted accordingly'. UHC policy: records requested when 'a billing pattern that deviates significantly from their peers'; 'level of care escalation data ... may be compared to their historical data and to peer data'. Anthem KY manual authorizes prepayment E&M review but states no exemption criteria. Dermatology Times: practices under a BCBS outlier program had 'a one-week window to submit records before payment is withheld'. Practice experience (from the task context): payers stop after enough successful appeals and resume months later.

**What it means for ODA:** Add a provider-profile view (E/M level mix per provider vs. ODA peers and vs. a dermatology benchmark - benchmark source is missing data) and an 'overturn ledger' per payer/provider; when overturned downcodes exceed a threshold (e.g., 10 in 90 days or overturn rate above 70%), auto-draft a program-removal letter to the provider representative citing the ledger and AMA principles, and open a recurrence watch that alerts when the payer's downcode rate for that provider rises again.

Sources: AMA downcoding guide PDF (local copy, see finding 1) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/comm-reimbursement/COMM-Evaluation-and-Management-Policy.pdf) · [anthem.com](https://www.anthem.com/content/dam/digital/docs/provider/commercial/manuals/PM_KY_00011.pdf) · [dermatologytimes.com](https://www.dermatologytimes.com/view/the-new-economics-of-dermatology-claims-understanding-and-responding-to-rising-denials)

### [BACKGROUND] Appeals-program KPIs should be built on HFMA MAP Key definitions (remittance denial rate from 835s, denial write-offs as % of net revenue, aged A/R) plus downcoding-specific measures; published figures show the leverage: fewer than 1% of denied claims are appealed while 75-80% of appeals succeed.

HFMA MAP Keys: 'Remittance Denial Rate (AR-5)' = claims denied / claims remitted, counted from 835 remittance files, including partial payments carrying a denial indicator and excluding PR-group amounts; 'Denial Write-offs as a Percentage of Net Patient Service Revenue (AR-6)' = net denial write-offs in the month / average monthly net patient service revenue (three-month average); 'Aged A/R as a Percentage of Total Billed A/R (AR-1)' by 0-30/31-60/61-90/91-120/>120-day buckets; AR-2 by payer group; 'Net Days in A/R (FM-1)'; HFMA defines no appeal-overturn key. Dermatology Times (June 23, 2026): 'fewer than 1%' of denied claims are appealed (about 85 million denials vs about 263,000 appeals in 2024); 75-80% of appealed denials are overturned (MA denials 75% per HHS OIG 2018); recommended operations: one owner for denials, weekly tracking of the top three CARC codes by dollar volume, a weekly 30-minute review of aging and denial reports by payer and reason code, and review of denial concentration by physician; payer response windows can be as short as one week for records. Payer SLAs to measure against: Cigna 60 days/75 business days; Aetna 30 business days (reconsideration) and 60 calendar days (appeal); Anthem MA 45 days (reconsideration) and 30 days (appeal); CGS 60 days; MA plans 60 days for payment reconsiderations; Kentucky internal appeals 30 days.

**What it means for ODA:** Dashboard KPI set: downcode incidence (lines and visits) by payer/provider/code pair; dollars at risk (expected allowed minus paid); appeal filing rate (% of flagged lines appealed) and median days-to-file vs. deadline; overturn rate by payer and by argument type (time vs MDM); dollars and interest recovered; payer response time vs. its stated SLA; recurrence index (months until a payer resumes after overturns); contractual write-off leakage (CO-45 above contract); and a weekly 'top 3 CARC by dollars' tile.

Sources: [hfma.org](https://www.hfma.org/map-keys/) · [dermatologytimes.com](https://www.dermatologytimes.com/view/the-new-economics-of-dermatology-claims-understanding-and-responding-to-rising-denials) · [cigna.com](https://www.cigna.com/health-care-providers/coverage-and-claims/appeals-disputes) · [aetna.com](https://www.aetna.com/health-care-professionals/disputes-appeals/disputes-appeals-overview.html) · [anthem.com](https://www.anthem.com/content/dam/digital/docs/provider/commercial/manuals/ABSCRPM-0010-20.pdf)

### [UNVERIFIED] Medicare's CY2027 physician fee schedule changes same-day E/M economics for dermatology and must be modeled so legitimate reductions are not flagged as downcoding: CMS proposed paying the lower-valued of a same-day E/M and a 0-, 10- or 90-day-global procedure at 50% and converting G2211 into a modifier worth +16%; per the AADA (reported Nov 5, 2026) the final rule limits the cut to E/M with 0-day-global procedures at 80%, with a ~6% overall dermatology reduction; OIG reportedly found modifier 25 on 61.5% of dermatology E/M claims.

CMS CY2027 PFS proposed rule fact sheet (July 14, 2026): CMS proposes reducing payment when a separately identifiable office/outpatient E/M visit is furnished by the same physician (or same practice) on the same day as a 0-, 10- or 90-day global procedure - 'The higher-valued service is paid at 100%, and other qualifying services that day are paid at 50%' - citing that 'we are likely duplicating payment under the current payment methodology'; G2211 to become a modifier that 'would increase the payment of the associated E/M code by 16%'; proposed CFs $33.17 (qualifying APM) and $32.84 (non-qualifying). AMA (Aug 20, 2026): 'CMS should reject a proposed 50% Medicare payment cut for same-day E/M visits' and 'CMS has not provided the evidence needed to justify a cut this large.' Dermatology Times (Nov 5, 2026) on the finalized rule per AADA: the reduction 'now applies only when a separate E/M visit occurs the same day as a procedure with a 0-day global period', CMS 'replaced the proposed 50% payment for the second service with 100% for the higher-paid service and 80% for the second service'; non-qualifying CF $32.63 (down 2.31%); AADA estimates 'an approximately 6% overall payment reduction for dermatologists in 2027'. Dermatology Times (June 23, 2026) citing a November 2025 OIG audit: 'Modifier 25 appears on 61.5% of dermatology E/M claims', roughly four times the specialty average; about 90% of audited claims compliant; estimated $62.9 million in overpayments from the rest (OIG primary page returned HTTP 503; not verified directly).

**What it means for ODA:** From DOS 2027-01-01, Medicare (and MA plans following Medicare) will legitimately pay E/M-25 billed with 0-day-global procedures (e.g., 17000/17110 destructions, 11102-11107 biopsies) at 80% - the expected-allowed engine needs a year-aware rule so these are not counted as downcodes, while commercial payers applying the same reduction without a contract basis should be flagged; the modifier-25 'significant, separately identifiable' documentation standard becomes the next audit/appeal front, so the template needs a modifier-25 variant and the ODA remits already showing E/M-25 paid $0 with CARC B10 (UMR) are the first use case.

Sources: [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/newsroom/fact-sheets/calendar-year-cy-2027-medicare-physician-fee-schedule-proposed-rule) · [ama-assn.org](https://www.ama-assn.org/about/leadership/50-medicare-pay-cut-would-put-physician-practices-risk) · [dermatologytimes.com](https://www.dermatologytimes.com/view/aada-warns-of-6-medicare-payment-cut-for-dermatologists-in-2027) · [dermatologytimes.com](https://www.dermatologytimes.com/view/the-new-economics-of-dermatology-claims-understanding-and-responding-to-rising-denials)

### Open questions

- Cigna reimbursement policy R49 ('E/M coding accuracy', effective October 2025): the policy text, affected codes (reported 99204/99205/99214/99215/99244/99245), the outlier-identification method, and any exclusion/exemption process could not be retrieved (all static.cigna.com URL guesses 404); obtain from the Cigna for Health Care Professionals portal or from ODA's Cigna provider rep.
- Humana participating (in-network) provider reconsideration/dispute deadline for Medicare Advantage and commercial claims: Humana's page points to its provider manual, which was not reachable; also unknown whether Humana applies an E/M level edit (its claims-payment-policies page lists none).
- Anthem's actual E/M level review mechanism (vendor/algorithm, whether records are requested, exemption criteria) and the text of its 'Evaluation and Management Services' commercial reimbursement policy; the anthem.com reimbursement policy library pages did not list individual policies.
- Whether Kentucky has a 2026-2027 downcoding bill pending (the KRS Subtitle 17A index showed no downcoding section as of Nov 2026; Indiana, Illinois and Virginia acted in 2026).
- ODA's own participation agreements: dispute-resolution/arbitration clauses and timelines for each MA and commercial contract (these override manual defaults), ODA's in-network status with each MA plan (determines contract route vs CMS Subpart M route), and the ERISA self-funded vs fully insured status of each UMR/UHSS/Anthem ASO account (determines whether Kentucky prompt-pay/interest law and the Kentucky DOI apply).
- Exact CPT 2024-2026 descriptor wording for the office-visit minimum times (verified only via the AMA press release on range removal and AAPC's code summary; confirm in the CPT 2026 codebook before quoting in letters).
- Whether 42 CFR 422.582(b) has been amended to 65 days: the eCFR text read on 2026-11-04 still says 60 calendar days with a 5-day receipt presumption, while CMS guidance and all MA plan manuals use 65 days from the notice date.
- OIG's November 2025 dermatology modifier-25 audit (report number, URL, exact findings): the OIG site returned HTTP 503; figures here are as reported by Dermatology Times.
- The AMA downcoding guide's canonical ama-assn.org URL (the PDF was read from a locally saved copy; several guessed URLs 404) and whether the AMA has published a 2025-2026 revision covering Cigna R49 and the new state laws.
- A dermatology-specific E/M level benchmark (e.g., Medicare utilization by specialty) for peer comparison in the provider-profile view, and AAD member-only resources (E/M coding tool, DermWorld 'Cracking the Code', audit response templates) which are behind login and could not be read.
- Whether Anthem's 2-year commercial reconsideration window applies to FEP and self-funded (ASO) accounts administered by Anthem, and whether the Kentucky DOI has issued any bulletin on E/M downcoding.

## Payment fees and offsets taken through PLB: Zelis, Optum, virtual cards, recoupments

Research lens: payment-vendor fees and offsets skimmed through the 835 PLB segment (Zelis, Optum Pay, VCC, origination fees) plus recoupment/sequestration rules. The per-turn WebSearch budget was already exhausted, so every fact below comes from direct reads of primary sources (CMS guidance letters/FAQ PDFs, eCFR via Cornell LII, Kentucky Legislature statute PDFs, X12 code lists, CAQH CORE rule PDFs, govinfo bill text, CMS fact sheets, payer manuals and vendor pages). Key results: (1) CMS GL-2022-04 (2022) says a health plan MUST pay by HIPAA-standard ACH EFT + 835 when the provider asks, with no exceptions, and may not condition that on accepting a vendor's fee-bearing services; but CMS's July 2022 FAQ says charging fees is not, in and of itself, a HIPAA violation. (2) Fee landscape: VCC up to 5%, vendor ACH typically 2-3% (Zelis 2.5%, MGMA/AMA polls), Optum Pay Premium 0.5% per payment while Optum Pay Basic is free; Anthem and UHC manuals admit they receive revenue from VCC/vendor programs; every payer offers a no-fee path (EnrollSafe for Anthem, Optum Pay Basic ACH for UHC, Zelis 'HIPAA Standard EFT/ERA' for UMR/Zelis-paid plans). (3) PLB semantics and Medicare's two-step FB/WO recoupment reporting give concrete detection rules; positive PLB amounts reduce the check. (4) Kentucky KRS 304.17A-714/-708 impose a 24-month limit on commercial recoupment and retroactive denial with a 30-day dispute right that freezes recoupment; MA, FEHB and self-funded ERISA plans are federally preempted. (5) Medicare recoupment: 15-day rebuttal, interest from day 31, recoupment no earlier than day 41, stopped by a timely redetermination (file by day 30), 60-day/6-year self-report rule. (6) Sequestration is 2% of the Medicare payment after cost sharing (CARC 253) since July 1, 2022; CY2026 CF is $33.40 non-QP / $33.57 QP with a -2.5% efficiency adjustment that exempts E/M.

### [UNVERIFIED] CMS Guidance Letter GL-2022-04 (dated March 22, 2022, signed May 24, 2022) states that if a provider requests payment via the adopted HIPAA EFT standard (NACHA CCD+ with the X12 835 TRN segment) and the X12 835 ERA, the health plan must comply with no exceptions, in-network or not, and may not require the provider to accept payment or reassociation services from the plan's vendor as a condition of receiving standard EFT/ERA.

Exact text: "Yes, the health plan must comply. 45 C.F.R. § 162.925(a)(1) dictates that if an entity requests that a health plan conduct a transaction as a standard transaction, the health plan must do so." ... "Section 45 C.F.R. § 162.925(a)(1) provides no exceptions ... a health plan must comply with all three standards regardless of whether a provider is in the plan's network." On vendors: "a health plan may not require that a provider agree to receive payment or reassociation services from its business associate (nor may the business associate otherwise require this) as a condition of receiving health care payments using the adopted EFT and ERA standards." ... "any health plan attempt to require a provider to agree to receive unwanted payment or reassociation services from a specific vendor ... may be construed as the health plan adversely affecting the transaction or the provider on the basis that the transaction is a standard transaction" (45 CFR 162.925(a)(2)). Also: "HHS has not promulgated HIPAA regulations governing claims payments made with VCCs. Therefore, HHS does not regulate ... fees assessed by health plans or credit card networks for VCC payments." The plan is liable for its business associate's noncompliance (45 CFR 162.923(c)). Complaints go to CMS NSG via ASETT; questions to AdministrativeSimplification@cms.hhs.gov. 45 CFR 162.925(a)(5) separately bars a plan that requires a clearinghouse from charging "fees or costs in excess of the fees or costs for normal telecommunications."

**What it means for ODA:** Appeal/demand-letter lever: ODA can send each payer (UHC/UMR/Optum, Anthem, Humana, Cigna) a written request to be paid by the HIPAA-standard ACH CCD+ EFT and X12 835 with no vendor services, cite 45 CFR 162.925(a)(1)-(2) and GL-2022-04, and file an ASETT complaint if the plan or its vendor (Zelis, Optum) keeps deducting fees. The dashboard should store the date each request was sent and flag any PLB fee that appears after that date as a complaint-ready event.

Sources: [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/document/guidance-letter-vcc-eft-era.pdf) · [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/45/162.925) · [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/45/162.923) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/training-education/look-up-topics/hipaa-administrative-simplification)

### [UNVERIFIED] CMS's July 14, 2022 FAQ on GL-2022-04 says charging fees for EFT/ERA is not, in and of itself, prohibited by HIPAA; the only payment method a plan must provide on request is ACH EFT (not paper check); and the 5010 835 Payment Method Codes cover ACH, check and wire but not VCC. Historically CMS posted (Aug/Sept 2017) and then withdrew (Feb 2018) an FAQ that said EFT fees were not allowed, after lobbying by Zelis.

FAQ text: "No. ... while there may be circumstances in which a health plan, or its business associate, charging fees could be considered adversely affecting a transaction or entity because it is a standard transaction, the guidance letter does not speak to whether charging fees to conduct standard transactions is, in and of itself, a violation of the HIPAA requirements." ... "The only payment method that HIPAA regulations require health plans to provide, when requested by a provider to do so, is EFT through the ACH Network." ... "Version 5010 includes Payment Method Codes that identify corresponding payments made through the ACH Network, by check, and by wire transfer, but not by VCC." NSG "does not have the authority to reimburse health care providers" for labor costs; civil money penalties go to Treasury. History (ProPublica, Aug 14, 2023, Cezary Podkul): CMS posted an FAQ Aug 11, 2017 saying fees were prohibited, removed it Aug 14, 2017 after Zelis protested, re-posted in Sept 2017 that payers "should not charge providers communications fees", then on Feb 9, 2018 CMS official Christine Gerhardt wrote "Let's just take it down" after Nixon Peabody (for Zelis) threatened suit; CMS later said it had "no legal authority to 'flat-out prohibit fees.'" The AMA's Oct 5, 2017 article quoted the 2017 FAQ: "A health plan cannot require a provider to accept virtual credit card payments" and that the only allowed fee was the provider's own bank charge "averaging 34 cents per transaction." The 2022 FAQ document metadata is dated July 11-14, 2022 (not August).

**What it means for ODA:** Do not claim in letters that fees are illegal per se. The strong argument is (a) the plan must honor a request for standard ACH EFT with no vendor add-ons, and (b) a fee imposed as a condition of standard EFT is 'adversely affecting' the transaction under 162.925(a)(2). The dashboard's appeal-packet generator should use that framing and cite the FAQ's own words.

Sources: [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/document/gl-2022-04-go-answers-faq.pdf) · [propublica.org](https://www.propublica.org/article/the-hidden-fee-costing-doctors-millions-every-year) · [ama-assn.org](https://www.ama-assn.org/practice-management/claims-processing/physicians-protected-health-plan-credit-card-fees)

### [UNVERIFIED] Vendor fee magnitudes are well documented: VCC fees run up to 5% and vendor ACH fees typically 2-3% (Zelis 2.5%, sometimes lowered to 2.1%); Optum Pay Premium is 0.5% per ACH payment while Optum Pay Basic is free; 57% of practices in MGMA's Aug 2021 poll were charged EFT fees they did not agree to.

ProPublica: "Fees for VCCs run as high as 5% versus a typical 2.5% for other kinds of electronic payments"; Zelis "sometimes offered to lower a fee from 2.5% to 2.1%"; MGMA poll: fees "can cost larger medical practices $1 million a year"; AdventHealth 2020 complaint: "$1.8M in expenses". MGMA Stat (Aug 10, 2021, 391 responses): 57% yes; among them 10% paid 1%, 43% paid 2%, 43% paid 3%, 4% paid 4%+. AMA sign-on letter to CMS, Oct 14, 2021 (signed by AMA, American Academy of Dermatology Association, American Society for Dermatologic Surgery Association, Kentucky Medical Association, MGMA and ~90 others): "86 percent reporting average fees of two percent‒three percent of the claim payment"; "these vendors do not offer physician practices the choice of electing basic EFT payments without charge"; physicians "lose up to five percent of claims payments due to EFT fees". Optum Pay portal: "Fees will apply at a rate of 0.5% per consolidated payment ($5 for every $1,000 in payments.)"; Optum sell sheet: "Basic No fees ... 13 months of historical remittance/claims payment data" vs "Premium Fees: 0.5% per ACH payment ... 36 months". Zelis enrollment page: payment options are "HIPAA Standard EFT/ERA," "Premium ACH powered by ZAPP Edge" and "Virtual card (VCC)"; "Zelis charges a fee for access to ZAPP Edge services."

**What it means for ODA:** Dollar-estimate rule for the dashboard: for every 835, compute fee_rate = PLB fee amount / (BPR02 + fee amount). A rate near 0.5% identifies Optum Pay Premium; 2-3% identifies Zelis Premium ACH/ZAPP Edge-type programs; VCC fees never appear in the 835 (they are merchant-processor charges), so VCC leakage must be estimated from bank/merchant statements at 2-5% of VCC volume. Annualize each payer's fee rate against its payment volume to show the recoverable amount from switching to no-fee standard EFT.

Sources: [propublica.org](https://www.propublica.org/article/the-hidden-fee-costing-doctors-millions-every-year) · [mgma.com](https://www.mgma.com/data/data-stories/more-than-half-of-medical-practices-report-being-f) · [searchlf.ama-assn.org](https://searchlf.ama-assn.org/letter/documentDownload?uri=%2Funstructured%2Fbinary%2Fletter%2FLETTERS%2F2021-10-14-Sign-on-Letter-re-EFT-Fees.pdf) · [myservices.optumhealthpaymentservices.com](https://myservices.optumhealthpaymentservices.com/) · [optum.com](https://www.optum.com/content/dam/optum4/resources/pdf/sell-sheet/Optum%20Pay%20Sell%20Sheet%20.pdf) · [zelis.com](https://www.zelis.com/providers/provider-enrollment/)

### [UNVERIFIED] UnitedHealthcare pays through Optum Pay: ACH Basic is free (13 months of data, downloadable 835), Premium costs 0.5% per ACH payment (36 months), and if a provider does not enroll in ACH, UHC issues virtual card payments by default with merchant card-processing fees; the opt-out is to enroll in ACH at myservices.optumhealthpaymentservices.com or call 877-620-6194. UHC's 835 companion guide says UHC uses PLB codes FB, L3, L6 and WO.

UHC Electronic Payment Solutions page: Basic "Available at no cost to health care professionals and facilities who enroll in ACH/direct deposit/EFT"; Premium "Fee is 0.5% per ACH/direct deposit/EFT"; "If you don't sign up for ACH/direct deposit, you'll continue to receive VCPs for services rendered"; "Merchant card processing fees may apply to VCPs"; "You can change your enrollment at any time to receive ACH/direct deposit payments"; "The VCP program is not available in the state of Kansas." 2026 UHC Care Provider Administrative Guide: "If ACH/direct deposit is not chosen, a virtual card payment (VCP) will be issued"; "UnitedHealthcare-affiliated companies may receive transaction fees or other compensation related to some payment options." Optum direct-deposit enrollment guide: "If you take no action to select an option, we will default your portal access package to ACH basic"; "ACH basic — free"; "ACH premium — 0.5% per payment fee"; "There are multiple payment options for Optum Pay premium fees, and if you opt to have us debit fees, you'll need to enter the routing number and account number" (ACH Company ID 12343848776). Optum VCP guide: enrolling for VCP triggers a pop-up "alerting you of card processing fees you may be responsible for." UHC 835 Companion Guide v4.0 (April 2025, 005010X221A1): "UHC will use: FB qualifier for the Forward Balance (negative amount) that will be recovered from a provider's future remits This includes Patient Acct number, Claim number, and remit number. L3 qualifier for Penalty Sanctions, L6 qualifier to illustrate interest owed along with the claim number, WO qualifier for the overpayment recovery." Optum Pay support 1-877-620-6194.

**What it means for ODA:** ODA's UHC remits show PLB*...*AH:<reference>*21.89. The dashboard should test whether AH amounts are ~0.5% of the remit (Optum Pay Premium) and, if so, generate a one-line action: downgrade to Optum Pay Basic (free) in the portal. If UHC payments arrive as VCPs, the 835 will not show the fee; the hands-off fix is ACH enrollment. Parser rule: treat UHC PLB FB (negative) as a pending recoupment linked by the claim number in PLB03-2, WO as the actual recoupment, L6 as interest received, L3 as penalty.

Sources: [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/en/claims-payments-billing/Electronic-Payment-Solutions.html) · [optum.com](https://www.optum.com/content/dam/o4-dam/resources/pdfs/white-papers/optum-pay-direct-deposit-enrollment-guide.pdf) · [optum.com](https://www.optum.com/content/dam/o4-dam/resources/pdfs/white-papers/optum-pay-virtual-card-payment-enrollment-guide.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/en/claims-payments-billing.html) · local file saved by sibling agent: <scratchpad> (2026 UnitedHealthcare Care Provider Administrative Guide) · local file saved by sibling agent: <scratchpad> (UHC 835 Companion Guide v4.0 April 2025)

### [UNVERIFIED] A PLB with reason code CS and reference text such as 'ZELIS TRANSACTION FEE' (as seen on ODA's UMR remits) is a vendor fee deducted inside the remittance, which only happens on Zelis's fee-bearing ACH program (Premium ACH / ZAPP Edge); VCC fees are charged by the merchant processor and never appear in the 835. Zelis publicly lists a 'HIPAA Standard EFT/ERA' option and a client-service line (877-828-8770) through which the fee program can be dropped.

Zelis enrollment FAQ: "What payment options does Zelis offer providers?" lists "HIPAA Standard EFT/ERA," "Premium ACH powered by ZAPP Edge," "Virtual card (VCC)"; "Does Zelis charge a service fee? Zelis charges a fee for access to ZAPP Edge services." Zelis payer page lists "Standard ACH" and "Premium ACH, powered by ZAPP Edge" separately. Zelis Client Service Team: 877.828.8770, 8 a.m.-7 p.m. ET; portal identity.zelispayments.com; Zelis footnote counts 725 payers as of 12/31/2025. ProPublica reported Zelis's typical fee at 2.5% (occasionally 2.1%) and that in 2023 Zelis offered no-fee EFTs only for one of its 700+ payers. X12's external Provider Adjustment Reason Code list defines CS as "Adjustment amount, detailed information is provided separately to explain the adjustment" and AH as "Claim transmission fee amount ... used for transmission fees that are not specific to or dependent upon individual claims." Medicare ch. 22: "Positive adjustment amounts reduce the amount of the payment and negative adjustment amounts increase it" and "The check amount is the sum of all claim-level payments ... less any provider level adjustments."

**What it means for ODA:** Detection rule: flag any PLB where PLB03-1 in {CS, AH} and (PLB03-2 matches /FEE|ZELIS|TRANS|ORIG|ECHO|PAYSPAN|VPAY|INSTAMED|OPTUM/i or 0.3% <= amount/(BPR02+amount) <= 6%). Aggregate by payer and by vendor string; compute annualized leakage; emit the vendor-specific opt-out instruction (Zelis: request 'HIPAA Standard EFT/ERA' at 877-828-8770 and cite GL-2022-04). For UMR specifically, note UMR's own 835s are the evidence that the fee is deducted before payment.

Sources: [zelis.com](https://www.zelis.com/providers/provider-enrollment/) · [zelis.com](https://www.zelis.com/payers/payments/) · [propublica.org](https://www.propublica.org/article/the-hidden-fee-costing-doctors-millions-every-year) · [x12.org](https://x12.org/codes/provider-adjustment-reason-codes) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/regulations-and-guidance/guidance/manuals/downloads/clm104c22.pdf)

### [UNVERIFIED] PLB reason-code semantics and Medicare's two-step recoupment reporting give exact parsing rules: FB (forward balance) with a negative amount flags an overpayment that will be taken from a future remit, and a later WO with the same control number is the actual recoupment; 72/B2 acknowledge provider refunds; L6 is interest paid to the provider; LE/WU/IR are levies/withholdings; J1 is a non-reimbursable amount; 50/51/L3 are penalties.

X12 external list (verbatim): 72 "Provider refund amount" (note: "acknowledges a refund received from a provider for previous overpayment"); AH "Claim transmission fee amount"; B2 "Rebate amount" (provider over-refunded); B3 "Recovery amount"; CS "Adjustment amount, detailed information is provided separately"; E3 "Withholding amount"; FB "Non-claim related balance forward amount"; IR "Internal Revenue Service 1099 withholding amount"; J1 "Amount not reimbursed based on a demonstration program or other limitation that prevents issuance of payment"; L3 "Penalty amount"; L6 "Interest amount"; LE "Internal Revenue Service non-1099 withholding amount"; OB "Affiliated provider(s) offset amount"; WO "Overpayment recovery amount"; WU "Non-Internal Revenue Service withholding amount related to a federal payment levy program"; 50 late charge/penalty; 51 "Late filing interest penalty assessment amount"; 90 "Early payment allowance amount." CMS Pub 100-20 Transmittal 812 (CR 7068, Nov 12, 2010): in 5010 "two new codes – AH and HM – have been added, and code ZZ has been deleted"; PLB03-2 is "Required when a control, account or tracking number applies to this adjustment" (max 50). Medicare recoupment reporting: "Step I: Claim Level: The original claim payment is taken back and the new payment is established (Reversal and Correction) Provider Level: PLB03-1 – PLB reason code FB (Forward Balance) PLB 03-2 shows the detail: 1-2: 00, 3-19: Adjustment CCN#, 20-30: HIC# ... PLB04 shows the adjustment amount to offset the net adjustment amount shown at the service level"; "Step II: ... PLB03-1 – PLB reason code WO (Overpayment Recovery) ... PLB04 shows the actual amount being recouped." "A demand letter is also sent to the provider when the Accounts Receivable (A/R) is created – Step I. This document contains a control number for tracking purpose that is also reported on the RA." The CR's crosswalk shows Medicare using 72 "Authorized return" for refunds, L6 "Interest Owed – Interest paid on claims in this 835", WO "Recovery", J1 "Non-reimbursable", LE "Levy", CS "Adjustment" for reissued invoices/garnishments. Ch. 22: "Every provider level adjustment must likewise be reported in the provider level adjustment section"; PLB 90 is a balancing failsafe.

**What it means for ODA:** Parser/ledger design: (1) for each PLB build a row {payer, TRN02, PLB01 provider ID, PLB02 fiscal period, code, reference, amount}; (2) link FB(-) rows to the CLP reversal/correction pairs in the same 835 (CLP02=22 reversal with negative amounts followed by a corrected CLP) and to later WO rows via the control number in PLB03-2 (Medicare: positions 3-19 = CCN; UHC: patient account/claim/remit numbers); (3) treat WO as 'recouped dollars' per claim and feed the appeal-deadline engine; (4) treat 72/B2/B3 as refund acknowledgments to reconcile against ODA's refund checks; (5) treat L6 as interest income (useful proof of late payment); (6) alert on any J1, LE, WU, IR, OB, E3, L3, 50, 51 because they are not routine for a dermatology practice.

Sources: [x12.org](https://x12.org/codes/provider-adjustment-reason-codes) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/regulations-and-guidance/guidance/manuals/downloads/clm104c22.pdf) · local file saved by sibling agent: <scratchpad> (CMS Pub 100-20 One-Time Notification Transmittal 812, CR 7068)

### [UNVERIFIED] Medicare (CGS J15/J8) recoupment follows 42 CFR 405.370-405.379 and MLN006379: a demand letter starts the clock; the provider may rebut within 15 days; interest accrues from day 31 in full 30-day periods; standard recoupment cannot begin before day 41; a valid, timely redetermination request (file by day 30 to prevent any recoupment; absolute deadline day 120) stops recoupment; after an unfavorable redetermination recoupment resumes on the 60th day unless a QIC reconsideration is requested; after the QIC decision recoupment resumes even if an ALJ appeal is filed; immediate recoupment on request avoids 935(f)(2)(B) interest.

42 CFR 405.370: Recoupment is "The recovery by Medicare of any outstanding Medicare debt by reducing present or future Medicare payments and applying the amount withheld to the indebtedness"; Offset is the same for a non-Medicare debt. 405.371(a)(3): offset/recoupment only after CMS or the contractor "has determined that the provider or supplier ... has been overpaid." 405.373(a): contractor must "Notify the provider or supplier of its intention to offset or recoup payment, in whole or in part" and state "the reasons"; no notice needed for offsets against another provider sharing the same TIN (405.373(f)). 405.374: "the provider or supplier has at least 15 days following the date of notification to submit the statement." 405.378: interest "for each full 30-day period that payment is delayed" from the final determination (written determination + written demand), at the higher of the Treasury rate based on "private consumer rates of interest" or "The current value of funds rate"; interest is also paid on underpayments and on recouped amounts later reversed, "calculated in full 30-day periods." 405.379(d)(1): recoupment "no earlier than 41 days after the initial overpayment demand"; (e)(1)(ii) after a fully-affirmed redetermination, "On the 60th calendar day after the redetermination notice"; (e)(2) stops again on a timely QIC reconsideration request; (f) after QIC action recoupment may begin/resume; (g) recouped funds applied first to interest, then principal. MLN006379 (July 2025) timeline: Day 15 last rebuttal day; Day 16 immediate recoupment if requested; "file the redetermination request by Day 30 from the date of the demand letter to prevent recoupment starting on Day 41"; "If you file an appeal after Day 30, then by Day 120 the law requires the MAC to stop recoupment" but "won't refund money they have already recouped until the redetermination process is adjudicated"; Day 31 interest; Day 41 standard recoupment; Days 61-90 intent-to-refer letter; Day 120 last day for redetermination; Days 126-150 Treasury referral. "If you make an immediate recoupment, it isn't subject to interest under Section 935(f)(2)(B)"; "A rebuttal is different than an appeal and doesn't stop recoupment activities." Demand letters are issued for overpayments of $25 or more.

**What it means for ODA:** Deadline engine for Medicare KY (J15) and Medicare IN (J8) remits: when an FB(-) or WO PLB appears (or a demand letter is logged), create tasks at demand+15 (rebuttal), demand+30 (file redetermination to block recoupment), demand+31 (interest starts), demand+41 (recoupment starts), demand+120 (redetermination hard deadline); after a redetermination notice, +60 days for QIC reconsideration. If ODA agrees with a demand, request immediate recoupment to avoid interest. Interest received (PLB L6) and interest owed should be tracked separately.

Sources: [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/42/405.370) · [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/42/405.371) · [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/42/405.373) · [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/42/405.374) · [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/42/405.378) · [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/42/405.379) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/outreach-and-education/medicare-learning-network-mln/mlnproducts/downloads/overpaymentbrochure508-09.pdf)

### [UNVERIFIED] Under the Medicare 60-day rule (42 CFR 401.305, as amended Dec 9, 2024, 89 FR 98553), a provider has 'identified' an overpayment when it 'knowingly receives or retains' one (False Claims Act knowledge standard), must report and return it within 60 days of identification (suspended up to 180 days during a good-faith investigation of related overpayments), subject to a 6-year lookback, using the MAC's claims-adjustment, credit-balance or self-reported-refund process.

Verbatim: "A person has identified an overpayment when the person knowingly receives or retains an overpayment" ('knowingly' per 31 U.S.C. 3729(b)(1)(A)); deadline is "The date which is 60 days after the date on which the overpayment was identified" or the cost-report due date if later; suspension ends no later than "The date that is 180 days after the date on which the initial identified overpayment was identified"; lookback: "within 6 years of the date the overpayment was received"; reporting via "an applicable claims adjustment, credit balance, self-reported refund, or other reporting process" set by the contractor. MLN006379 restates: return within "60 days of identifying the overpayment" and "6 years of getting an overpayment, generally known as the 'lookback period'."

**What it means for ODA:** The same 835 analytics that find underpayments will surface Medicare overpayments to ODA (duplicate primary payments, secondary payments exceeding the OA-23 remainder, payments after a reversal). Because detection by the dashboard can constitute 'knowing' identification, the system must route Medicare credit balances to a 60-day refund workflow with an audit trail; this is a compliance obligation, not optional.

Sources: [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/42/401.305) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/outreach-and-education/medicare-learning-network-mln/mlnproducts/downloads/overpaymentbrochure508-09.pdf)

### [UNVERIFIED] Kentucky law (KRS 304.17A-714 and 304.17A-708, effective 2000/2002) limits commercial insurers' recoupments and retroactive denials to 24 months after the claim was paid, requires itemized written/electronic notice, gives the provider 30 calendar days to dispute, bars recoupment while a dispute is pending, and requires the dispute to be completed within 30 days through the insurer's appeals process; KRS 304.17A-730 imposes 12%/18%/21% interest on late clean claims. Medicare Advantage, FEHB and self-funded ERISA plans are federally preempted from these state rules.

KRS 304.17A-714(1): "Except for overpayments which are a result of an error in the payment rate or method, an insurer that determines that a provider was overpaid shall, within twenty-four (24) months from the date that the insurer paid the claim, provide written or electronic notice to the provider of the amount of the overpayment, the covered person's name, patient identification number, date of service ..., insurer reference number for the claim, and the basis for determining that an overpayment exists" and must either request a refund or state that recoupment will occur if no dispute is received "within thirty (30) calendar days"; (2) provider disputes within 30 calendar days by submitting additional information; (3) "If a provider files a dispute ..., no recoupment shall be made until the dispute is resolved"; (4) disputes "completed within thirty (30) days with the insurer's provider appeals process"; (6) for recoupments, the insurer must "within twenty-four (24) months from the date that the insurer paid the claim, and at the actual time of recoupment give the provider written or electronic documentation that specifies: (a) The amount of the recoupment; (b) The covered person's name ...; (c) Patient identification number; and (d) Date of service." KRS 304.17A-708(3)(a): "Except in cases of fraud, an insurer may only retroactively deny reimbursement to a provider during the twenty-four (24) month period after the date that the insurer paid the claim"; (1) "An insurer shall not require a provider to appeal errors in payment where the insurer has not paid the claim according to the contracted rate. Miscalculations ... shall be corrected and paid within thirty (30) calendar days upon the insurer's receipt of documentation from the provider"; (2) provider's correction request must be within 24 months of receiving payment. KRS 304.17A-712: refunds/recoupments for ineligible members or non-covered services must follow 714. KRS 304.17A-702: clean claims paid, denied or contested within 30 calendar days. KRS 304.17A-730: interest 12% (1-30 days late), 18% (31-60), 21% (over 60), added automatically. KRS 304.17A-724: the sections also apply "to any entity an insurer contracts with to perform claims processing functions"; 'insurer' has the meaning in KRS 304.17A-005 (not retrieved). Preemption: 42 CFR 422.402 "The standards established under this part supersede any State law or regulation (other than State licensing laws or State laws relating to plan solvency) with respect to the MA plans"; 5 U.S.C. 8902(m)(1): FEHB contract terms relating to coverage or benefits "(including payments with respect to benefits) shall supersede and preempt any State or local law ... which relates to health insurance or plans"; 29 U.S.C. 1144(a)/(b)(2)(B): ERISA supersedes state laws relating to employee benefit plans and a plan "shall not be deemed to be an insurance company" (deemer clause), so self-funded plans administered by UMR are outside state insurance law.

**What it means for ODA:** Dispute rules for Anthem KY commercial, Cigna, UHC commercial fully insured, Humana commercial: (1) any WO/FB recoupment or retroactive denial whose original payment date is more than 24 months earlier is presumptively unlawful under KRS 304.17A-714/-708 and should auto-generate a dispute letter; (2) every recoupment notice must be answered within 30 calendar days to freeze recoupment; (3) underpayments (e.g., downcoded E/M paid below contract) can be demanded as 'payment errors' that the insurer must correct within 30 days without a formal appeal, and ODA has 24 months to raise them; (4) late clean-claim payments earn 12-21% interest. The dashboard must tag each payer/plan with its regime: KY-insured vs MA (federal, contract governs) vs FEP (OPM) vs self-funded ERISA (UMR), and apply the matching deadlines.

Sources: [apps.legislature.ky.gov](https://apps.legislature.ky.gov/law/statutes/statute.aspx?id=29336) · [apps.legislature.ky.gov](https://apps.legislature.ky.gov/law/statutes/statute.aspx?id=29333) · [apps.legislature.ky.gov](https://apps.legislature.ky.gov/law/statutes/statute.aspx?id=55766) · [apps.legislature.ky.gov](https://apps.legislature.ky.gov/law/statutes/statute.aspx?id=29329) · [apps.legislature.ky.gov](https://apps.legislature.ky.gov/law/statutes/statute.aspx?id=29344) · [apps.legislature.ky.gov](https://apps.legislature.ky.gov/law/statutes/statute.aspx?id=29341) · [apps.legislature.ky.gov](https://apps.legislature.ky.gov/law/statutes/statute.aspx?id=48419) · [apps.legislature.ky.gov](https://apps.legislature.ky.gov/law/statutes/chapter.aspx?id=38715) · [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/42/422.402) · [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/5/8902)

### [UNVERIFIED] Payer manuals set shorter contractual windows that the dashboard must honor: UHC gives 30 calendar days to disagree with a recoupment and 12 months total for reconsideration + appeal, offsets audit overpayments after 45 days; UMR attempts recoupment for 120 days then refers to Payment Resolution Solutions; Anthem MA uses a 4-year recovery lookback and keeps recouping during an appeal; Anthem commercial gives 30 days after a Notice of Overpayment and may recoup against unrelated payments; FEP reserves the right to 'reduce subsequent benefit payments to offset overpayments'.

UHC 2026 Administrative Guide: "If you disagree with the claim adjustment, or request for an overpayment refund or recoupment, you may submit a notice of disagreement within 30 calendar days (or as required by law or your Agreement) from the date of receipt of notification"; "You must submit your reconsideration and appeal to us within 12 months from the date of the original claim EOB or PRA"; audits: "we recoup or offset the identified overpayment and/or disallowed charge amounts after 45 calendar days from the date of the refund request, except when ... You have provided written notification of disagreement"; UMR: "If an overpayment is eligible for recoupment, UMR will attempt to recoup the overpayment for 120 days ... the overpayment will be sent to Payment Resolution Solutions for recovery assistance." UHC MA capitation: "The UnitedHealthcare MA plans reduce health care provider capitation payments for MA membership by 2% ... a result of the CMS-announced sequestration reductions." Anthem KY MA Guidebook 2.19.7: "To align with CMS guidelines, 42 CFR § 405.980, 405.986, Anthem recovers Medicare Advantage claim overpayments within four years of the claim payment date"; 2.20: refund within 30 days of notice, otherwise offset "against any payments due and payable"; "such appeal shall not suspend Anthem's right to recoup the erroneous payment amount during the appeal process, unless suspension of the right to recoup is otherwise required by regulatory requirements." Anthem KY Commercial Manual: "Recoupment may be performed against a separate Anthem payment unrelated to the service"; after a Notice of Overpayment the provider "has thirty (30) calendar days to reimburse" or may appeal; refunds not received "in accordance with Provider contractual language, and state regulations" result in claim recoupment; disputes via the Availity Overpayment Portal. 2026 FEP brochure: "We will make diligent efforts to recover benefit payments we made in error but in good faith. We may reduce subsequent benefit payments to offset overpayments."

**What it means for ODA:** Per-payer deadline table for the recoupment module: UHC/UMR/Optum = 30 days to dispute a recoupment, 12 months reconsideration+appeal from the PRA date; Anthem commercial = 30 days from Notice of Overpayment (plus KRS 24-month cap); Anthem MA = 30 days, 4-year lookback, recoupment continues during appeal; FEP = OPM/FEHB regime. The dashboard should show, for each WO/FB event, the governing regime, the days remaining, and whether the original payment date exceeds the applicable lookback (24 months KY commercial; 4 years Anthem MA).

### [UNVERIFIED] The federally mandated CAQH CORE Phase III rules (45 CFR 162.1603, effective Jan 1, 2014) require the 835 to be released no sooner than 3 business days before and no later than 3 business days after the CCD+ effective entry date, define 'late/missing' as 4 business days, require written late/missing resolution procedures, require BPR16 to equal the ACH effective date, and cap the EFT enrollment data a plan may demand; CMS says the rule set 'requires health plans to offer a standardized, online enrollment for EFT and ERA'.

45 CFR 162.1603 adopts (Jan 1, 2014) "Phase III CORE 380 EFT Enrollment Data Rule, version 3.0.0, June 2012", "Phase III CORE 382 ERA Enrollment Data Rule", "Phase III 360 CORE Uniform Use of CARCs and RARCs (835) Rule" with the code-combination companion, "Phase III CORE 370 EFT & ERA Reassociation (CCD+/835) Rule", and "Phase III CORE 350 ... Infrastructure Rule ... except Requirement 4.2" (batch acknowledgements). CORE 370 §4.2: "A health plan must release for transmission to the healthcare provider the v5010 X12 835 ... No sooner than three business days ... prior to the CCD+ Effective Entry Date And No later than three business days after the CCD+ Effective Entry Date. A health plan must ensure that the CCD+ Effective Entry Date is a valid banking day and that the corresponding v5010 X12 835 BPR16 date is the same valid banking day." §4.2.2: must meet this "ninety percent (90%) of the time as measured within a calendar month." §4.3: "Late or missing is defined as a maximum elapsed time of four business days following the receipt of either the Healthcare EFT Standards or v5010 X12 835." CMS Aug 7, 2012 fact sheet: the rule set "requires health plans to offer a standardized, online enrollment for EFT and ERA" and "requires health plans to send the EFT within a certain number of days of the ERA." CMS EFT basics PDF: "Health plans must conduct the EFT and ERA standard transactions with providers when asked to do so"; "Health plans are not permitted to use proprietary codes in the ERA." CMS operating-rules page: "all health plans must transmit payments through the Automated Clearing House (ACH) Network" when using EFT standards. Current CORE-required code combinations: v3.10.0, February 2026 (xlsx on dataspring.com).

**What it means for ODA:** Reassociation module: ingest bank deposit data (TRN02 trace numbers from the bank's CCD+ addenda) and match to 835 TRN02/BPR02; flag (a) 835s with no deposit within 4 business days of BPR16, (b) deposits with no 835, and (c) deposit amount < BPR02 (a deduction not disclosed in the 835, e.g., a bank or vendor fee). Payers that use proprietary codes or miss the 3-business-day window are non-compliant and can be reported via ASETT. The dashboard should also use the CORE code-combination list (v3.10.0, Feb 2026) to validate CARC/RARC pairs such as CO-45/N-codes and PI-B10 on downcoded or zeroed E/M lines.

Sources: [law.cornell.edu](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/45/162.1603) · [dataspring.com](https://www.dataspring.com/hubfs/EFTERA_Reassociation_Rule.pdf) · [dataspring.com](https://www.dataspring.com/core/operating-rules) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/newsroom/fact-sheets/hhs-adopts-operating-rules-electronic-funds-transfersremittance-advice) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/priorities/key-initiatives/burden-reduction/administrative-simplification/operating-rules/eft-remittance-advice) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/document/electronic-funds-transfer-and-electronic-remittance-advice-transactions.pdf) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/newsroom/fact-sheets/hhs-adopts-hipaa-standard-electronic-funds-transfersremittance-advice-cms-0024-ifc)

### [BACKGROUND] The 'No Fees for EFTs Act' (H.R. 6487, 118th Congress) would amend Social Security Act §1173(g)(4)(B)(ii) to bar any 'charge, fee, or other payment ... including a withhold from payment' by a health plan or an entity completing the transaction on its behalf for EFT/ERA transactions on or after Jan 1, 2024; it was introduced Nov 28, 2023 and referred to committee, and no enactment could be confirmed. Organized medicine (AMA + ~90 societies including AADA, ASDSA and the Kentucky Medical Association) and MGMA have formally asked CMS to guarantee a no-fee basic EFT option.

Bill text (govinfo, 'ih' version): H.R. 6487, introduced by Mr. Murphy with Mr. Griffith, Mrs. Miller-Meeks, Mr. Kilmer, Mr. Bera and Ms. Schrier, Nov 28, 2023, referred to Energy and Commerce and Ways and Means; adds a new subclause barring health plans and any "entity completing the transaction on the plan's behalf" from imposing "any charge, fee, or other payment" including "a withhold from payment" for EFT and payment/remittance advice transactions "made on or after January 1, 2024"; no exceptions stated. AMA letter (Oct 14, 2021): asks CMS to "issue guidance stating that all health plans and their contracted vendors must offer at least one EFT standard transaction that does not require purchase of extra services for an additional fee" and to require "full transparency ... (a) the clear option to select basic EFT without additional fees"; argues fee programs violate 42 U.S.C. 1320d-4 and 45 CFR 162.925(a)(2) and (a)(5), and that CMS's 2012 rule estimated "no direct costs to physician practices." MGMA: "MGMA believes CMS has the statutory authority and the statutory requirement to prohibit EFT fees." Congress.gov and GovTrack blocked automated access, so later status and any 119th-Congress reintroduction are unconfirmed.

**What it means for ODA:** Use as context in payer letters (industry-wide recognition that fee programs are coercive) but not as binding law. The dashboard's 'fee leak' report can quote AADA/ASDSA/KMA's signature on the 2021 letter to show dermatology's position.

Sources: [govinfo.gov](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/BILLS-118hr6487ih/html/BILLS-118hr6487ih.htm) · [searchlf.ama-assn.org](https://searchlf.ama-assn.org/letter/documentDownload?uri=%2Funstructured%2Fbinary%2Fletter%2FLETTERS%2F2021-10-14-Sign-on-Letter-re-EFT-Fees.pdf) · [mgma.com](https://www.mgma.com/data/data-stories/more-than-half-of-medical-practices-report-being-f)

### [UNVERIFIED] Medicare FFS sequestration reduces the Medicare payment (after deductible, coinsurance and MSP adjustments) by 2% on claims with dates of service on or after April 1, 2013, reported with CARC 253 'Sequestration - reduction in federal payment'; it was suspended during COVID through March 31, 2022, set at 1% for April 1-June 30, 2022, and has been 2% again since July 1, 2022, with P.L. 117-71 scheduling 2.25%/3% for FY2030. UHC's MA plans apply the same 2% to capitated providers.

CMS listserv March 8, 2013: "Medicare FFS claims with dates-of-service or dates-of-discharge on or after April 1, 2013, will incur a 2 percent reduction in Medicare payment"; "The claims payment adjustment shall be applied to all claims after determining coinsurance, any applicable deductible, and any applicable Medicare Secondary Payment adjustments"; "beneficiary payments for deductibles and coinsurance are not subject to the 2 percent payment reduction." X12 CARC 253: "Sequestration - reduction in federal payment" (start 06/02/2013). P.L. 117-71 §2(a) extends the CARES Act suspension end date to "March 31, 2022"; §2(b): "beginning on April 1, 2022, and ending on June 30, 2022, the payment reduction shall be 1.0 percent"; FY2030: "the payment reduction shall be 2.25 percent" for the first 6 months and "3 percent" for the second 6 months. UHC 2026 guide: "The UnitedHealthcare MA plans reduce health care provider capitation payments for MA membership by 2%." Whether Humana, Anthem MA or BCBS MA pass sequestration through to fee-for-service physician contracts was not confirmed (contract-dependent).

**What it means for ODA:** Expected-payment formula for Medicare KY/IN lines: expected_paid = 0.98 × (allowed − deductible − coinsurance) for DOS on/after July 1, 2022 (0.99 for DOS Apr 1-Jun 30, 2022; 1.00 for DOS Apr 2020-Mar 2022). A CO-253 amount that is not exactly 2% of the post-cost-sharing payment, or a 253 on a commercial/MA line without contractual basis, is an underpayment flag. Do not count CO-253 as a payer 'take' in the downcoding dollar totals; show it separately as statutory.

Sources: [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/outreach-and-education/outreach/ffsprovpartprog/downloads/2013-03-08-standalone.pdf) · [govinfo.gov](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/PLAW-117publ71/html/PLAW-117publ71.htm) · [x12.org](https://x12.org/codes/claim-adjustment-reason-codes) · local file saved by sibling agent: <scratchpad>

### [UNVERIFIED] CMS's CY 2026 Physician Fee Schedule final rule (CMS-1832-F, released Oct 31, 2025; Federal Register doc 2025-19787, Nov 5, 2025, corrected Nov 28, 2025 and Mar 12, 2026) sets a conversion factor of $33.40 for non-qualifying-APM clinicians (+3.26%, +$1.05 vs $32.35) and $33.57 for qualifying APM participants (+3.77%), including a one-year +2.50% statutory increase, and applies a -2.5% 'efficiency adjustment' to work RVUs of non-time-based services while exempting E/M visits, care management, behavioral health, telehealth-list services and maternity codes; it also shifts indirect practice expense toward office-based settings.

CMS fact sheet: "final CY 2026 qualifying APM conversion factor of $33.57" ... "represents a projected increase of $1.22 (+3.77%)"; "final CY 2026 nonqualifying APM conversion factor of $33.40" (+$1.05, +3.26%); both include "a one-year increase of +2.50 percent for CY 2026 stipulated by statute"; "final efficiency adjustment of -2.5% for CY 2026" applied to non-time-based services, exempting "time-based codes, such as evaluation and management (E/M) services," care management, behavioral health, Medicare telehealth-list services and maternity codes with an MMM global; CMS is "finalizing to recognize greater indirect costs for practitioners in office-based settings compared to facility settings." Federal Register catalog: final rule 2025-19787 (2025-11-05), corrections 2025-21458 (2025-11-28) and 2026-04797 (2026-03-12). The fact sheet did not state a dermatology-specific impact figure; federalregister.gov blocked automated reading of the rule text.

**What it means for ODA:** Expected-allowed engine: for 2026 Medicare E/M lines use CF $33.40 (ODA is unlikely to be a QP unless in an Advanced APM) × 2026 RVUs × Kentucky/Indiana GPCI; E/M RVUs carry no efficiency cut, whereas dermatology procedures (biopsies, destructions, excisions, Mohs) carry the -2.5% work-RVU efficiency adjustment, so 2026 procedure allowed amounts legitimately fall relative to 2025 while E/M should rise with the CF. Any 2026 Medicare E/M allowed amount that implies a CF below $33.40 (or a 2025 amount implying below $32.35) is a pricing error, not a downcode, and should be flagged as a separate underpayment class. Commercial plans that peg to 'Medicare rates' need the contract's reference year to avoid false positives.

Sources: [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/newsroom/fact-sheets/calendar-year-cy-2026-medicare-physician-fee-schedule-final-rule-cms-1832-f) · [federalregister.gov](https://www.federalregister.gov/api/v1/documents.json?conditions[term]=%22CY+2026+Payment+Policies+Under+the+Physician+Fee+Schedule%22&conditions[type][]=RULE&order=newest)

### [UNVERIFIED] CARC and group-code definitions needed to classify offsets and secondary-payer adjustments: CARC 23 'The impact of prior payer(s) adjudication including payments and/or adjustments' is valid only with group OA; CARC 45 'Charge exceeds fee schedule' cannot equal the whole charge; CARC 59 is 'Processed based on multiple or concurrent procedure rules'; CARC 187 is a consumer-spending-account payment; CARC 252 and 237 require a RARC; CARC B10 is 'Allowed amount has been reduced because a component of the basic procedure/test was paid'; group CR is not valid in 5010, and Medicare says CO adjustments are provider write-offs that MACs may never omit.

X12 CARC list (verbatim): 23 "The impact of prior payer(s) adjudication including payments and/or adjustments. (Use only with Group Code OA)"; 45 "Charge exceeds fee schedule/maximum allowable or contracted/legislated fee arrangement" with usage "This adjustment amount cannot equal the total service or claim charge amount" and must not duplicate prior-payer adjustments; 59 "Processed based on multiple or concurrent procedure rules" (refer to the 835 Healthcare Policy Identification REF if present); 94 "Processed in Excess of charges"; 187 "Consumer Spending Account payments"; 223 mandated law/regulation adjustment; 237 "Legislated/Regulatory Penalty" (RARC required); 252 "An attachment/other documentation is required to adjudicate this claim/service" (RARC required); 253 sequestration; 97 benefit included in another service; B10 "Allowed amount has been reduced because a component of the basic procedure/test was paid. The beneficiary is not liable for more than the charge limit for the basic procedure/test." Group codes: CO Contractual Obligation; OA Other Adjustment; PI Payer Initiated Reductions; PR Patient Responsibility; CR "This value is not to be used with 005010 and up." Medicare ch. 22 §60.1: "MACs do not have discretion to omit appropriate codes and messages"; CO adjustments "are considered a write off for the provider and are not billed to the patient."

**What it means for ODA:** Classification rules: (1) OA-23 on a secondary remit (e.g., Anthem FEP as secondary) is the primary's payment+adjustments, not a payer take; compute the secondary's expected payment as min(secondary allowed, remaining patient liability) and flag shortfalls; (2) PI-B10 on an E/M with modifier 25 paid $0 is a payer-initiated bundling reduction (not patient liability) and belongs in the downcoding/bundling appeal queue, especially when 17110 or another procedure on the same claim paid in full; (3) CO-45 equal to the full charge, or CR group codes, are code-usage violations to report; (4) CO-59 reductions must be checked against the payer's published multiple-procedure percentage; (5) PR-187 with a matching negative offset is a consumer-account payment and nets to zero patient balance.

Sources: [x12.org](https://x12.org/codes/claim-adjustment-reason-codes) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/regulations-and-guidance/guidance/manuals/downloads/clm104c22.pdf)

The UHC PLB AH fee ($21.89 in the excerpt) should be tested against 0.5% of the remit (BPR02 + fee); Optum's guide says premium fees can be ACH-debited or paid other ways, so whether the AH deduction is the Optum Pay fee or another 'transmission fee' needs confirmation in the Optum Pay portal (1-877-620-6194).

Confirm via the Zelis portal statement and request 'HIPAA Standard EFT/ERA' at 877-828-8770.

UHC's note that 'The VCP program is not available in the state of Kansas' suggests at least one state restriction exists but it was not verified.

CMS, CourtListener docket 18545072) but the docket page was blocked and it is not a class action against a vendor.

- The exact 005010X221A1 TR3 internal PLB code descriptions (e.g., whether AH is labeled 'Origination Fee' in the TR3) were not verified from the TR3 itself; the X12 external list wording ('Claim transmission fee amount') and CMS CR 7068 were used instead.
- The current quarterly Medicare overpayment interest rate (42 CFR 405.378) was not retrieved.
- Whether a statutory PAYGO sequester affects Medicare payments in 2026 was not researched.
- Fee schedules and opt-out mechanics for ECHO Health (enrollment site returned 403), Payspan (page empty), InstaMed (no fee text), VPay (503), Cigna and Humana (pages 404) could not be read; Anthem/UHC/Zelis/Optum were confirmed.

## Multiple procedure reductions, NCCI bundling and G2211 rules

Research lens: multiple-procedure reductions, NCCI bundling, modifier 25, G2211 and global-surgery rules for a Kentucky dermatology group (CGS J15). WebSearch budget was exhausted for this turn, so all facts come from primary documents fetched directly: CMS Claims Processing Manual ch.12 §40.6, the CY2025/CY2026 PFS RVU files (parsed locally, including Kentucky GPCIs), the RVU file record layout, the 2026 NCCI Policy Manual chapters 1 and 3, the 2026Q4 NCCI practitioner PTP and MUE tables (parsed locally), MLN907166 Global Surgery (Dec 2025), the CMS global-surgery data-collection page and 2025 99024 reporting list, the CMS G2211 FAQ and MLN Matters MM13272/MM13473, the CY2026 PFS final rule (90 FR 49266, govinfo PDF), the CY2024/CY2025 fact sheets, X12 CARC/RARC lists, UnitedHealthcare commercial and Medicare Advantage reimbursement policies (MPPR, Add-on, Global Days, E/M, CCI, Same Day), and Cigna's public multiple-procedure policy. Key results: Medicare MPPR is 100/50/50/50/50 ranked by fee-schedule amount for MULT PROC=2 codes only; every dermatology add-on (17003, 11103/11105/11107, 11201, 13102/13122/13133/13153, 17312/17314/17315), 17004, 11720/11721, 88305, E/M and G2211 carry indicator 0 and must never be reduced; UHC uses 100/50/50 (100/50/25 for ASO/Medicaid) ranked by non-facility RVU with no reduction when only one reducible code is present; Cigna states 100/50 with modifier-51-exempt codes excluded. NCCI has 1,464 active edits making 99212-99215 column-2 to essentially every 10040-17380 procedure with modifier indicator 1 (modifier 25 bypasses), but none for 99202-99205 (9 only), G2211 or 99024, so E/M-25 denials with modifier 25 present (UMR PI-B10) are payer policy, not NCCI. G2211 is $17.37 national / $16.68 KY in 2026 (CF $33.4009), not payable with modifier 25 except AWV/vaccine/Part B preventive since 2025, and CMS's FAQ names mole removal as a non-qualifying relationship. Kentucky 2025 Medicare non-facility amounts computed from the RVU file ($83.30 for 99213, $117.60 for 99214) exactly match the allowed amounts observed in ODA's remits. Groups of 10+ practitioners in Kentucky must report 99024 for listed 10/90-day global codes (17000, 17110, 11400-11646, 12031-13152, 14040-14301 are on the 2025 list). The 2026 final rule applies a -2.5% efficiency adjustment to procedure work RVUs while E/M visits are exempt, and CMS states the 1.0 work GPCI floor expired 9/30/2025.

### [UNVERIFIED] Medicare's standard multiple-surgery rule (MPFS MULT PROC indicator 2) pays 100% of the fee-schedule amount for the highest-valued procedure and 50% for the second through fifth, ranked by the Medicare fee-schedule amount (not billed charge); indicator-0 codes are paid at the lower of billed or fee schedule with no reduction, and CMS instructs MACs to 'pay by the unit for services that are already reduced (e.g., 17003)'.

Medicare Claims Processing Manual ch.12 §40.6.C: items 10-12 (indicator '2': rank in descending order by MFSDB amount; 100% highest, 50% second through fifth; sixth+ suspended 'by report' and never below 50%; 'Pay by the unit for services that are already reduced (e.g., 17003)'); item 4 (indicator '0': multiple surgery rules do not apply; pay lower of billed or fee schedule); item 1 (multiple surgeries identified by modifier 51 OR by more than one separately payable surgical procedure, including units >1 on one line); item 15 (equal fee-schedule values: rank by billed amount descending); item 16 (bilateral procedures valued at 150% first, then ranked); item 17 (round to nearest cent); §40.6.A 'Major surgical procedures are determined based on the MFSDB approved amount and not on the submitted amount'. The RVU file record layout defines indicator 2 as '(100%, 50%, 50%, 50%, 50% and by report)' and indicator 1 (pre-1995) as '(100%, 50%, 25%, 25%, 25%, and by report)'. Indicator 3 = endoscopy base rule; 4 = diagnostic imaging TC 50%/PC 5%; 5 = therapy PE 50%; 6 = cardiovascular TC 25%; 7 = ophthalmology TC 20%; 9 = concept does not apply.

**What it means for ODA:** Expected-payment engine: for each claim/date/rendering NPI, take lines whose code has MULT PROC=2 (expand units into separate ranked instances), rank by the payer's allowed amount (Medicare: fee schedule; never by charge), pay rank 1 at 100% and ranks 2-5 at 50%; lines with indicator 0 (all add-ons, 17004, E/M, G2211, 88305, 11720/11721) and 9 are paid at 100% regardless of how many procedures are on the claim. Any CARC 59 on an indicator-0 line, on the top-ranked line, or exceeding 50% of allowed is an appealable underpayment under Medicare rules and under payers that state they follow CMS indicators.

Sources: [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/regulations-and-guidance/guidance/manuals/downloads/clm104c12.pdf) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/zip/rvu25a-updated-01/10/2025.zip) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/medicare/payment/fee-schedules/physician/pfs-relative-value-files/rvu25a)

### [UNVERIFIED] Parsed from the CMS 2026 RVU file: dermatology codes subject to MPPR (indicator 2) are 17000, 17110, 17111, 11102, 11104, 11106, 11200, 11300-11313, 11400-11646, 11900, 11901, 12001-12057, 13100-13152, 14040-14302 family primaries, 17260-17286, 17311, 17313, 10060/10061, 11042/11043, 11055-11057 and 15271-15277; the codes that are NOT reducible (indicator 0) are every add-on (17003, 11103, 11105, 11107, 11201, 13102, 13122, 13133, 13153, 14302, 17312, 17314, 17315), 17004, 11720, 11721, 96910, 96912, 96567, 96573, 88305/88304/88312/88342, all E/M 99202-99215 and G2211.
