**What it means for ODA:** Load the CMS RVU file per year as the dashboard's reference table (code -> status, global days, MULT PROC, BILAT, RVUs). Detection rules: flag CARC 59 (or any reduction below 100% of the payer's own allowed) on indicator-0 codes, especially 17003 units, 11103/11105/11107, 11201, 13102/13122/13133/13153, 17312/17314/17315, 17004, 88305 and E/M; flag reductions on a line when the claim has only one indicator-2 code; flag 11300-11313 or any BILAT-0/9 code paid at 150% logic. Mohs claims (17311 + 17312 x n) must show 17312 at 100% each.

Sources: [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/zip/rvu26a-updated-12-29-2025.zip) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/medicare/payment/fee-schedules/physician/pfs-relative-value-files/rvu26a) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/zip/rvu25a-updated-01/10/2025.zip)

### [UNVERIFIED] Kentucky (CGS J15, locality 00) Medicare non-facility allowed amounts computed from the CMS RVU/GPCI files reproduce ODA's observed remits exactly: 2025 99213 = $83.30 and 99214 = $117.60 (the Anthem FEP line AMT*B6*117.60 equals the 2025 Medicare KY 99214 rate), and the 2026 amounts rise for E/M (99213 $89.52, 99214 $127.80) while most procedures move little.

Formula per RVU25A.pdf: [(work RVU x work GPCI) + (PE RVU x PE GPCI) + (MP RVU x MP GPCI)] x CF. KY GPCIs: 2025 work 1.000 (with floor), PE 0.877, MP 0.913; 2026 work 0.981 without floor / 1.000 with floor, PE 0.889, MP 0.915 (Indiana 2026: 0.988/1.000/0.927/0.486). CF: 2024 $32.74; 2025 $32.3465; 2026 $33.4009 non-qualifying APM ($33.5675 qualifying APM). 2025 KY non-facility (national in parentheses): 99202 $65.06 (69.87); 99203 $102.15 (109.01); 99204 $153.88 (163.35); 99205 $203.53 (215.75); 99212 $51.07; 99213 $83.30 (88.95); 99214 $117.60 (125.18); 99215 $165.42 (175.64); G2211 $14.95 (15.53); 17000 $60.64; 17003 $5.83; 17004 $147.48; 17110 $99.31 (109.98); 17111 $117.16; 11102 $86.66; 11103 $43.54; 11104 $108.10; 11200 $82.50; 11401 $137.76. 2026 KY with 1.0 work floor (national): 99202 $70.32 (75.15); 99203 $110.59; 99204 $167.52 (177.36); 99205 $223.82; 99212 $55.50; 99213 $89.52 (95.19); 99214 $127.80 (135.61); 99215 $181.60 (192.39); G2211 $16.68 (17.37); 17000 $61.33 (66.47); 17003 $5.79; 17004 $149.39; 17110 $101.46 (111.22); 17111 $119.10; 11102 $87.35 (95.53); 11103 $44.76; 11104 $110.87; 11106 $138.25; 11200 $84.99; 11201 $17.40; 11401 $142.55; 11402 $157.41; 11403 $184.42; 11404 $213.43; 11602 $222.19; 11642 $246.96; 12031 $238.44; 12032 $275.99; 13131 $355.64; 13132 $428.68; 13133 $157.53; 14040 $714.09; 14301 $1043.40; 17311 $615.92; 17312 $370.33; 17313 $577.31; 17315 $73.59; 11900 $52.41; 10060 $118.84; 11720 $30.27; 88305 $65.08; 96910 $102.16; 96912 $86.43. Without the 1.0 work floor, 2026 KY amounts are about 0.5-1% lower on E/M (e.g., 99214 approx $126.53).

**What it means for ODA:** Build a per-year, per-locality Medicare fee table from the CMS files (recompute each January and quarterly update) and use it as the benchmark allowed amount for Medicare KY/IN, Medicare Advantage plans and Medicare-pegged commercial contracts (Anthem FEP evidently pays 100% of Medicare KY). Downcoding dollars = allowed(submitted code) minus allowed(paid code) from this table; MPPR expected payment uses these amounts for ranking. Humana MA 99213 $66.64 and UHC MA 99213 $76.64 are 80% and 92% of the 2025 Medicare rate, so MA underpayment must be judged against contract, not Medicare.

Sources: [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/zip/rvu26a-updated-12-29-2025.zip) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/zip/rvu25a-updated-01/10/2025.zip) · [govinfo.gov](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2025-11-05/pdf/2025-19787.pdf) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/newsroom/fact-sheets/calendar-year-cy-2025-medicare-physician-fee-schedule-final-rule) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/newsroom/fact-sheets/calendar-year-cy-2024-medicare-physician-fee-schedule-final-rule)

### [UNVERIFIED] CARC 59 ('Processed based on multiple or concurrent procedure rules') is the remittance signature of an MPPR cut; an excessive-reduction detector should recompute the ranked 100/50 schedule from the payer's own allowed amounts and flag five error patterns: reduction on indicator-0 add-ons (17003, 11103, 11201, 17312), reduction when only one reducible code exists, reduction on the highest-valued line, reduction deeper than 50% (25% third-procedure 'alternate' methods), and ranking by charge instead of allowed.

X12 CARC 59 text: 'Processed based on multiple or concurrent procedure rules. (For example multiple surgery or diagnostic imaging, concurrent anesthesia.)' with usage note 'Refer to the 835 Healthcare Policy Identification Segment (loop 2110 Service Payment Information REF), if present.' CMS ranking rules (§40.6.C items 4, 10-12, 15, 16) and UHC's policy ('If two codes are billed but only one is subject to reduction, no reduction will be taken on either procedure'; units of one code are ranked as separate procedures, e.g., 11300 x3 -> units 2 and 3 reduced) give the arithmetic. Worked Medicare KY 2026 example: 17110 + 17000 + 17003 x5 + 11102 same day -> rank 17110 $101.46 at 100%, 11102 $87.35 at 50% = $43.68, 17000 $61.33 at 50% = $30.67, 17003 5 x $5.79 = $28.95 at 100%; expected allowed $204.76. Errors to flag: 17003 reduced (CMS: pay by the unit, already reduced), 11103/11105/11107 reduced, 17004 reduced (indicator 0, modifier-51 exempt), 88305 or E/M reduced, G2211 reduced, 11300-11313 or any indicator-2 code reduced when it is the only reducible code, two reducible codes both at 50%, or a 25% tier applied by a payer whose published method is 100/50/50. 835 evidence per line: SVC02 charge, SVC03 paid, AMT*B6 allowed, CAS*CO*59 amount, and SVC06 (payer-altered procedure, e.g., modifier 51 added by UMR).

**What it means for ODA:** Implement 'MPPR audit' as a claim-level rule: group SVC lines by claim + rendering NPI + DOS, derive each line's allowed (AMT*B6 or charge minus CO-45 minus CO-59), identify indicator-2 lines, rank by allowed, compute expected reduction, compare with the CO-59 amounts and SVC03; emit a finding with dollar delta and the specific rule violated. The 10,724 CARC-59 lines already in ODA's data should be re-scored with this engine; the appeal packet quotes §40.6.C item 4 (indicator 0) or item 11 (50%) and the payer's own policy text.

Sources: [x12.org](https://x12.org/codes/claim-adjustment-reason-codes) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/regulations-and-guidance/guidance/manuals/downloads/clm104c12.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/comm-reimbursement/COMM-MPPR-for-Medical-Surgical-Services-Policy.pdf)

### [UNVERIFIED] UnitedHealthcare's commercial MPPR policy (2026R0034D) and its Medicare Advantage twin (2025R9021A) apply reductions only to codes with CMS MULT PROC indicators 2 and 3, rank by CMS non-facility total RVUs for office claims, take no reduction when only one reducible code is on the claim, ignore modifier 51 as a trigger, pay 100/50/50 under the Standard method (100/50/25 'Alternate' only for ASO groups that did not adopt Standard and for Medicaid), and exempt only modifier-78 procedures; UHC's Add-on policy pays add-ons (CMS global 'ZZZ') only when the primary code is paid.

COMM-MPPR policy text: 'Medical and surgical services which have multiple procedure indicators 2 and 3 are subject to the multiple procedure concept'; 'If two codes are billed but only one is subject to reduction, no reduction will be taken on either procedure'; 'A single code subject to the multiple procedure concept is submitted with multiple units. For example, CPT code 11300 is submitted with 3 units. Multiple procedure reductions would apply to the second and third unit'; 'The use of modifier 51 appended to a code is not a factor'; 'Procedures performed in a place of service other than the facility POS setting will be ranked by the CMS Non-Facility RVUs'; Standard Method '100% ... primary/major procedure. 50% ... secondary procedure. 50% ... all subsequent procedures'; Alternate Method '100% ... 50% ... 25% ... all subsequent'; 'There are no modifiers that override the multiple procedure concept other than ... modifier 78'; gap-fill codes use Optum Essential RBRVS; 0.00-RVU codes ranked secondary; bilateral split into two lines ranked primary/secondary (50% to side 2); history: list updates 1/1/2025, 2/16/2025, 6/29/2025, policy implemented 4/1/1999. MA policy 2025R9021A (history 4/1/2025) repeats indicators 2 and 3, facility vs non-facility RVU ranking, 100%/50% example, units rule, and states ranking is 'based on the National Physician Fee Schedule (NPFS) Relative Value Unit (RVU) and not on the submitted amount'. Add-on Codes policy: add-ons 'are never reimbursed unless a primary procedure code is also reimbursed'; Mohs add-ons 17312/17314/17315 may be on a different DOS but should be on the same claim; add-on may be on a separate claim but same claim recommended. UHC CCI policy recognizes modifiers 24, 25, 57, 58, 59, 78, 79, XE/XP/XS/XU and anatomic modifiers to bypass NCCI edits with modifier indicator 1.

**What it means for ODA:** For UHC commercial, UHC MA, Optum/UHC Shared Services and (by TPA relationship, to be confirmed) UMR claims, the dashboard can compute expected MPPR using CMS non-facility total RVUs and the 100/50/50 method and treat any 25% tier, any reduction of an indicator-0 add-on, or a reduction when only one indicator-2 code exists as a policy violation quoting policy number 2026R0034D. The ODA UMR line showing SVC06 with modifier 51 added confirms UMR applies MPPR logic; verify UMR's own method (UMR plans may be ASO 'Alternate' 100/50/25).

Sources: [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/comm-reimbursement/COMM-MPPR-for-Medical-Surgical-Services-Policy.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/medadv-reimbursement/MEDADV-Multiple-Procedure-Payment-Reduction-Medical-Surgical-Services-Policy.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/comm-reimbursement/COMM-Add-On-Codes-Policy.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/comm-reimbursement/COMM-CCI-Editing-Policy.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/en/policies-protocols/commercial-policies/commercial-reimbursement-policies.html) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/en/policies-protocols/medicare-advantage-policies/medicare-advantage-reimbursement-policies.html)

### [UNVERIFIED] Cigna's published commercial multiple-procedure rule pays 100% of the allowable for the major/first surgical procedure and 50% for all other procedures in the same session, 150% for bilateral-eligible procedures, applies the reduction to assistant-surgeon fees, and does not apply to procedures deemed modifier-51 exempt.

Cigna 'Coverage for Multiple Surgical Procedures' (provider policies page): 'Procedures performed during the same operative session by the same provider (known as multiple procedures) are reimbursed following these guidelines: 100% allowable for major procedure, or first surgical procedure; 50% allowable for all other procedures'; 'Bilateral Procedures ... 150% of the fee schedule amount for a single procedure'; 'This policy does not apply to procedures deemed to be Modifier 51-exempt'; 'Assistant surgeon fees are also subject to multiple procedure policy'; 'In some cases, the office visit is not separately reimbursable from the surgical code'. The detailed Cigna reimbursement policy documents (R12 Multiple Procedure Reduction, R49 modifier 25) could not be retrieved (static.cigna.com URLs returned 404; the index is script-rendered).

**What it means for ODA:** Use 100/50 for Cigna expected-payment calculations; flag any third-procedure 25% tier and any reduction on modifier-51-exempt codes (CPT Appendix E includes add-ons and 17004) as contrary to Cigna's stated policy. Cigna is a small share of ODA's mix, so prioritize UHC/Anthem/Humana rules first.

Sources: [cigna.com](https://www.cigna.com/health-care-providers/coverage-and-claims/policies)

### [UNVERIFIED] The NCCI practitioner PTP table (v32.3, effective 10/1/2026) makes established-patient E/M codes 99211-99215 column-2 to essentially every integumentary procedure 10040-17380 with modifier indicator 1 (modifier 25 bypasses), but contains no PTP edits pairing new-patient 99202-99205 (only 9 active edits with any 1xxxx code), G2211 or 99024 with dermatology procedures; therefore an E/M-25 denial coded as NCCI (CARC 236) or bundling (97, B10) when modifier 25 is present is a payer-specific policy, not an NCCI edit.

Parsed ccipra-v323r0-f1.TXT: 1,464 active edits with column 2 in 99212-99215 and column 1 in 10000-19999, all modifier indicator 1, rationale 'CPT Manual or CMS manual coding instruction', effective 10/1/2020 (an earlier series ran 7/1/2013 to 12/31/2019); 366 active edits with 99211; 9 active edits with 99202-99205; 0 with G2211 as column 2; 0 with 99024 as column 2. Column-1 codes carrying the 99213 edit include 10060, 11042, 11055-11057, 11102-11107, 11200, 11300-11313, 11400-11446, 11600-11646, 11720/11721, 11900/11901, 12001-12057, 13100-13152, 14040-14301, 15xxx, 17000, 17004, 17110, 17111, 17260-17286, 17311, 17313; dermatology codes with no 99213 edit: 17003, 96910, 96912, 96567, 96573. UHC's CCI Editing policy: 'A modifier indicator of 1 indicates that an NCCI designated modifier can be used to allow both submitted services', and modifier 25 is on UHC's recognized list. X12: CARC 236 'This procedure or procedure/modifier combination is not compatible with another procedure ... according to the National Correct Coding Initiative'; CARC 97 'The benefit for this service is included in the payment/allowance for another service/procedure that has already been adjudicated'; CARC B10 'Allowed amount has been reduced because a component of the basic procedure/test was paid'; RARC M80 'Not covered when performed during the same session/date as a previously processed service'; N19 'Procedure code incidental to primary procedure'; M15 'Separately billed services/tests have been bundled'; N20 'Service not payable with other service rendered on the same date'.

**What it means for ODA:** Detection rule 'E/M-25 bundled': E/M 99202-99215 paid $0 or reduced with CARC 97/B10/236/M80/N19 on a claim where a 000/010-global procedure was paid; sub-classify (a) modifier 25 missing in SVC01 -> coding fix and corrected claim, (b) modifier 25 present -> payer policy denial, appeal with the NCCI manual ch.1 §D/E quotes and the claim's documentation; (c) new-patient 9920x denied citing NCCI -> cite that no NCCI PTP edit exists for 99202-99205. The UMR example (99214:25 paid $0 with PI-B10 beside a paid 17110) belongs to class (b). Load the quarterly PTP table into the app to verify every bundling denial against the real edit and modifier indicator.

Sources: [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/zip/medicare-ncci-2026q4-practitioner-ptp-edits-ccipra-v323r0-f1.zip) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/medicare/coding-billing/national-correct-coding-initiative-ncci-edits/medicare-ncci-procedure-procedure-ptp-edits) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/comm-reimbursement/COMM-CCI-Editing-Policy.pdf) · [x12.org](https://x12.org/codes/claim-adjustment-reason-codes) · [x12.org](https://x12.org/codes/remittance-advice-remark-codes)

### [UNVERIFIED] Under Medicare global-surgery rules, an E/M on the same day as a 000- or 010-day minor procedure is included in the procedure payment, including the work of deciding to perform the minor procedure; it is separately payable only when a significant, separately identifiable E/M is documented and reported with modifier 25, a different diagnosis is not required, a 'new patient' status alone does not justify it, and modifier 57 applies only to 090-day major surgery; UHC's commercial and Medicare Advantage Global Days policies restate the same rule.

NCCI Policy Manual 2026 ch.1 §D: 'If a procedure has a global period of 000 or 010 days, it is defined as a minor surgical procedure. In general, E&M services performed on the same date of service as a minor surgical procedure are included in the payment for the procedure. The decision to perform a minor surgical procedure is included in the payment ... However, a significant and separately identifiable E&M service unrelated to the decision to perform the minor surgical procedure is separately reportable with modifier 25. The E&M service and minor surgical procedure do not require different diagnoses. ... The fact that the patient is new ... is not sufficient alone'. ch.1 §E.b: modifier 25 may be appended with 000/010 or XXX procedures; 'Medicare Global Surgery Rules prevent the reporting of a separate E&M service for the work associated with the decision to perform a minor surgical procedure regardless of whether the patient is a new or established patient'. MLN907166 (Dec 2025): 0-day and 10-day definitions ('Total global period is 11 days'), 'Don't use modifier 57 with minor surgeries', 'MACs may not pay an E/M service billed with CPT modifier 57 if it's provided on the day of, or the day before, a procedure with a 000- or 010-day global', modifier 25 'You don't need to report different diagnoses', modifiers 24 (unrelated E/M in post-op period, documentation must be sent), 58 (staged, new post-op period starts), 78 (unplanned return to OR), 79 (unrelated procedure, new global). UHC COMM Global Days: '000 ... Evaluation and Management (E/M) services on the day of the procedure are not reimbursable except as noted'; '010 ... E/M services on the day of the procedure and during the 10-day postoperative period are not reimbursable except as noted'; 'The initial evaluation is always included in the allowance for a procedure having a Global Days Value other than 090'; 'Visits that are unrelated ... (Use modifier 25 for the day of the procedure and modifier 24 during the postoperative period)'; modifier 78 paid at CMS intraoperative percentage. UHC MA 2026R9013A (history 4/1/2026): 'Visits by the same physician on the same day as a minor surgery or endoscopy are included in the global package, unless a significant, separately identifiable service is also performed. Modifier -25 is used'; documentation must support both but 'is not required to be submitted with the claim'. RARC N525 'These services are not covered when performed within the global period of another service'; M144 'Pre-/post-operative care payment is included in the allowance for the surgery/procedure'.

**What it means for ODA:** Dashboard needs a global-period engine: for each paid 010/090 procedure by NPI (same specialty/TIN), compute the window (DOS+10 or DOS-1 to DOS+90) and classify later E/M lines as inside-global; denials with N525/M144 inside the window are correct unless modifier 24 (unrelated) or 25/57 rules apply, in which case the appeal must attach documentation. Appeal letters for E/M-25 denials should quote NCCI ch.1 §D verbatim and show the separately identifiable problem; the app should pull the EMA note's diagnoses and problem list to pre-draft that justification.

Sources: [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/document/01-chapter1-ncci-medicare-policy-manual-2026-final.pdf) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/document/03-chapter3-ncci-medicare-policy-manual-2026-final.pdf) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/document/mln907166-global-surgery-booklet.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/comm-reimbursement/COMM-Global-Days-Policy.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/medadv-reimbursement/MEDADV-Global-Days-Policy.pdf) · [x12.org](https://x12.org/codes/remittance-advice-remark-codes)

### [UNVERIFIED] Dermatology-specific NCCI pairs that drive same-day bundling denials: 17000 is column-2 to 17110 (indicator 1), 17003 is column-2 to 17110 (1), 11102 is column-2 to every excision 11400-11646 and to Mohs 17311 (1), 17000 is column-2 to 11102 (1, with an NCCI manual warning about modifier 59 misuse), 88305 is column-2 to 17311 (1), while 17004/17000 and 17111/17110 and 11104/11102 have indicator 0 (never payable together).

From ccipra-v323r0-f1.TXT (active edits): 17110/17000 eff 7/1/2007 ind 1 'Mutually exclusive'; 17110/17003 eff 4/1/2019 ind 1; 17110/11102 eff 1/1/2019 ind 1; 11400, 11401, 11402, 11602, 11642 / 11102 ind 1 'CPT Manual or CMS manual coding instruction'; 11102/17000 eff 1/1/2020 ind 1; 17311/11102 ind 1; 17311/88305 eff 1/1/2007 ind 1 'HCPCS/CPT procedure code definition'; 17110/11200 ind 1; 17110/11900 ind 1; 17000/11900 ind 1; 17004/17000 ind 0; 17111/17110 ind 0; 11104/11102 ind 0; no edits for 11402 with 12031/12032, 11642 with 13131/12051 (intermediate/complex repair separately payable), nor 17000 vs 17110 in the other direction. NCCI ch.3 §E: 'Only 1 removal HCPCS/CPT code may be reported for a lesion'; lesion removal 'include[s] the procurement of tissue from the same lesion by biopsy ... CPT codes 11102-11107 ... may be separately reportable ... if the biopsy is performed on a different lesion'; simple repairs 12001-12021 integral to lesion removal; 'excision of benign lesions with excised diameter of 0.5 cm or less (CPT codes 11400, 11420, 11440) includes simple, intermediate, or complex repairs'; §E.14: PTP edits pairing 11102/11104/11106 with 17000 and 17004 'are often bypassed by using modifier 59 or XE or XS ... only appropriate if the 2 procedures ... are performed on separate lesions'. §F: Mohs 17311-17315 include biopsy/excision/pathology codes; a separate diagnostic biopsy and frozen section may be reported with 59/XS/XU or 58. MUE (2026Q4 practitioner table, MAI 2 = policy, not bypassable; MAI 3 = clinical, appealable): 17000 1 (MAI 2); 17003 13 (2); 17004 1 (2); 17110 1 (2); 17111 1 (2); 11102/11104/11106 1 (2); 11103 6, 11105 3, 11107 2 (3); 11200 1 (2); 11201 1 (3); 11300 5; 11400-11402 3; 11403/11404 2; 11601 2, 11602 3, 11642 3; 11900/11901 1 (2); 12001-13132 1 (2); 13133 7; 17311 4; 17312 6; 17313 3; 17314 4; 17315 15; 88305 16; 99212-99214 2; 99215 1; 99202-99205 1 (MAI 2); 99024 1.

**What it means for ODA:** Rules: (1) 17000 or 17003 denied CO-97/236 on a claim with 17110 -> check for 59/XS on 17000; if different lesions were treated, corrected claim with XS, else write-off is correct; (2) 11102 denied with an excision/Mohs on the same day -> appeal only if a different lesion (XS) was biopsied; (3) 17004 with 17000/17003 or 17111 with 17110 -> coding error, not appealable; (4) unit denials (CARC 151, RARC N362/N640) above MUE -> MAI-3 codes (biopsy add-ons, excisions, 17312, 88305) can be appealed with documentation, MAI-2 codes cannot; (5) treat intermediate/complex repair denials with excisions > 0.5 cm as appealable (no NCCI edit).

Sources: [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/zip/medicare-ncci-2026q4-practitioner-ptp-edits-ccipra-v323r0-f1.zip) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/document/03-chapter3-ncci-medicare-policy-manual-2026-final.pdf) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/zip/medicare-ncci-2026-q4-practitioner-services-mue-table.zip) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/medicare/coding-billing/national-correct-coding-initiative-ncci-edits/medicare-ncci-medically-unlikely-edits)

### [UNVERIFIED] G2211 (visit complexity add-on, 0.52 total RVU in 2026 = $17.37 national / $16.68 Kentucky non-facility; $15.53 / $14.95 in 2025) is payable by Medicare only with 99202-99205/99211-99215 (and from 2026 home/residence E/M), is denied when the base E/M carries modifier 25 (CR 13272, effective 1/1/2024) except, from 1/1/2025, when the modifier-25 service is an AWV, vaccine administration or other Part B preventive service (CR 13705), carries usual coinsurance/deductible, and CMS's own FAQ names 'a mole removal or referral to a physician for removal of a mole' as a relationship for which G2211 is not appropriate.

MM13272 (Related CR 13272, effective 1/1/2024, implementation 1/2/2024, transmittal R12424CP): 'implement edits to deny payment of O/O E/M visit complexity add-on code G2211 when you report an associated O/O E/M visit, codes 99202-99205 and 99211-99215, with modifier 25 for the same patient by the same practitioner'. MM13473 (CR 13473 & CR 13705, effective 1/1/2024 and 1/1/2025): 'G2211 is payable even if you report the base code with modifier 25 only when the service or other procedure requiring the reporting of modifier 25 is an allowed Part B service ... Part B preventive services, Immunization administrations, Annual Wellness Visits'; no additional documentation requirement; coinsurance and deductible apply. CMS G2211 FAQ: separately payable 'in both facility and non-facility settings and is not limited to any physician specialties'; not appropriate where the relationship 'is of a discrete, routine, or time-limited nature; such as ... a mole removal or referral to a physician for removal of a mole; for treatment of a simple virus'; appropriate for 'ongoing care for a single, serious condition or a complex condition'; Q6 confirms the 2025 exception and extension to home visits in 2026; no specific diagnosis required. CY2026 final rule (90 FR 49266, 49462-49464): G2211 extended to 99341-99350, descriptor refined; commenters asked CMS to 'revisit the modifier 25 payment policy restriction' and CMS did not change it. CY2024 fact sheet: CF $32.74; CY2025 fact sheet: CF $32.35; 2026 CF $33.4009 (non-QP). RVU file: G2211 status A, global ZZZ, MULT PROC 0, 2026 work 0.33/PE 0.17/MP 0.02; 2025 0.33/0.13/0.02 (0.48).

**What it means for ODA:** G2211 rule set: (1) Medicare FFS/MA G2211 denied on a claim whose E/M has modifier 25 is correct per CMS (unless the 25 was for an AWV/vaccine) - classify as 'billing pattern fix', not appeal; consider whether the procedure-day E/M-25 or the G2211 is worth more and stop pairing them; (2) G2211 denied on a Medicare claim with no modifier 25 is an underpayment ($16.68 KY 2026) to appeal; (3) G2211 on visits that are purely procedural/mole-check relationships is audit risk per the FAQ; longitudinal management of psoriasis, hidradenitis, melanoma surveillance, biologic therapy fits the 'single serious or complex condition' language. Dollar model: count G2211-eligible established visits x $16.68.

Sources: [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/document/mm13272-edits-prevent-payment-g2211-office/outpatient-evaluation-and-management-visit-and-modifier.pdf) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/document/mm13473-how-use-office-and-outpatient-evaluation-and-management-visit-complexity-add-code-g2211.pdf) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/document/hcpcs-g2211-faq.pdf) · [govinfo.gov](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2025-11-05/pdf/2025-19787.pdf) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/newsroom/fact-sheets/calendar-year-cy-2025-medicare-physician-fee-schedule-final-rule) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/newsroom/fact-sheets/calendar-year-cy-2024-medicare-physician-fee-schedule-final-rule) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/zip/rvu26a-updated-12-29-2025.zip)

### [UNVERIFIED] Commercial and Medicare Advantage handling of G2211 is payer-specific and, on ODA's own remits, UMR denies it with PI-B10 and UHC pays $0 with CO-45 plus RARC M80; UnitedHealthcare's published commercial and Medicare Advantage reimbursement-policy indexes contain no G2211/visit-complexity policy, so these denials are adjudication-system decisions rather than a published UHC policy, and Anthem, Humana, Cigna and Aetna G2211 positions could not be retrieved in this run.

ODA 835 excerpts: UMR 'SVC*HC:G2211*29.19*0.00~CAS*PI*B10*29.19' (B10 = 'Allowed amount has been reduced because a component of the basic procedure/test was paid'); UHC 'SVC*HC:G2211*26.02*0.00~CAS*CO*45*26.02~LQ*HE*M80' (M80 = 'Not covered when performed during the same session/date as a previously processed service for the patient'). UHC commercial reimbursement policy index (about 100 policies) and MA index list MPPR, Add-on, Global Days, E/M, CCI, Same Day, Modifier policies but no G2211 title; the UHC E/M policy text (4 pages, history 4/1/2026) does not mention G2211. Medicare FFS pays G2211 (status A). Humana's claims-payment-policies page is script-rendered ('No claims payment policies found' without JavaScript); Anthem's reimbursement pages require state/plan selection and returned no policy list; Aetna's payment-policies URL returned 404; Cigna's reimbursement PDFs returned 404.

**What it means for ODA:** The dashboard should produce a payer-by-payer G2211 payment-rate matrix from the 835s (paid / billed / denied, with CARC-RARC pairs and whether modifier 25 was on the base E/M) and let the practice decide per payer whether to keep billing it; for UHC MA, a G2211 denial with M80 on a claim whose E/M had no modifier 25 contradicts CMS coverage that MA plans must at least match for Part B services and is a candidate for appeal, whereas with modifier 25 present it mirrors CMS policy. Request the missing payer policies (Anthem KY, Humana, UMR plan documents) from provider representatives or Availity.

Sources: [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/en/policies-protocols/commercial-policies/commercial-reimbursement-policies.html) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/en/policies-protocols/medicare-advantage-policies/medicare-advantage-reimbursement-policies.html) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/comm-reimbursement/COMM-Evaluation-and-Management-Policy.pdf) · [x12.org](https://x12.org/codes/claim-adjustment-reason-codes) · [x12.org](https://x12.org/codes/remittance-advice-remark-codes)

### [UNVERIFIED] The CY2026 PFS final rule sets the conversion factor at $33.4009 for non-qualifying-APM clinicians ($33.5675 qualifying APM), applies a -2.5% 'efficiency adjustment' to the work RVUs and intraservice time of all non-time-based services (procedures such as destructions, biopsies, excisions, repairs and Mohs) while exempting E/M visits, care management, behavioral health, telehealth-list and maternity codes, and CMS states the statutory 1.0 work GPCI floor expired September 30, 2025 and it lacks authority to extend it.

90 FR 49266 (Nov 5, 2025), p. 49961: 'We estimate the CY 2026 PFS nonqualifying APM CF to be $33.4009 which reflects a 0.49 percent positive budget neutrality adjustment ... and the 0.25 percent update adjustment factor ... [and] the 1-year increase of 2.50 percent for CY 2026 established by statute. We estimate the CY 2026 PFS qualifying APM CF to be $33.5675'. Efficiency adjustment: 'this methodology yielded a proposed efficiency adjustment of 2.5 percent, which would be a downward (negative) adjustment for certain codes, for CY 2026'; 'We proposed to apply the adjustment to all codes except time-based codes, including but not limited to, E/M visits, care management services, behavioral health services, services on the CMS telehealth list, and maternity codes with a global period of MMM. This adjustment would apply to all codes that are assigned a procedure status of A (active), B (bundled), C ...'; CMS response: E/M codes 'are akin to other time-based services and thus properly excluded'. GPCI response: 'The 1.0 work GPCI floor is established by statute and expired on September 30, 2025 ... CMS does not have the authority to extend the 1.0 work floor beyond the September 30, 2025 expiration'. Observed RVU effects 2025->2026: 17110 total NF 3.40->3.33, 17000 2.05->1.99, 11102 2.96->2.86, 17311 20.44->19.97; 99213 2.75->2.85, 99214 3.87->4.06 (PE increases). The rule also discusses using 99024 data: 'only 2 percent of visits following procedures with 10-day global periods and 28 percent of visits following procedures with 90-day global periods were provided', and CMS is considering revaluing global packages using 99024-based post-op visit counts.

**What it means for ODA:** Expected-allowed tables must be versioned by year and quarter; 2026 Medicare E/M allowed amounts in Kentucky rise about 7-9% while procedure amounts are roughly flat, which changes the downcoding dollar delta (99214 vs 99213 in KY: $34.30 in 2025, $38.28 in 2026). Flag any 2026 Medicare line paid at 2025 rates. The GPCI floor uncertainty means two possible 2026 KY amounts; store both and detect which one CGS actually uses from early-2026 remits. CMS's finding that 10-day-global post-op visits are rarely furnished signals future downward pressure on 17110/11400-series values and makes accurate 99024 reporting strategically important.

Sources: [govinfo.gov](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2025-11-05/pdf/2025-19787.pdf) · [federalregister.gov](https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2025/11/05/2025-19787/medicare-and-medicaid-programs-cy-2026-payment-policies-under-the-physician-fee-schedule-and-other) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/zip/rvu26a-updated-12-29-2025.zip)

### [UNVERIFIED] Practices with 10 or more practitioners in Kentucky (one of nine mandated states) must report CPT 99024 for post-operative visits following procedures on CMS's annual global-surgery reporting list, and the 2025 list includes the core dermatology 10-day and 90-day codes: 10040, 10060, 10061, 11200, 11400-11446, 11601-11646, 12031-12052, 13101, 13121, 13131, 13132, 13151, 13152, 14020-14301, 15100, 15120, 15240, 15260, 17000, 17004, 17110, 17111.

MLN907166 (Dec 2025): 'Practitioners must report post-operative E/M visits using CPT code 99024 if they: Practice in a group of 10 or more practitioners in 1 of these 9 states: Florida, Kentucky, Louisiana, Nevada, New Jersey, North Dakota, Ohio, Oregon, Rhode Island ... Provide global services under 1 of the required procedure codes. More than 100 practitioners nationally provide the required procedure codes more than 10,000 times annually or have more than $10 million in annual allowed charges'; 'You're exempt from required reporting if your practice has less than 10 practitioners, but we encourage you to report'. CMS Global Surgery Data Collection page: reporting 'beginning July 1, 2017'; 'Global Reporting List 2025' zip (295 rows, columns hcpcs, desc, global, status). 99024 is status B (bundled, no payment), MULT PROC 9 in the 2025 RVU file, MUE 1 (MAI 3). The CY2026 final rule restates the requirement ('practitioners in nine states and in practices of 10 or more National Provider Identifiers (NPIs) to report post-operative visits ... using no-pay HCPCS code 99024').

**What it means for ODA:** ODA (about 10 providers, Kentucky) is at the threshold: the dashboard should count billing NPIs under the TIN and, if 10 or more, monitor that 99024 lines appear on Medicare claims after listed procedures (expected rate per 10-day global) and flag providers with zero 99024 reporting. 99024 lines should be excluded from underpayment math (always $0) and from MPPR ranking. Since CMS may revalue 10-day globals using 99024 counts, accurate reporting protects future 17110/excision payment.

Sources: [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/document/mln907166-global-surgery-booklet.pdf) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/medicare/payment/fee-schedules/physician/global-surgery-data-collection) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/zip/global-reporting-list-2025.zip) · [govinfo.gov](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2025-11-05/pdf/2025-19787.pdf)

### [UNVERIFIED] UnitedHealthcare's E/M reimbursement policy (commercial; history 4/1/2026) states that office-visit E/M codes may be 'adjusted' or denied when documentation does not support the level, that medical records are requested when a provider's billing pattern 'deviates significantly from their peers' or claim attributes indicate possible errors, that 'level of care escalation data' may be compared to historical and peer data, and that providers 'may resubmit the claim with a revised E/M code for denied claims'; UHC's Same Day/Same Service policy allows modifier 25 for a second same-group E/M only for an unrelated problem.

COMM-Evaluation-and-Management-Policy: 'This reimbursement policy explains when medical records may be requested to ensure that the appropriate level of CPT E/M code is reimbursed'; 'Providers may experience adjustments to, or denials of the office visit or other outpatient E/M code ... if the documentation does not support the E/M level submitted. The provider may resubmit the claim with a revised E/M code for denied claims'; Q&A 2: 'UnitedHealthcare may request medical records when the data indicates a physician ... has a billing pattern that deviates significantly from their peers, or claim attributes indicate possible billing errors'; 'A providers' level of care escalation data and parenteral narcotic use data may be compared to their historical data and to peer data'; cloned EHR documentation 'would not be acceptable'; time-based level selection may not include 'Additional distinct service procedures provided the same day'; MDM uses CMS Pub 100 ch.12 §40.1 definitions of minor (000/010) vs major (090) surgery for risk. X12 CARC 150 'Payer deems the information submitted does not support this level of service'; RARC N839 'The procedure code was added/changed because the level of service exceeds the compensable condition(s)'; M25 'The information furnished does not substantiate the need for this level of service'; N661 'Documentation does not support that the services rendered were medically necessary'.

**What it means for ODA:** For UHC-family downcodes (SVC06 submitted code higher than SVC01 paid code, or CARC 150/N839/M25 present), the appeal should demand the documentation basis UHC's own policy presupposes and attach the note showing MDM elements; the dashboard should track whether a records request preceded the adjustment (none in the 835 means a claims-edit downcode) and compute each provider's level distribution versus peers, because UHC states it targets outliers. Procedure time must be excluded when E/M level is time-based, so E/M-25 visits on procedure days should be leveled by MDM.

Verbatim X12 definitions read: 59 'Processed based on multiple or concurrent procedure rules'; 97 'The benefit for this service is included in the payment/allowance for another service/procedure that has already been adjudicated'; B10 'Allowed amount has been reduced because a component of the basic procedure/test was paid. The beneficiary is not liable for more than the charge limit for the basic procedure/test'; 236 'This procedure or procedure/modifier combination is not compatible with another procedure or procedure/modifier combination provided on the same day according to the National Correct Coding Initiative or workers compensation state regulations/ fee schedule requirements'; 4 'The procedure code is inconsistent with the modifier used'; 151 'Payment adjusted because the payer deems the information submitted does not support this many/frequency of services'; 150 'Payer deems the information submitted does not support this level of service'; 252 'An attachment/other documentation is required to adjudicate this claim/service'; 187 'Consumer Spending Account payments'; 94 'Processed in Excess of charges'; B15 'This service/procedure requires that a qualifying service/procedure be received and covered'. RARC: M80 'Not covered when performed during the same session/date as a previously processed service for the patient'; N19 'Procedure code incidental to primary procedure'; M15 'Separately billed services/tests have been bundled'; N20 'Service not payable with other service rendered on the same date'; N525 'These services are not covered when performed within the global period of another service'; M144 'Pre-/post-operative care payment is included in the allowance for the surgery/procedure'; N362 'The number of Days or Units of Service exceeds our acceptable maximum'; N640 'Exceeds number/frequency approved/allowed within time period'; N463 'Missing support data for claim'; N431 'Not covered with this procedure'; N822 'Missing procedure modifier(s)'; N519 'Invalid combination of HCPCS modifiers'; N674 'Not covered unless a pre-requisite procedure/service has been provided'; N839 'The procedure code was added/changed because the level of service exceeds the compensable condition(s)'; N661 'Documentation does not support that the services rendered were medically necessary'; N381 'Alert: Consult our contractual agreement for restrictions/billing/payment information'. CARC usage notes direct readers to the 835 loop 2110 REF Healthcare Policy Identification segment, which some payers use to cite the policy applied.

**What it means for ODA:** Use this mapping as the denial-taxonomy seed: MPPR = CARC 59; bundling/modifier-25 = 97, B10, 236 with M80/N19/M15/N20; global period = N525/M144; units = 151 with N362/N640; downcode = SVC06 != SVC01 and/or 150, N839, M25; documentation request = 252 with N463; modifier problems = 4, N822, N519; secondary-payer artifacts = OA-94, PR-187. Parse the 2110 REF policy-identification segment when present and surface it in the appeal packet.

Sources: [x12.org](https://x12.org/codes/claim-adjustment-reason-codes) · [x12.org](https://x12.org/codes/remittance-advice-remark-codes)

### [UNVERIFIED] Multiple-procedure and E/M-with-procedure denials should be evaluated against the submitted-versus-adjudicated code in the 835 (SVC01 vs SVC06): ODA's UMR line 'SVC*HC:11401:51*0.00*64.15***HC:11401' shows the payer itself appending modifier 51 when applying its reduction, so SVC06 differences are an MPPR/downcode signal, not a provider coding error.

The X12 835 SVC01 carries the adjudicated procedure (with payer-applied modifiers) and SVC06 the submitted procedure when the payer changed it; CMS and UHC both state modifier 51 is not required to trigger MPPR (CMS §40.6.C.1 identifies multiple surgeries by 'The presence ... of the -51 modifier; and the billing of more than one separately payable surgical procedure'; UHC 'The use of modifier 51 appended to a code is not a factor'). CPT 11401 (excision benign trunk 0.6-1.0 cm) has MULT PROC 2 and 2026 KY Medicare non-facility value $142.55; a paid amount of $64.15 is roughly 45% of that, consistent with a 50% secondary-procedure reduction on a Medicare-pegged allowed amount.

**What it means for ODA:** Parser rule: when SVC06 is present and differs from SVC01 only by an added modifier 51, classify the line as 'payer-applied MPPR' and run the MPPR audit; when SVC06 shows a higher E/M level than SVC01, classify as 'downcode'; when SVC06 shows a different procedure code family, classify as 'recode' and route to coder review. Store both codes and all modifiers for every line so appeals can show the payer altered the claim.

Sources: [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/regulations-and-guidance/guidance/manuals/downloads/clm104c12.pdf) · [uhcprovider.com](https://www.uhcprovider.com/content/dam/provider/docs/public/policies/comm-reimbursement/COMM-MPPR-for-Medical-Surgical-Services-Policy.pdf) · [cms.gov](https://www.cms.gov/files/zip/rvu26a-updated-12-29-2025.zip)

The 2017-2018 Anthem modifier-25 E/M reduction (announced 50%, revised to 25%, then withdrawn) is remembered but was not verified from a source in this run and should not be cited in appeals until confirmed.
- Cigna's formal reimbursement policy documents (R12 Multiple Procedure Reduction, R49 Evaluation and Management with modifier 25, and any G2211 position) returned 404 at every static.cigna.com path tried; only the public 100%/50%/150% summary page was read. The status of Cigna's 2022 modifier-25 documentation-submission requirement is unconfirmed.
- Humana (Medicare Advantage and commercial) claims payment policies page is JavaScript-rendered and returned no policy list; Humana's MPPR percentages, modifier 25 handling and G2211 position for MA are unverified (MA plans generally mirror CMS MPPR, but this is not confirmed from a Humana document).
- Aetna payment-policy pages returned 404; Aetna is not a listed ODA payer, so this was deprioritized.
- AAD coding guidance pages (modifier 25, E/M with procedures, G2211) are member-gated (3 KB login shells); the AAD-specific documentation guidance requested in the lens should be pulled by a logged-in AAD member or from AAD's public position statements.
- CGS J15 publishes no standalone MPPR or modifier 25 page that could be located (the Part B site map lists only appeals/reopenings); CGS follows the CMS manuals cited, but a CGS-specific Modifier 25 fact sheet or self-service article, if one exists, was not read.
- Whether Congress extended the 1.0 work GPCI floor into 2026 after the final rule (CMS says it expired 9/30/2025; RVU26A ships both with-floor and without-floor GPCI columns). Early-2026 CGS remits will reveal which Kentucky amount applies (99214 $127.80 with floor vs about $126.53 without).
- MM13272 does not state which CARC/RARC combination MACs use to deny G2211 billed with a modifier-25 E/M; ODA's UHC MA example used CO-45 + M80. Confirm the Medicare FFS code pair from CGS remits in the data set.
- UMR (a UHC TPA) is governed by each self-funded plan's choice of UHC Standard (100/50/50) or Alternate (100/50/25) method and may apply its own E/M-with-minor-procedure bundling (the PI-B10 denial of 99214-25); the specific UMR plan documents for ODA's UMR patients were not available.
- The nine active NCCI edits that pair new-patient 99202-99205 with 1xxxx codes were not individually identified; they are few and unlikely to involve dermatology codes, but should be listed when the PTP table is loaded into the app.
- The UHC 'Multiple Procedure Reduction Codes' and 'Global Days Assignments' attachment lists referenced by the UHC policies were not downloaded; they should be loaded so UHC expected payments use UHC's gap-fill RVUs for codes CMS does not value.
- WebSearch was unavailable for this turn (shared budget exhausted), so no secondary coverage (AMA, MGMA, specialty-society news) was reviewed; all findings rest on primary documents fetched directly.
